One-loop central-emission vertex for two gluons in 𝒩 = 4 \mathcal{N}=4 super Yang-Mills theory Preprint: SLAC–PUB–17654
Emmet P. Byrne, Vittorio Del Duca, Lance J. Dixon, Einan Gardi, and Jennifer M. Smillie

Abstract
A necessary ingredient for extending the BFKL equation to next-to-next-to-leading logarithmic (NNLL) accuracy is the one-loop central emission vertex (CEV) for two gluons which are not strongly ordered in rapidity. Here we consider the one-loop six-gluon amplitude in 𝒩 = 4 \mathcal{N}=4 super Yang-Mills (SYM) theory in a central next-to-multi-Regge kinematic (NMRK) limit, we show that its dispersive part factorises in terms of the two-gluon CEV, and we use it to extract the one-loop two-gluon CEV for any helicity configuration within this theory. This is a component of the two-gluon CEV in QCD. Although computed in the NMRK limit, both the colour structure and the kinematic dependence of the two-gluon CEV capture much of the complexity of the six-gluon amplitudes in general kinematics. In fact, the transcendental functions of the latter can be conveniently written in terms of impact factors, trajectories, single-emission CEVs and a remainder, which is a function of the conformally invariant cross ratios which characterise the six-gluon amplitudes in planar 𝒩 = 4 \mathcal{N}=4 SYM. Finally, as expected, in the MRK limit the two-gluon CEV neatly factorises in terms of two single-emission CEVs.



1
Introduction

In the Regge limit, in which the squared centre-of-mass energy
s
is much larger than the momentum transfer
|t|
,
s\gg|t|
, any
2\to 2
scattering process is dominated by the exchange in the
t
channel of the highest-spin particle. In the case of QCD or
{\cal N}=4
Super Yang-Mills (SYM) theory, that implies the exchange of a gluon in the
t
channel. Contributions that do not feature gluon exchange in the
t
channel are power suppressed in
t/s
.
Building upon this feature, the Balitsky-Fadin-Kuraev-Lipatov (BFKL) equation describes
strong-interaction processes with two large and disparate scales,
s\gg|t|
, by resumming the radiative corrections to parton-parton scattering to all orders at leading logarithmic (LL)
Lipatov:1976zz
;
Kuraev:1976ge
;
Kuraev:1977fs
;
Balitsky:1978ic
and next-to-leading logarithmic (NLL) accuracy
Fadin:1998py
;
Ciafaloni:1998gs
;
Kotikov:2000pm
;
Kotikov:2002ab
in
\log(s/|t|)
. The resummation of large energy logarithms extends to higher-multiplicity final states in special kinematic limits and improves the description of scattering events where large rapidity intervals are spanned
Mueller:1986ey
;
DelDuca:1993mn
;
Stirling:1994he
;
Andersen:2001kta
;
Colferai:2010wu
;
Andersen:2011hs
;
Ducloue:2013hia
.
In the last decade or so, the Regge limit has been explored extensively in both
{\cal N}=4
SYM
Bartels:2008ce
;
Dixon:2012yy
;
Basso:2014pla
;
DelDuca:2016lad
;
DelDuca:2019tur
;
Caron-Huot:2020vlo
and QCD
Caron-Huot:2013fea
;
Caron-Huot:2017fxr
;
Caron-Huot:2017zfo
;
Caron-Huot:2020grv
;
Falcioni:2021buo
amplitudes and cross sections
DelDuca:2013lma
;
DelDuca:2017peo
. Furthermore, owing to its relative simplicity, it has been used to constrain, compute or validate amplitudes in general kinematics
DelDuca:2009au
;
DelDuca:2010zg
;
Dixon:2014iba
;
Henn:2016jdu
;
Caron-Huot:2016owq
;
Almelid:2017qju
;
Caron-Huot:2019vjl
;
Falcioni:2021buo
;
Caola:2021izf
.

The BFKL equation
Lipatov:1976zz
;
Kuraev:1976ge
;
Kuraev:1977fs
;
Balitsky:1978ic

describes rapidity evolution in terms of an integral of the gluon propagator exchanged in the
t
channel over all transverse momenta. The kernel of this integral equation consists of two elements, which are described schematically in fig.
1
and
which we review in section
2.1
:
a virtual component corresponding to the Regge trajectory, which first occurs in the one-loop
2\to 2
scattering amplitude at LL accuracy in
\log(s/|t|)
(fig.
1
(a)),
and a real component involving the emission of a gluon into the final state. The latter involves a vertex termed
the Lipatov or
central-emission vertex
(CEV), which first occurs in the tree-level
2\to 3
amplitude in multi-Regge kinematics (MRK) (fig.
1
(b)), where the outgoing partons are strongly ordered in light-cone momentum, or equivalently in rapidity.

Figure 1:
Interference diagrams which contain the building blocks required for constructing the BFKL equation at LL accuracy: (a) gluon Regge trajectory at one loop; (b) one-gluon central emission vertex at tree level. Tree-level impact factors (shown in green) are required for the computation of cross sections, but are not required to determine the evolution of the
t
-channel Reggeon.

Upon integrating the BFKL evolution equation one thus resums corrections in which all emissions are ordered in rapidity, forming the famous ladder graphs. At LL accuracy, each gluon emission along the ladder introduces a factor of
{\cal O}(\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}\log(s/|t|))
after rapidity integration.
The impact factors, eq. (
2
), are not part of the gluon ladder, but sit at its ends. In fig.
1
, they are represented by the green blobs. When they are squared, they constitute the jet impact factors and contribute to jet cross sections at LL accuracy
Mueller:1986ey
;
DelDuca:1993mn
;
Stirling:1994he
;
Andersen:2001kta
;
Andersen:2011hs
.

The
2\to 2
scattering amplitudes at LL accuracy are real-valued, and the terms of
{\cal O}(\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{n}\log^{n}(s/|t|))
exponentiate, manifesting the phenomenon of
gluon Reggeization

Fadin:2020lam
. The hard scattering is effectively mediated by the
t
-channel exchange of a
single
Reggeized gluon, or Reggeon, carrying an octet colour charge, just as at tree level, and it gives rise to a Regge pole in the complex angular momentum plane.

The BFKL equation has been extended to next-to-leading logarithmic (NLL) accuracy
Fadin:1998py
;
Ciafaloni:1998gs
;
Kotikov:2000pm
;
Kotikov:2002ab
, allowing one to resum the terms of
{\cal O}(\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{n}\log^{n-1}(s/|t|))

Fadin:2006bj
;
Fadin:2015zea
.
This generalization involves next-to-leading-order corrections to the BFKL kernel, consisting of three elements, which are depicted schematically in fig.
2
:
two-loop corrections to the Regge trajectory
Fadin:1995xg
;
Fadin:1996tb
;
Fadin:1995km
;
Blumlein:1998ib
;
DelDuca:2001gu
(fig.
2
(a)), one-loop corrections to the central emission vertex
Fadin:1993wh
;
Fadin:1994fj
;
Fadin:1996yv
;
DelDuca:1998cx
;
Bern:1998sc
(fig.
2
(b)), which we review in section
2.2
,
and a new tree-level vertex function for the emission of two gluons (fig.
2
(c)) (or of a quark-antiquark pair) that are
not
strongly ordered in rapidity
Fadin:1989kf
;
DelDuca:1995ki
;
Fadin:1996nw
;
DelDuca:1996nom
;
DelDuca:1996km
, which we review in section
3
.

Figure 2:
Interference diagrams which contain the building blocks required for constructing the BFKL equation at NLL accuracy: (a) gluon Regge trajectory at two loops; (b) one-gluon central-emission vertex at one loop; (c) two-gluon central emission vertex at tree level.

The latter requires the evaluation of a six-parton tree-level amplitude in the so-called next-to-multi-Regge kinematics (NMRK), in which the outgoing partons are strongly ordered in rapidity except for two gluons (or a quark-antiquark pair) emitted centrally off the gluon ladder. When these are integrated in rapidity, they yield a factor of
{\cal O}(\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{2}\log(s/|t|))
, thus contributing at NLL accuracy.
Crucial to carrying out the BFKL program at NLL accuracy is the fact that the real part – or more precisely, the
s\leftrightarrow u
signature-odd part – of the
2\to 2
amplitude is still described by the exchange of a single Reggeized gluon
Fadin:2006bj
;
Fadin:2015zea
. Note that the same is not true for
the imaginary (signature even) part of the amplitude, which is governed by the exchange of a pair of Reggeized gluons
Caron-Huot:2013fea
;
Caron-Huot:2017zfo
;
Caron-Huot:2020grv
.

In order to compute jet cross sections at NLL accuracy
Colferai:2010wu
;
Ducloue:2013hia
through the BFKL equation, jet impact factors at next-to-leading order (NLO) in
\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}

Bartels:2001ge
;
Bartels:2002yj
are required. They are based on the one-loop impact factor
Fadin:1993wh
;
Fadin:1992zt
;
Fadin:1993qb
;
DelDuca:1998kx
;
Bern:1998sc
, eq. (
18
), and the impact factor for the emission of two gluons or of a quark-antiquark pair
Fadin:1989kf
;
DelDuca:1995ki
;
Fadin:1996nw
;
DelDuca:1996nom
;
DelDuca:1996km
;
Duhr:2009uxa
, evaluated in the NMRK in which the gluons are strongly ordered in rapidity except for two partons emitted at one end of the ladder.

Extending the BFKL equation to next-to-next-to-leading logarithmic (NNLL) accuracy faces additional challenges
1
1

1

The BFKL equation has been extended to NNLL
Gromov:2015vua
;
Velizhanin:2015xsa
and to
{\rm N}^{3}
LL accuracy
Velizhanin:2021bdh
in planar
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM theory.
. Chief amongst these is the fact that starting at this logarithmic order, the real part of
2\to 2
amplitudes is not anymore governed by a single Reggeized gluon. Instead, it also involves contributions from three Reggeon exchange
DelDuca:2001gu
;
DelDuca:2014cya
;
Fadin:2017nka
;
Caron-Huot:2017zfo
;
Falcioni:2020lvv
;
Falcioni:2021buo
and the mixing of the latter with a single Reggeon. These give rise to a Regge cut as well as a Regge pole in the complex angular momentum plane. This makes the determination of the three-loop Regge trajectory, characterizing the Regge-pole term, a subtle problem, as it requires to disentangle Regge pole and cut contributions, see e.g.
DelDuca:2014cya
;
Caron-Huot:2017zfo
;
Fadin:2021csi
. Recently this problem has been resolved
Falcioni:2021buo
;
Falcioni:2021dgr

and the three-loop Regge trajectory in QCD has been determined
DelDuca:2021vjq
;
Falcioni:2021dgr
;
Caola:2021izf
. This gives some hope that the analytic structure of amplitudes in the Regge limit can be understood beyond NLL accuracy also in the multi-leg case, and indeed that the BFKL programme could eventually be extended to NNLL and beyond.

Carrying out the BFKL program at NNLL accuracy one needs,
in addition to the three-loop Regge trajectory (fig.
3
(a)), which has recently been determined
DelDuca:2021vjq
;
Falcioni:2021dgr
;
Caola:2021izf
,
various corrections to the central emission vertex. These are described schematically in fig.
3
and include the following elements: the emission vertex of three partons along the gluon ladder
DelDuca:1999iql
;
Antonov:2004hh
;
Duhr:2009uxa
(fig.
3
(e)),
which have been obtained by evaluating seven-parton tree-level amplitudes in next-to-next-to-multi-Regge kinematics (NNMRK);
the square of the one-loop correction to the emission vertex of one gluon (fig.
3
(c));
the one-loop correction to the emission vertex of two gluons (fig.
3
(d)) or of a quark-antiquark pair along the gluon ladder;
and the two-loop correction to the central-emission vertex of one gluon (fig.
3
(b)). The latter two contributions are yet to be determined. In this paper, we compute the one-loop corrections to the central-emission vertex of two gluons in
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM. Utilizing the supersymmetric decomposition of QCD loop amplitudes
Bern:1993mq
;
Bern:1994zx
;
Bern:1994cg
, the
\mathcal{N}=4
result is a first step toward the determination of the same quantity in QCD. To this end we use the one-loop six-gluon amplitudes in
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM based on refs.
Bern:1994zx
;
Bern:1994cg
. By considering the appropriate NMRK kinematic limit of these amplitudes and comparing the result with a Regge-factorized form, we extract the two-gluon central-emission vertex in both the same helicity and opposite helicity configurations.

Figure 3:
Interference diagrams which contain the building blocks required for constructing the BFKL equation at NNLL accuracy: (a) gluon Regge trajectory at three loops; (b) one-gluon CEV at two loops; (c) self-interference of one-gluon CEV at one loop; (d) two-gluon CEV at one loop; (e) three-gluon CEV at tree level.

An important motivation for our investigation, besides the extension of the BFKL program, stems from the potential of the NMRK limit to unravel the analytic structure (as well as the colour structure) of multi-leg amplitudes. Indeed, direct analytical evaluation of multi-leg amplitudes in general kinematics at one-loop level is highly demanding, and the extension to two loops and beyond is ever more so. Even when such amplitudes are known, bringing them to a compact form and evaluating them efficiently are in general unsolved problems, which are of high interest both from a theoretical perspective and because of their immediate relevance to precision collider physics. Understanding the behaviour of amplitudes in various limits, where drastic simplifications occur, is a key strategy towards understanding them in general kinematics. Specifically, in the MRK limit one-loop amplitudes are very simple indeed: they are proportional to the tree amplitude, and involve simple kinematic dependence on the transverse momenta of the emitted gluons. We shall see that the NMRK limit of six gluon amplitudes (already in
{\cal N}=4
SYM) features several new aspects compared to MRK, including new colour structures as well as rational functions that depart from the tree-level amplitude, making it closer in complexity to the amplitude in general kinematics, while still more tractable.

We find that a useful way to organise the one-loop amplitudes in
{\cal N}=4
SYM is by separately considering the dispersive and absorptive parts of the amplitude. We find that in the NMRK limit, the dispersive part of the one-loop amplitude factorises in terms of a one-loop two-gluon CEV, while the absorptive part admits no such simple factorisation. However, upon considering the helicity-summed interference of the one-loop amplitude with the tree-level amplitude, we observe that only the dispersive part of the amplitude contributes at NNLL accuracy, which is a promising result for the prospect of extending the BFKL kernel to this logarithmic accuracy. We also find that simplifications occur when considering the interference of the dispersive part of the one-loop amplitude with the tree-level amplitude: While, as mentioned above, the one-loop two-gluon CEV has an additional, fully symmetric colour structure which does not feature at tree level, this new colour structure does not contribute at NNLL accuracy. It does however, contribute to cross sections at N
3
LL accuracy, where one must consider the square of the one-loop two-gluon CEV, giving rise to a quartic Casimir invariant.

The structure of this paper is as follows.
In section
2
we review the factorised structure of amplitudes at LL and NLL accuracy. In section
3
we review the properties of the tree-level two-gluon central-emission vertex and we obtain new representations of this vertex.
In section
4
we take the central NMRK limit of the one-loop six-gluon amplitude in
{\cal N}=4
SYM. We find that the dispersive part of the amplitude factorises in terms of a one-loop two-gluon CEV. We obtain this CEV for the case of same-helicity gluons in section
4.1
and for the case of opposite-helicity gluons in section
4.2
. In section
4.3
we consider the NLO
gg\to gggg
squared matrix element and show that several simplifications occur in this context. We discuss the implications of these results for the NNLL BFKL kernel. In section
5
we speculate how the behaviour we have observed for six-gluon one-loop amplitudes may be extended to higher multiplicity and loop order. Finally, we conclude in section
6
. Several appendices are included:
in appendices
A
and
B
we display the kinematic regions and invariants of interest; in appendix
C
we list the power-suppressed helicity configurations; in appendix
D
we list the tree-level and one-loop six-gluon NMHV amplitudes in
{\cal N}=4
SYM in general kinematics; in appendix
E
we include
alternative forms of the tree-level opposite-helicity CEV; in appendix
F
we display the absorptive part of the one-loop six-gluon amplitude in
{\cal N}=4
SYM in
the NMRK limit; in appendix
G
we compute the MRK, soft and collinear limits of the one-loop two-gluon CEV.

2
The building blocks of the BFKL equation

In this section we review the factorized structure of four and five gluon amplitudes in the Regge limit.
We define the elements which appear in this factorization, which are also the building blocks of the BFKL equation, as shown schematically in figs.
1
,
2

and
3
. We will need these when discussing the more involved two-gluon central emission vertex in
{\cal N}=4
SYM, which we determine from the six-gluon amplitude in the following sections. As high-energy factorization properties are universal, that is independent of the particular gauge theory being considered, our discussion in the present section will be entirely general. Note however that the functions appearing in the factorized amplitudes, such as impact factors, Regge trajectory and central emission vertices, do depend on the theory under consideration starting from one loop.

2.1
Building blocks at LL accuracy

In the Regge limit, one can write the tree-level
2\to 2
amplitudes in a factorised way. For example, the amplitude for gluon-gluon scattering
g_{1}\,g_{2}\to g_{3}\,g_{4}
at tree level and at fixed helicities may be written as
Lipatov:1976zz
;
Kuraev:1976ge
,

{\cal M}_{4g}^{(0)}=\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{3}})_{a_{2}c}\,C^{g(0)}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}})\right]\frac{s}{t}\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{4}})_{a_{1}c}\,C^{g(0)}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{4}^{\nu_{4}})\right]\,,

(1)

with the incoming momenta
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
parametrised as in eq. (
163
), and with
s=(p_{1}+p_{2})^{2}
,
q=-(p_{2}+p_{3})
,
t=q^{2}\simeq-|q_{\perp}|^{2}
and
(F^{c})_{ab}=i\sqrt{2}f^{acb}
,
and where the superscript
\nu_{i}
labels the helicity of gluon
g_{i}
. We consider all the momenta as outgoing, so the helicities for incoming partons are reversed, see appendix
A.1
.
As is apparent from the colour coefficient
(F^{a_{3}})_{a_{2}c}(F^{a_{4}})_{a_{1}c}
, in eq. (
1
) only the antisymmetric octet
{\bf 8}_{a}
is exchanged in the
t
channel.

Since the four-gluon amplitude is a maximally helicity-violating (MHV) amplitude, eq. (
1
) describes
\binom{4}{2}=6
helicity configurations. However, at leading power in
t/s
, helicity is conserved along the
s
-channel direction, so in eq. (
1
) four helicity configurations are leading, two for each tree impact factor,
g^{*}\,g\rightarrow g
, with
g^{*}
an off-shell gluon
DelDuca:1995zy
,

C^{g(0)}(p_{2}^{\ominus},p_{3}^{\oplus})=1\,,\qquad C^{g(0)}(p_{1}^{\ominus},p_{4}^{\oplus})=\frac{p_{4\perp}^{*}}{p_{4\perp}}\,,

(2)

with complex transverse coordinates
p_{\perp}
as in eq. (
162
). At this order, the impact factors are just overall phases, and they transform under parity into their complex conjugates,

[C^{g}(p^{\nu},p^{\prime\nu^{\prime}})]^{*}=C^{g}(p^{-\nu},p^{\prime-\nu^{\prime}})\,.

(3)

The helicity-flip impact factor
C^{g(0)}(p^{\oplus},p^{\prime\oplus})
and its parity conjugate
C^{g(0)}(p^{\ominus},p^{\prime\ominus})
are power suppressed in
t/s
.
In eq. (
2
), and in what follows, we encircle the helicity index to avoid any confusion with the light-cone direction labelling.

The tree amplitudes for quark-gluon or quark-quark scattering have the same form as eq. (
1
), up to replacing one or both gluon impact factors
C^{g(0)}
(
2
) with quark impact factors
C^{q(0)}
, and the colour factors
(F^{c})_{ab}
in the adjoint representation with the colour factors
T^{c}_{ij}
in the fundamental representation of SU(
N_{c}
), which we normalise as
\mbox{tr\,}(T^{a}T^{b})=T_{F}\delta^{ab}
, with
T_{F}=1
. So in the Regge limit,
2\to 2
scattering amplitudes factorise into gluon or quark impact factors and a gluon propagator in the
t
channel, and are uniquely determined by them.

At LL accuracy in
\log(s/|t|)
, the four-gluon amplitude is given to all orders in
\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}
by Reggeizing the gluon, i.e. by dressing the gluon propagator in eq. (
1
) as
Lipatov:1976zz
;
Kuraev:1976ge

\frac{1}{t}\to\frac{1}{t}\left(\frac{s}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t)}\,,

(4)

where
\tau>0
is a Regge factorisation scale, which is of order of
|t|
, and much smaller than
s
.
The function
\alpha(t)
is the gluon Regge trajectory which is related to the loop transverse-momentum integration. The latter is infrared divergent, and in dimensional regularization with
d=4-2\epsilon
, it can be written as

\alpha(t)=\frac{\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}}{4\pi}\alpha^{(1)}(t)+\mathcal{O}(\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{2})\,,\qquad{\rm with}\qquad\alpha^{(1)}(t)=N_{c}\,\frac{2}{\epsilon}\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{-t}\right)^{\epsilon}\kappa_{\Gamma}\,,

(5)

where for brevity we omit the dependence of
\alpha(t)
on
\mu^{2}
. In eq. (
5
),
\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}\equiv g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{2}/(4\pi)
is the bare coupling, and the
\mathcal{O}(\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{2})
term indicates the presence of higher corrections which become relevant beyond LL accuracy,
N_{c}
is the number of colours, and

\kappa_{\Gamma}\equiv(4\pi)^{\epsilon}\,\frac{\Gamma(1+\epsilon)\,\Gamma^{2}(1-\epsilon)}{\Gamma(1-2\epsilon)}\,.

(6)

It is said that in the Regge limit, the four-gluon amplitude of eq. (
1
) with the dressing in eq. (
4
), is determined by the
t
-channel exchange of a single Reggeized gluon, or Reggeon.

In five-gluon scattering
g_{1}\,g_{2}\to g_{3}\,g_{4}\,g_{5}
, the multi-Regge kinematics (MRK) are defined as a strong ordering in the light-cone momenta,

p_{3}^{+}\gg p_{4}^{+}\gg p_{5}^{+}\,,\qquad|p_{3\perp}|\simeq|p_{4\perp}|\simeq|p_{5\perp}|\,.

(7)

or equivalently in the rapidities, of the produced gluons, eq. (
175
).
In MRK, appendix
A.2
, the tree five-gluon amplitude takes the factorised form (fig.
1
(b)),

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{5g}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle s\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{3}})_{a_{2}c_{1}}\,C^{g(0)}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}}))\right]\,\frac{1}{t_{1}}

(8)

\displaystyle\times\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{4}})_{c_{1}c_{2}}\,V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},q_{2})\right]\,\frac{1}{t_{2}}\,\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{5}})_{a_{1}c_{2}}\,C^{g(0)}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{5}^{\nu_{5}})\right]\,,

with
q_{1}=-(p_{2}+p_{3})
,
q_{2}=q_{1}-p_{4}
, and
t_{i}=q_{i}^{2}\simeq-q_{i\perp}q_{i\perp}^{\ast}
(
i=1,2
). The impact factors are given in eq. (
2
), while the emission vertex of a real gluon from the
t
-channel gluon, which is termed the CEV, is
Lipatov:1976zz
;
Lipatov:1991nf

V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{2})=\frac{q_{1\perp}^{\ast}q_{2\perp}}{p_{4\perp}}\,,

(9)

which transforms under parity into its complex conjugate,

\left[V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu},q_{2})\right]^{\ast}=V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p^{-\nu},q_{2})\,.

(10)

The one-loop gluon Regge trajectory, eq. (
5
), and the tree-level central-emission vertex (CEV), eq. (
9
), are the building blocks of an iterative structure, in the sense that a tree six-gluon amplitude in MRK,
g_{1}g_{2}\to g_{3}g_{4}g_{5}g_{6}
, will display two
CEVs along the gluon ladder, with the four outgoing gluons separated by three large rapidity gaps. At LL accuracy, each of the corresponding
t
-channel gluons Reggeizes according to eq. (
4
) with the one-loop trajectory of eq. (
5
). Similarly, a seven-gluon amplitude in MRK will display three CEVs, and at LL accuracy it will feature four Reggeized
t
-channel gluons across each of the rapidity gaps, and so on for higher-multiplicity amplitudes. This iterative structure is captured by the leading-order BFKL equation. It is depicted in figure
4
, which is suggestive of an all-multiplicity, all-order generalization.

Figure 4:
The factorised structure of gluon amplitudes in MRK. The zigzag line represents the Reggeized gluon propagator given in eq. (
4
).
The coloured blobs represent impact factors, and one-gluon CEVs. At LL accuracy these elements are only required at tree level, but much like the gluon Regge trajectory
\alpha(t)
, they can be defined to all orders in perturbation theory (note that this interpretation departs from the previous figures where the blobs are specific to a given loop order).

2.2
Building blocks at NLL accuracy

As mentioned in the introduction, in order to extend the BFKL equation to NLL accuracy, as done in refs.
Fadin:1998py
;
Ciafaloni:1998gs
;
Kotikov:2000pm
;
Kotikov:2002ab
, one needs three ingredients, which are described schematically by fig.
2
. First, one needs one-loop corrections to the single-gluon CEV of eq. (
9
), which can be extracted from a five-gluon one-loop amplitude
Fadin:1993wh
;
Fadin:1994fj
;
Fadin:1996yv
;
DelDuca:1998cx
;
Bern:1998sc
(fig.
2
(b)), as we show later in this section.
Second, one requires the tree-level CEV for two gluons, which are not ordered in rapidity. This object can be extracted from a tree-level six-gluon amplitude at NMRK
Fadin:1989kf
;
DelDuca:1995ki
;
Fadin:1996nw
;
DelDuca:1996km
;
Duhr:2009uxa
(fig.
2
(c)) and it will be again extracted and analysed in some detail in section
3
below.
Finally, one needs two-loop corrections to the Regge trajectory
\alpha(t)

Fadin:1995xg
;
Fadin:1996tb
;
Fadin:1995km
;
Blumlein:1998ib
;
DelDuca:2001gu
.
The latter may be extracted from
2\to 2
amplitudes at two loops (fig.
2
(a)). This requires an operative prescription to disentangle the Regge trajectory from the impact factor. In what follows, we briefly present such a prescription following ref.
Fadin:1993wh
.

We recall that in the Regge limit,
2\to 2
scattering amplitudes
g_{1}\,g_{2}\to g_{3}\,g_{4}
, can be conveniently split into odd
{\cal M}_{4g}^{(-)}
and even
{\cal M}_{4g}^{(+)}
components under so-called signature symmetry, corresponding to a kinematic
s\leftrightarrow u
interchange,

{\cal M}_{4g}(s,t)={\cal M}_{4g}^{(-)}(s,t)+{\cal M}_{4g}^{(+)}(s,t)\,,\quad\qquad{\cal M}_{4g}^{(\pm)}(s,t)=\frac{{\cal M}_{4g}(s,t)\pm{\cal M}_{4g}(u,t)}{2}\,,

(11)

with
u=-s-t\simeq-s
.
In order to resum energy logarithms in
{\cal M}_{4g}(s,t)
beyond LL accuracy it is useful to define a signature-symmetric logarithm,

L\equiv\log\left(\frac{s}{-t}\right)-\frac{i\pi}{2}=\frac{1}{2}\left[\log\left(\frac{-s-i0}{-t}\right)+\log\left(\frac{-u}{-t}\right)\right],

(12)

and expand the amplitude at leading power in the Regge limit as

{\cal M}_{4g}^{(\pm)}=g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{2}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}}{4\pi}\right)^{n}\sum_{m=0}^{n}L^{m}{\cal M}_{4g}^{(\pm,n,m)}\,,

(13)

with
m=n
yielding the coefficients at LL accuracy,
m=n-1
the coefficients at NLL accuracy, and so on.
With eqs. (
12
) and (
13
) at work, one can show
Caron-Huot:2017fxr
that the coefficients of the odd (even) amplitude,
{\cal M}_{4g}^{(\mp,n,m)}
, are purely real (imaginary). Moreover,
{\cal M}_{4g}^{(-)}
only receives contributions from
t
-channel exchange of an odd number of Reggeons, while
{\cal M}_{4g}^{(+)}
, from an even number.

Owing to Bose symmetry, also the colour factors of
{\cal M}_{4g}^{(-)}
and
{\cal M}_{4g}^{(+)}
are respectively odd and even under the interchange of the colour indices of the corresponding gluons
g_{2}
and
g_{3}
(or, instead, those of
g_{1}
and
g_{4}
). The tree amplitude in eq. (
1
) is entirely odd under signature, and indeed, its colour structure is antisymmetric under interchanging
a_{2}
and
a_{3}
, corresponding to a pure
t
-channel octet exchange,
{\bf 8}_{a}
.
Higher-order contributions to this process
involve in general additional colour structures, as dictated by the decomposition of the product
{\bf 8}_{a}\otimes{\bf 8}_{a}
into irreducible representations,

{\bf 8}_{a}\otimes{\bf 8}_{a}\,=\,\{{\bf 1}\oplus{\bf 8}_{s}\oplus{\bf 27}\}\oplus[{\bf 8}_{a}\oplus{\bf 10}\oplus\overline{{\bf 10}}]\ \,,

(14)

where the curly (square) brackets contain the representations which are even (odd) under signature. However, at NLL accuracy, the signature-odd component of the amplitude, i.e.
{\cal M}_{4g}^{(-,n,n-1)}
for all
n
in eq. (
13
), is still governed by the exchange of a single Reggeon in the
t
channel, and eq. (
4
) generalises in a straightforward way to
Fadin:1993wh
,

\displaystyle{{\cal M}^{(-)[8_{a}]}_{4g}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{3}})_{a_{2}c}\,C^{g}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}}))\right]\frac{s}{t}\left[\left(\frac{s}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t)}+\left(\frac{-s}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t)}\right]\,\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{4}})_{a_{1}c}\,C^{g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{4}^{\nu_{4}})\right]\,,

(15)

where the colour and kinematic parts of the amplitude are each odd under
g_{2}\leftrightarrow g_{3}
interchange.

In eq. (
15
), we expand the gluon Regge trajectory in
\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}
as

\alpha(t)=\frac{\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}}{4\pi}\alpha^{(1)}(t)+\left(\frac{\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\alpha^{(2)}(t)+{\cal O}(\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{3})\,,

(16)

with
\alpha^{(1)}
in eq. (
5
), and
\alpha^{(2)}
is the two-loop Regge trajectory which enters the BFKL equation at NLL accuracy. Likewise, we expand the helicity-conserving impact factor,

C^{g}(p^{\oplus},p^{\prime\ominus})=C^{g(0)}(p^{\oplus},p^{\prime\ominus})+\frac{\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}}{4\pi}\,C^{g(1)}(p^{\oplus},p^{\prime\ominus})+\mathcal{O}\left(\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{2}\right)\,,

(17)

with one-loop corrections,

C^{g(1)}(p^{\oplus},p^{\prime\ominus})=C^{g(0)}(p^{\oplus},p^{\prime\ominus})\,c^{g(1)}(t)\,.

(18)

The same applies to the opposite helicity-conserving impact factor
C^{g}(p^{\ominus},p^{\prime\oplus})
. Note that the one-loop coefficients
c^{g(1)}
are real and independent of the helicity configuration. The coefficient
c^{g(1)}
, and therefore also
C^{g}
, depend on the renormalisation and Regge-factorisation scales
\mu^{2}
and
\tau
, but we omit this dependence for brevity.

The
2\to 3
scattering amplitudes,
g_{1}\,g_{2}\to g_{3}\,g_{4}\,g_{5}
, can be likewise split into odd and even components under signature symmetry,

{\cal M}_{5g}={\cal M}_{5g}^{(+,+)}+{\cal M}_{5g}^{(+,-)}+{\cal M}_{5g}^{(-,+)}+{\cal M}_{5g}^{(-,-)}\,,

(19)

with

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{5g}^{(\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b})}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5}^{\nu_{5}})

(20)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}{\cal M}_{5g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5}^{\nu_{5}})+\frac{\sigma_{a}}{4}{\cal M}_{5g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5}^{\nu_{5}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{b}}{4}{\cal M}_{5g}(p_{5}^{\nu_{5}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{1}^{\nu_{1}})+\frac{\sigma_{a}\sigma_{b}}{4}{\cal M}_{5g}(p_{5}^{\nu_{5}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{1}^{\nu_{1}})\,,

where the two signature indices
\sigma_{a}
and
\sigma_{b}
can separately be
+1
or
-1
.
By Bose symmetry, the upper and lower vertices (see fig. (
11
)) then acquire a corresponding colour symmetry: the upper vertex is symmetric/antisymmetric upon interchanging the colour indices
a_{2}
and
a_{3}
for
\sigma_{a}=+
and
\sigma_{a}=-
, respectively. Similarly, the lower one is symmetric/antisymmetric upon interchanging
a_{1}
and
a_{5}
for
\sigma_{b}=+
and
\sigma_{b}=-
, respectively.
Specifically, the
{\cal M}^{(-,-)}
amplitude, which we discuss below, is
antisymmetric in colour in both the upper and lower vertices.

In MRK, eq. (
20
) is understood to involve the exchanges
s_{34}\leftrightarrow s_{24}=-s_{34}
and
s_{45}\leftrightarrow s_{41}=-s_{45}
along with the associated change in sign of
s=s_{12}=-s_{13}=-s_{52}=s_{35}
.
At NLL accuracy, the signature odd-odd component of the amplitude,
{\cal M}^{(-,-)}
, is governed
by an antisymmetric octet exchange,
{\bf 8}_{a}
, in the
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
channels,
corresponding to a single Reggeon. One can then generalise eq. (
8
),

\displaystyle{\cal M}^{(-,-)[8_{a}]}_{5g}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}s\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{3}})_{a_{2}c_{1}}\,C^{g}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}}))\right]\,\frac{1}{t_{1}}\left[\left(\frac{s_{34}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{1})}+\left(\frac{-s_{34}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{1})}\right]

(21)

\displaystyle\times\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{4}})_{c_{1}c_{2}}\,V^{g}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},q_{2})\right]\,\frac{1}{t_{2}}\left[\left(\frac{s_{45}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{2})}+\left(\frac{-s_{45}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{2})}\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{5}})_{a_{1}c_{2}}\,C^{g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{5}^{\nu_{5}})\right]\,,

where the CEV now includes one-loop corrections

\displaystyle V^{g}(q_{1},p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},q_{2})=V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},q_{2})+\frac{\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}}{4\pi}V^{g(1)}(q_{1},p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},q_{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{2}\right)\,,

(22)

which we express as

V^{g(1)}(q_{1},p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},q_{2})=V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},q_{2})\,v^{g(1)}(t_{1},|p_{4\perp}|^{2},t_{2})\,.

(23)

where
v^{g(1)}
is real and helicity-independent. Similarly to
c^{g(1)}
, we omit the dependence on the scales
\mu^{2}
and
\tau
in
v^{g(1)}
and
V^{g}
.

Finally, we note that
while the signature-odd NLL
gg\to gg
amplitude is governed by a pure
t
-channel
{\bf 8}_{a}
exchange, leading to the simple resummation formula in eq. (
15
), the signature-even NLL amplitude
{\cal M}_{4g}^{(+)}
, which is purely imaginary, is governed by two-Reggeon exchange, leading to a more complex structure involving all
t
-channel representations that are compatible with the even signature in (
14
), namely the singlet
{\bf 1}
, the symmetric octet
{\bf 8}_{s}
and the
{\bf 27}
. Such a contribution occurs already in the non-logarithmic signature-even one-loop term
{\cal M}_{4g}^{(+,1,0)}
, which is proportional to
i\pi

DelDuca:1998kx
. More generally, the signature-even amplitude obeys the BFKL equation
Kuraev:1976ge
;
Kuraev:1977fs
;
Balitsky:1978ic
: it is described by ladder graphs formed between two Reggeized gluons, which admits an all-order iterative solution
Caron-Huot:2013fea
;
Caron-Huot:2017zfo
;
Caron-Huot:2020grv
, rendering both infrared-singular and finite corrections

{\cal M}_{4g}^{(+,n,n-1)}
computable for all
n
for general colour.
The signature-even gluon-gluon amplitude at NLL accuracy can only contribute to a squared amplitude, and thus to a cross section, at NNLL accuracy.

2.3
Building blocks at NNLL accuracy

As outlined in the introduction, computing the next-to-next-to-leading-order corrections to the BFKL kernel requires to determine five elements:
the three-loop correction to the Regge trajectory
DelDuca:2021vjq
;
Falcioni:2021dgr
;
Caola:2021izf
(fig.
3
(a));
the two-loop correction to the single-gluon CEV
2
2

2

The two-loop correction to the single-gluon CEV is known in planar
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM theory
DelDuca:2009ae
.
(fig.
3
(b));
the square of the one-loop correction to the single-gluon CEV
3
3

3

The one-loop correction to the single-gluon CEV is known in planar
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM theory to all orders
of
\epsilon

DelDuca:2009ae
. Beyond
{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})
, the
one-loop single-gluon CEV requires the one-loop pentagon in
6-2\epsilon
dimensions
DelDuca:2009ac
;
Kniehl:2010aj
;
Kozlov:2015kol
;
Syrrakos:2020kba
.
(fig.
3
(c));
the one-loop correction to the two-parton (two gluons or a
quark-antiquark pair) CEV (fig.
3
(d));
the tree-level three-parton CEV (fig.
3
(e)), which is known for the case of three gluons
DelDuca:1999iql
;
Antonov:2004hh
;
Duhr:2009uxa
. We note in passing that the tree-level four-gluon CEV, which would occur at
{\rm N^{3}}
LL accuracy, is also known
Duhr:2009uxa
.

Computing the three-loop gluon Regge trajectory has required to tackle a new hurdle: starting at NNLL accuracy the signature-odd part of the
gg\to gg
amplitude (namely its real part) cannot be described purely in terms of a single-Reggeon exchange
DelDuca:2001gu
;
DelDuca:2011wkl
;
DelDuca:2011ae
;
DelDuca:2013ara
;
DelDuca:2014cya
;
Fadin:2016wso
;
Caron-Huot:2017fxr
;
Fadin:2017nka
;
Falcioni:2020lvv
;
Falcioni:2021buo
;
Falcioni:2021dgr
. Instead, contributions from three Reggeon exchange occur, starting from two loops
Fadin:2016wso
;
Caron-Huot:2017fxr
;
Fadin:2017nka
, and furthermore, three-Reggeon mixing with a single Reggeon kicks in starting at three loops
Caron-Huot:2017fxr
. As a consequence, a resummation formula of the form eq. (
15
), which corresponds to a pure Regge pole in the complex angular momentum plane, is insufficient. It must be supplemented by additional contributions which do not admit such simple factorization and exponentiation properties, and build up a Regge-cut in the complex angular momentum plane.
Since
t
-channel
{\bf 8}_{a}
contributions, which are proportional to the tree amplitude, arise from both single and multiple Reggeon exchange, it had not been obvious how to disentangle Regge pole and cut contributions when working at fixed order.

Infrared factorisation shows that
Bret:2011xm
;
DelDuca:2011ae
, in addition to the usual diagonal terms of the colour octet exchange, also non-diagonal terms in the
t
-channel colour basis contribute to the real part of
M^{(-,2,0)}_{4g}

DelDuca:2013ara
;
DelDuca:2014cya
.
More recently, following the computation of NNLL corrections
{\cal M}_{4g}^{(-,n,n-2)}
to the odd amplitude through four loops
Falcioni:2020lvv
;
Falcioni:2021buo
,
the structure of the NNLL tower has been understood
Falcioni:2021dgr
, uncovering a deep connection to the non-planar origin of Regge cuts
Mandelstam:1965zz
;
Collins:1977jy
.
Specifically, it has become clear that
planar
multi-Reggeon contributions at NNLL only occur at two and three loops. These contribute to the Regge pole, and factorise along with the single-Reggeon contribution as in eq. (
15
), while the
non-planar
multi-Reggeon contributions are precisely those that build up the Regge cut. Accordingly, the three-loop gluon Regge trajectory has been determined
DelDuca:2021vjq
;
Falcioni:2021dgr
;
Caola:2021izf
, providing the fully-virtual input, shown in fig.
3
(a), that is necessary for extending the BFKL equation to NNLL accuracy,

While the two-loop corrections to the single-gluon CEV (fig.
3
(b)) are yet to be determined, in this paper, we evaluate the one-loop corrections to the two-gluon CEV (fig.
3
(d)) in
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM, as a first step toward computing the same quantity in QCD.

Let us also note that in order to compute jet cross sections at NNLL accuracy through the BFKL
equation, jet impact factors at next-to-next-to-leading order (NNLO) in
\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}
will be needed. Building blocks to evaluate the latter are the two-loop impact factors
DelDuca:2014cya
;
the one-loop impact factors for the emission of two gluons
Canay:2021yvm
or of a quark-antiquark pair, evaluated in NMRK; and the tree impact factor for the emission of three partons at one end of the ladder
DelDuca:1999iql
evaluated in NNMRK. Further, one must include the square of the one-loop helicity-violating impact factor
DelDuca:1998kx
.

3
The two-gluon central-emission vertex at tree level

In this section we investigate several equivalent representations of the two-gluon CEV at tree level. In section
3.1
we introduce a new set of kinematic variables which, in particular, greatly simplifies the task of showing equivalence between the different representations of the CEV. In section
3.2
we obtain new representations for the opposite-helicity CEV from amplitudes that were derived via Britto-Cachazo-Feng-Witten (BCFW) recursion relations
Britto:2004ap
;
Britto:2005fq
. Not only do these representations make clear the collinear structure of the tree-level CEV, the individual rational terms that comprise the BCFW amplitudes appear also in the one-loop six-gluon NMHV amplitudes of
{\cal N}=4
SYM. The study of these rational terms therefore also lays the groundwork for section
4
, where we obtain the one-loop two-gluon CEV in
{\cal N}=4
SYM.

We begin by reviewing the factorisation properties of six-gluon amplitudes in the central NMRK. We consider the production of four gluons of momenta
p_{i}
, with
i=3,4,5,6
in the scattering
of two gluons of momenta
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
, with the momenta parametrised in terms of lightcone coordinates as in eq. (
163
). We suppose that the outgoing gluons are in a central NMRK configuration, as described in appendix
A.3
,
where the rapidities and transverse momenta of the outgoing gluons satisfy

y_{3}\gg y_{4}\simeq y_{5}\gg y_{6}\,;\qquad|p_{3\perp}|\simeq|p_{4\perp}|\simeq|p_{5\perp}|\simeq|p_{6\perp}|\,.

(24)

In the NMRK region of eq. (
24
), the tree-level amplitude for the
g_{1}\,g_{2}\to g_{3}\,g_{4}\,g_{5}\,g_{6}
scattering process is
Fadin:1989kf
;
DelDuca:1995ki
;
Fadin:1996nw
;
DelDuca:1996km
:

\displaystyle\left.{\cal M}^{(0)}_{6g}\right|_{\rm NMRK}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2}}g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{4}\,(F^{a_{3}})_{a_{2}c_{1}}(F^{a_{\sigma_{4}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{5}}})_{c_{1}c_{3}}(F^{a_{6}})_{a_{1}c_{3}}\,M^{(0)}_{6g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{4}}}_{\sigma_{4}},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{5}}}_{\sigma_{5}},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}})\,,

(25)

where the sum is over the two permutations of the gluon labels 4 and 5, and on the right-hand side an NMRK subscript on the colour-ordered amplitudes
M^{(0)}_{6g}
is understood. The latter factorise in this limit as

\displaystyle M^{(0)}_{6g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}})

\displaystyle=s\,C^{g(0)}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}})\,\frac{1}{t_{1}}\,A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})\,\frac{1}{t_{3}}\,C^{g(0)}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}})\,,

(26)

with
s=s_{12}=(p_{1}+p_{2})^{2}
,
q_{1}=-(p_{2}+p_{3})
,
q_{3}=p_{1}+p_{6}
,
s_{23}=t_{1}=q_{1}^{2}\simeq-|q_{1\perp}|^{2}
,

s_{61}=t_{3}=q_{3}^{2}\simeq-|q_{3\perp}|^{2}
.
Here
A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})
is the tree-level two-gluon central-emission vertex, which is the subject of this section. This quantity has been studied previously
Fadin:1989kf
;
DelDuca:1995ki
;
Fadin:1996nw
;
DelDuca:1996km
and our analysis here is primarily intended to prepare the grounds for the determination of this quantity at one loop in the next section.

Since the six-gluon amplitude can be in either a maximally helicity-violating (MHV) or next-to-maximally helicity-violating (NMHV)
configuration, eq. (
25
) describes a total of
2\binom{6}{2}+\binom{6}{3}=50
helicity configurations.
Out of these, 34 configurations are associated to amplitudes which have at least one helicity-flip impact factor, appendix
C
, and thus are power suppressed in
t_{i}/s
, with
i=1,3
. Conversely, at leading power in
t_{i}/s
, helicity is conserved along the
s
-channel direction in Minkowski space,
so in eq. (
25
) 16 helicity configurations are leading. Two are associated to each tree impact factor,
g^{*}\,g\rightarrow g
, with
g^{*}
an off-shell gluon, eq. (
2
).

Multiplying the four helicity configurations associated to the impact factors, there are four helicity configurations associated to the
central-emission vertex for the emission of two gluons,
g^{*}\,g^{*}\rightarrow g\,g
, which read
4
4

4

Note that having defined
(F^{c})_{ab}=i\sqrt{2}f^{acb}
, the central-emission vertices, eqs. (
9
), (
27
) and (
28
), do not display the usual overall powers of
\sqrt{2}
.

Fadin:1989kf
;
DelDuca:1995ki
;
Fadin:1996nw
;
DelDuca:1996km

\displaystyle A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{q_{1\perp}^{\ast}q_{3\perp}}{p_{4\perp}}\sqrt{\frac{x_{4}}{x_{5}}}\,\frac{1}{\langle 4\,5\rangle}\,,

(27)

\displaystyle\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\right|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{4\perp}^{\ast}}{p_{4\perp}}\left\{-\frac{1}{s_{45}}\left[\frac{y_{5}\,p_{5\perp}|q_{1\perp}|^{2}}{p_{5\perp}^{\ast}}+\frac{x_{4}\,p_{4\perp}|q_{3\perp}|^{2}}{p_{4\perp}^{\ast}}+\frac{s_{234}\,p_{4\perp}p_{5\perp}}{p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+}}\right]\right.

(28)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\left.\frac{(q_{3\perp}+p_{5\perp})^{2}}{s_{234}}-\frac{q_{3\perp}+p_{5\perp}}{s_{45}}\left[\frac{p_{4\perp}}{y_{4}}-\frac{p_{5\perp}}{x_{5}}\right]\right\}\,,

where the kinematic invariants are given in eqs. (
186
), (
188
), and (
191
), and where we use the momentum fractions,

x_{i}=\frac{p_{i}^{+}}{p_{4}^{+}+p_{5}^{+}}\,,\qquad y_{i}=\frac{p_{i}^{-}}{p_{4}^{-}+p_{5}^{-}}\,,\qquad i=4,5\,.

(29)

In eqs. (
27
)–(
28
), similarly to eq. (
2
), we have encircled the helicity index
and we have appended the index
\left.\right|_{1}
to the opposite-helicity vertex (eq. (
28
)) in order to stress that this is
the first amongst several equivalent representations we are going to consider for this quantity.

Note that the impact factors, eq. (
3
), and the central-emission vertex, eqs. (
27
)–(
28
), transform under parity into their complex conjugates,

\left[A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})\right]^{\ast}=A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{-\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{-\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})\,,

(30)

allowing one to obtain
A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})
and

A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3})
from those in eq. (
28
). If the further MRK limit is taken,
i.e. further requiring
p_{4}^{+}\gg p_{5}^{+}
, the two-gluon CEV factorises into a product of two Lipatov vertices times an intermediate
t
-channel pole. The MRK limit is discussed in appendix
G.1
.

In ref.
Duhr:2009uxa
an alternative form of the opposite-helicity two-gluon CEV was found by using the CSW rules
Cachazo:2004kj
, eq. (
220
).
This is a compact form of the vertex, consisting of only four rational terms, but unlike eq. (
28
), this representation appears to have unphysical poles e.g. at
p_{4\perp}+p_{5\perp}=0
. This is a feature which is shared with the new representations we will obtain from the BCFW recursion relations in section
3.2
. Before obtaining these new representations, we will first introduce a minimal set of independent variables which we will use throughout this paper. In particular, these variables will make it simple to analytically verify the equivalence of all the representations of the opposite-helicity vertex we will study, thereby proving that the apparent unphysical singularites in the CSW and BCFW representations are spurious.

3.1
A minimal set of variables

In MRK the central-emission vertex for the emission of one gluon can be expressed in terms of one independent
transverse momentum, i.e. in terms of two real parameters. We expect that the emission of one more gluon out of the central-emission vertex will require the introduction of the variables of one more on-shell massless particle, i.e. three more real parameters, such that the central-emission vertex for the emission of
n
gluons will be expressed in terms of a set of
(3n-1)
independent real parameters. In particular, the central-emission vertex for the emission of two gluons will be given in terms of five real parameters.

We write
p_{4\perp}
and
p_{5\perp}
in terms of MRK
t
-channel transverse momenta,

p_{4\perp}=q_{1\perp}-q_{2\perp}\,,\qquad p_{5\perp}=q_{2\perp}-q_{3\perp}\,.

(31)

In the sum of the equations above,
q_{2\perp}
cancels such that transverse momentum conservation in NMRK is fulfilled. We parametrise the ratios of transverse momenta in eq. (
31
) in terms of two complex variables,
w
and
z
,

\displaystyle\frac{p_{4\perp}}{q_{1\perp}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-z}\,,\qquad\frac{q_{2\perp}}{q_{1\perp}}=\frac{-z}{1-z}\,,

\displaystyle\frac{p_{5\perp}}{q_{3\perp}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{1-w}\,,\qquad\frac{q_{2\perp}}{q_{3\perp}}=\frac{-w}{1-w}\,.

(32)

Inverting these relations gives us

z=-\frac{q_{2\perp}}{p_{4\perp}}\,,\qquad w=\frac{q_{2\perp}}{p_{5\perp}}.

(33)

Using complex parametrization of the transverse momenta (see appendix
A.1
),
z
and
w
are also complex. We will use
\bar{z}
and
\bar{w}
to denote their complex conjugates.

In summary, we may parametrise the central-emission vertex for the emission of two gluons, involving five real degrees of freedom, in terms of the set
\{w,z,X\}
, where
X

is a real positive variable which depends on the rapidity difference between
p_{4}
and
p_{5}
,

X\equiv\frac{p_{4}^{+}}{p_{5}^{+}}=\frac{x_{4}}{x_{5}}\,.

(34)

The MRK limit is reached by taking
X\to\infty
. When we use the acronym MRK, we will always mean this canonical MRK limit with the strict rapidity ordering
y_{3}\gg y_{4}\gg y_{5}\gg y_{6}
. The multi-Regge regime with strict rapidity ordering
y_{3}\gg y_{5}\gg y_{4}\gg y_{6}
is also contained within the central NMRK limit of appendix
A.3
, and we will refer to this kinematic condition as
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
. The
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limit is reached by taking
X\to 0
.

The leading NMRK behaviour of several Lorentz invariant quantities are listed in terms of the minimal set of variables in appendix
B
. In terms of this minimal set, eqs. (
27
)–(
28
) become

\displaystyle A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3})=

\displaystyle\frac{q_{1\perp}^{*}}{q_{1\perp}}\frac{z(w-1)(z-1)X}{(w+Xz)}\,,

(35)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\right|_{1}=&\frac{\bar{w}^{2}z^{2}|z-1|^{2}X^{2}}{|w+Xz|^{2}(|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2})}+\frac{z^{2}X}{(1+X|z|^{2})}\\
+&\frac{Xz}{|w+Xz|^{2}}\bigg(\frac{|w-1|^{2}X\bar{z}}{(1+X)}+\bar{w}(1+X|z|^{2})\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt-(|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2})-\bar{w}z(1+X)\bigg)\,.\end{split}

(36)

We note that the same-helicity vertex, eq. (
35
), contains a remaining phase
q_{1\perp}^{*}/q_{1\perp}
, which cannot be expressed in terms of the minimal variables. From eq. (
195
) we see that the physical singularity of this vertex corresponds to

\displaystyle\langle 4\,5\rangle=0\quad\leftrightarrow\quad w+Xz=0\,.

(37)

The opposite-helicity vertex, eq. (
36
), has a more complicated singularity structure. From eq. (
196
) we see that the further physical singularities of this vertex correspond to the vanishing of three-particle Mandelstam invariants,

\begin{array}[]{rrrrr}s_{123}=0&\leftrightarrow&\quad 1+X=0\,,\\
s_{234}=0&\leftrightarrow&\quad 1+X|z|^{2}=0\,,\\
s_{345}=0&\leftrightarrow&\quad|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2}=0\,.\\
\end{array}

(38)

The right-hand side of eq. (
37
) and eq. (
38
) define the physical-singularity surfaces of the minimal set
\{w,z,X\}
.
However, in NMRK, if we require all transverse momenta to be non-vanishing, the only singularity surface which can be reached is the collinear limit
w=-zX
. This collinear limit is studied in appendix
G.3
. This agrees with the intuitive picture of NMRK where, for example, the large rapidity separation
y_{3}\gg y_{4}
means we cannot take the further collinear limit
p_{3}||p_{4}
. Relaxing the requirement of non-vanishing transverse momenta, eqs. (
35
) and (
36
) are singular in the soft limits
p_{4}\to 0
and
p_{5}\to 0
, which are explored in appendix
G.2
.

3.2
BCFW representations

In ref.
DelDuca:1996km
, the opposite-helicity vertex of eq. (
28
) was obtained from the tree-level six-gluon amplitude given in ref.
Mangano:1987xk
. Compact expressions for NMHV six-gluon amplitudes were later derived using the BCFW on-shell recursion relations
Britto:2004ap
;
Britto:2005fq
. We present one of the amplitudes obtained in ref.
Britto:2004ap
here, which we will use as an example in the following
5
5

5

This amplitude is equal to the complex conjugate of eq. (
209
) and we have expanded the
D_{i}
functions to display the physical and spurious poles of the amplitude.
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}M^{(0)}_{(\ref {eq:BCFWexample})}\left(p_{1}^{\oplus},p_{2}^{\oplus},p_{3}^{\ominus},p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus},p_{6}^{\ominus}\right)=&\ -\frac{\langle 3|1+2|4]^{4}}{\langle 1\,2\rangle\langle 2\,3\rangle[4\,5][5\,6]s_{123}\langle 1|2+3|4]\langle 3|4+5|6]}\\
&-\frac{\langle 5\,6\rangle^{4}[2\,4]^{4}}{\langle 5\,6\rangle\langle 6\,1\rangle[2\,3][3\,4]s_{234}\langle 5|6+1|2]\langle 1|2+3|4]}\\
&-\frac{\langle 3\,5\rangle^{4}[1\,2]^{4}}{[6\,1][1\,2]\langle 3\,4\rangle\langle 4\,5\rangle s_{345}\langle 3|4+5|6]\langle 5|6+1|2]}.\end{split}

(39)

A complete set of six-gluon NMHV tree amplitudes are listed in eqs. (
208
)–(
210
). A second set of representations of the tree-level amplitudes are given by eqs. (
213
)–(
215
); these are discussed further in appendix
D.2
. As mentioned earlier, the individual terms of these BCFW-based amplitudes appear also in the six-gluon one-loop amplitudes in
{\cal N}=4
SYM. As an example, note that the rational coefficients that appear in the one-loop
amplitude (
217
) consist of the individual rational terms that make up the tree-level amplitudes (
209
) and (
214
). In this section we are therefore interested not only in the NMRK limit of the tree-level amplitudes as a whole, but also in the NMRK behaviour of the individual rational terms which will appear outside the context of tree-level amplitudes in section
4.2
.

BCFW-based representations have the additional advantage of making the collinear singularity structure of the tree amplitudes manifest.
For example, eq. (
39
) has separate poles at
\langle 4\ 5\rangle=0
and
[45]=0
, while eq. (
28
) only displays a pole at
s_{45}=0
. The form in eq. (
28
) entangles the two types of contributions, from different helicity intermediate states; also, individual terms are more singular than the actual amplitude. The BCFW-based representations are therefore more amenable to the study of collinear limits, a property which is utilised in appendix
G.3
.
In addition to physical singularities (spinor products of two cyclically adjacent momenta, and Mandelstam invariants of three cyclically adjacent momenta), the individual terms in these BCFW-based representations also contain unphysical singularities (which cancel in the sum of terms appearing in the amplitude). A simple example is provided by the spinor string

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle 3|4+5|6]\,&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}\,-q_{3\perp}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{p_{3}^{+}}{p_{6}^{+}}}\,\left(p_{4\perp}+p_{5\perp}\right)\,=\,|q_{1\perp}|^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p_{3}^{+}}{p_{6}^{+}}}\,\frac{\bar{z}(\bar{w}-1)(w-z)}{|w|^{2}|z-1|^{2}}\end{split}

(40)

which appears in the denominator of terms in eq. (
39
).

For six-particle kinematics there is a simple relationship between the square of such spinor strings and two- and three-particle Mandelstam invariants,

\langle 3|4+5|6]\langle 6|4+5|3]=s_{123}s_{345}\left(1-\frac{s_{12}s_{45}}{s_{123}s_{345}}\right).

(41)

The quantity appearing on the right-hand side,
s_{12}s_{45}/(s_{123}s_{345})
, is a conformally-invariant cross ratio
Drummond:2007au
. In this way we see that each of the unphysical singularity surfaces of the rational terms in eqs. (
208
)–(
210
) corresponds to one of the following cross ratios equalling unity,

\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}u_{A}&=&\frac{s_{12}s_{45}}{s_{123}s_{345}}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&\frac{s_{45}}{(p_{4}^{+}+p_{5}^{+})(p_{4}^{-}+p_{5}^{-})}&=&\frac{|w+Xz|^{2}}{(1+X)(|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2})}\,,\\
v_{A}&=&\frac{s_{23}s_{56}}{s_{234}s_{123}}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&\frac{(-t_{1})p_{5}^{+}}{(|q_{2\perp}|^{2}+p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+})(p_{4}^{+}+p_{5}^{+})}&=&\frac{X|1-z|^{2}}{(1+X|z|^{2})(1+X)}\,,\\
w_{A}&=&\frac{s_{34}s_{61}}{s_{345}s_{234}}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&\frac{(-t_{3})p_{4}^{-}}{(p_{4}^{-}+p_{5}^{-})(|q_{2\perp}|^{2}+p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+})}&=&\frac{X|1-w|^{2}|z|^{2}}{(|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2})(1+X|z|^{2})}\,.\end{array}

(42)

We use the label
A
to indicate that these are the cross ratios of the colour ordering
\{1,2,3,4,5,6\}
, which we will refer to as
\sigma_{A}
. In the NMRK region these cross ratios lie in the range
[0,1]
, and are studied further in appendix
B.1
.
We choose to label the unphysical singularities of the BCFW representations by the cross ratio which tends to unity as this unphysical singularity surface is approached,

P_{x}^{A}=0\quad\Rightarrow\quad x_{A}=1,\qquad x\in\{u,v,w\}.

(43)

This notation will be helpful in section
4.2
for demonstrating that the NMHV one-loop six-gluon amplitudes in
{\cal N}=4
are free from unphysical singularities.
We use the term
P_{x}^{A}
to generically refer to the unphysical-singularity surfaces of the terms in the BCFW representation of the amplitudes. We list these unphysical-singularity surfaces in NMRK in terms of both light-cone coordinates and the minimal variables,

\begin{array}[]{rrrrrrr}\langle 1|2+3|4]=0&\ \leftrightarrow&P^{A}_{v}=0&\ \leftrightarrow&q_{2\perp}^{*}-p_{4\perp}^{*}\displaystyle{\frac{p_{5}^{+}}{p_{4}^{+}}}=0&\ \leftrightarrow&1+X\bar{z}=0\,,\\
\langle 3|4+5|6]=0&\leftrightarrow&P^{A}_{u}=0&\leftrightarrow&p_{4\perp}+p_{5\perp}=0&\leftrightarrow&w-z=0\,,\\
\langle 5|6+1|2]=0&\leftrightarrow&P^{A}_{w}=0&\leftrightarrow&q_{2\perp}^{*}+p_{5\perp}^{*}\displaystyle{\frac{p_{4}^{-}}{p_{5}^{-}}}=0&\leftrightarrow&w+X|z|^{2}=0\,,\\
\end{array}

(44)

which provides intuition for the origin and nature of the unphysical singularities that appear in eqs. (
45
) and (
47
) below. Equations (
192
) and (
197
) list the full expressions for the NMRK limit of these spinor strings in lightcone and minimal coordinates respectively, from which the unphysical singularity surfaces of eq. (
44
) can be identified.

It is interesting to note that a given BCFW representation of a tree amplitude can give rise to different representations of the opposite-helicity two-gluon CEV. As an example, we can obtain a representation for this CEV from eq. (
39
) by taking the central NMRK limit of this amplitude and dividing by impact factors and
t
-channel propagators according in eq. (
26
). In this way we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\right|_{2}=R^{A}_{uv}+R^{A}_{vw}+R^{A}_{wu}\,,\end{split}

(45)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{A}_{uv}&=\frac{X^{3}|w-1|^{2}(\bar{z}-1)|z|^{2}}{(X+1)(\bar{w}+X\bar{z})(w-z)(1+X\bar{z})}\,,\\
R^{A}_{vw}&=\frac{zX(w-1)(z-1)}{(1+X|z|^{2})(1+X\bar{z})(w+X|z|^{2})}\,,\\
R^{A}_{wu}&=-\frac{z^{3}X^{3}(\bar{w}-1)|z-1|^{2}|z|^{2}}{(w+Xz)(|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2})(w+X|z|^{2})(w-z)}\,.\end{split}

(46)

Eq. (
45
) is our second representation for the opposite helicity vertex, equivalent to eq. (
36
) above.
In addition to the physical singularities of eq. (
37
) and eq. (
38
), individual terms in eq. (
45
) feature the unphysical singularities of eq. (
44
). We denote the rational terms in (
46
) by their singularities, where
R^{A}_{xy}
features unphysical poles at
P_{x}^{A}=0
and
P_{y}^{A}=0
.

We can obtain another equivalent representation for the opposite-helicity vertex from eq. (
39
) by taking the central NMRK limit of

M^{(0)}_{(\ref {eq:BCFWexample})}(p_{3}^{\oplus},p_{4}^{\ominus},p_{5}^{\oplus},p_{6}^{\oplus},p_{1}^{\ominus},p_{2}^{\ominus})^{*}.

This gives us our third representation of the CEV,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\right|_{3}=R^{A}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}+R^{A}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}+R^{A}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}\,,\end{split}

(47)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{A}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}&=\frac{X|w-1|^{2}(z-1)|z|^{2}}{(X+1)(w+Xz)(\bar{w}-\bar{z})(1+Xz)}\,,\\
R^{A}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}&=\frac{X^{3}(\bar{w}-1)(\bar{z}-1)z^{4}\bar{z}}{(1+X|z|^{2})(1+Xz)(\bar{w}+X|z|^{2})}\,,\\
R^{A}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}&=-\frac{Xz(w-1)\bar{w}^{4}|z-1|^{2}}{(\bar{w}+X\bar{z})(|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2})(\bar{w}+X|z|^{2})(\bar{w}-\bar{z})}\,.\end{split}

(48)

The notation
R^{A}_{\bar{x}\bar{y}}
indicates these rational terms have unphysical singularities at
\bar{P}_{x}^{A}=0
and
\bar{P}_{y}^{A}=0
, where as usual, we use bar to denote complex conjugation.
Of course, these six rational terms are not independent due to the relation,

R^{A}_{uv}+R^{A}_{vw}+R^{A}_{wu}=R^{A}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}+R^{A}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}+R^{A}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}.

(49)

These rational terms, when divided by the appropriate MHV central-emission vertex, correspond to the NMRK limit of the dual-conformal-invariant
R
-invariants of
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM
Drummond:2008vq
.
In this paper however, we do not normalise by the MHV vertex as this will avoid the introduction of the MHV phase, and will lead to simpler expressions when we take further kinematic limits of these rational terms.

Having studied the physical and spurious poles of the BCFW representation of the opposite-helicity CEV, we briefly return to the CSW representation of the vertex, eq. (
220
).
We write this in terms of the minimal variables in eq. (
221
). We can now see that this representation has the expected physical singularities of eqs. (
37
) and (
38
), and also spurious poles at
P_{u}^{A}=0
and
\bar{P}_{v}^{A}=0
.
In addition to these representations, one can find a compact representation of the central-emission vertex, eq. (
222
), which only has a single spurious pole at
P_{u}^{A}=0
.
We have checked analytically that the five representations of the opposite-helicity vertex in
eqs. (
36
), (
45
), (
47
), (
221
) and (
222
) are all equivalent.
The equivalence of these expressions shows that
P_{x}^{A}=0
are not true singularities of the central-emission vertex, and we say that they are spurious, or removable poles of this expression.

3.3
Other colour orderings

Figure 5:
The colour connections of the three representative colour orderings we discuss in this paper, displayed on a two-sided rapidity plot: (a)
\sigma_{A}
; (b)
\sigma_{A^{\prime}}
; (c)
\sigma_{B}
, which uses ’t Hooft graphical notation, with untwisted colour lines. Modulo cyclic permutations of the colour ordering, the gluons which fall between incoming gluons 2 and 1 are placed on the “front” side of the plot, while the gluons which fall between 1 and 2 are placed on the “back” side.
In MRK, the gluons are ordered in rapidity both on the front and on the back sides, however the relative ordering between the two sides is immaterial
DelDuca:1993pp
;
DelDuca:1995zy
. On the front side, colour and rapidity have the same ordering; on the back, the opposite ordering. In NMRK, the strict orderings may be relaxed on either side as appropriate
DelDuca:1995ki
.

So far we have only investigated a single colour ordering, which we termed
\sigma_{A}
. All colour orderings were studied in NMRK at tree-level in ref.
DelDuca:1995ki
. There the leading behaviour was found to be given by colour orderings that have
p_{1}
adjacent to
p_{6}
and
p_{2}
adjacent to
p_{3}
. An interchange of
p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{6}
or
p_{2}\leftrightarrow p_{3}
leads only to a change in the sign of the amplitude. In addition to
\sigma_{A}
we should therefore consider the representative orderings
\sigma_{A^{\prime}}=\{1,2,3,5,4,6\}
and
\sigma_{B}=\{1,4,2,3,5,6\}
, which are displayed in fig.
5
using the two-sided rapidity plot
DelDuca:1993pp
;
DelDuca:1995zy
6
6

6

An earlier version of the two-sided rapidity plot, for which though colour was absent, was used in multiperipheral and dual models
Weis:1971yxx
;
Veneziano:1973nw
.
. The ordering
\sigma_{A^{\prime}}
, is related to the former by a simple interchange of
p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}
, which we may directly apply to the functions in eq. (
28
). In terms of the minimal variables this operation is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}z&\xrightarrow[p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}]{}1+w\frac{1-z}{z},\\
w&\xrightarrow[p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}]{}1+z\frac{1-w}{w},\\
X&\xrightarrow[p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}]{}\frac{1}{X}.\end{split}

(50)

Throughout this paper we will use a prime to refer to an interchange of
p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}
, for example

q_{2}\equiv q_{1}-p_{4}=q_{3}+p_{5},\qquad\text{so}\qquad q_{2}^{\prime}\equiv q_{1}-p_{5}=q_{3}+p_{4},

(51)

which is the relevant
t
-channel momentum for the
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limit. For example, the equal-helicity tree-level CEV in the minimal variables is given by

A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})=\frac{q_{1\perp}^{*}}{q_{1\perp}}\frac{w(w-1)(z-1)}{(w+Xz)}\,.

(52)

For completeness, we list the full analysis of the BCFW representations of the opposite-helicity vertex for the ordering
\sigma_{A^{\prime}}
in appendix
E.1
.

In the central NMRK limit, the colour-ordered amplitude of the remaining ordering
\sigma_{B}
similarly factorises as

M^{(0)}_{6g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p^{\nu_{2}}_{2},p^{\nu_{3}}_{3},p_{5}^{\nu_{5}},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}})=s\,C^{g(0)}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}})\frac{1}{t_{1}}B^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})\frac{1}{t_{3}}C^{g(0)}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}})\,,

(53)

However, the tree-level CEV for the ordering
\sigma_{B}
is not independent to that of the orderings
\sigma_{A}
and
\sigma_{A^{\prime}}
due to the relationship
DelDuca:1995ki
,

\displaystyle A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})+A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},q_{3})+B^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})=0\,.

(54)

This relation follows
DelDuca:1999iql
from the U(1) decoupling equation
Berends:1988zn
;
Bern:1990ux
, or as the leading contribution in NMRK to a Kleiss-Kuijf relation
Kleiss:1988ne

which relates colour-ordered amplitudes at tree level
DelDuca:1999rs
.
Eq. (
54
) is simple to verify in the equal-helicity case using eqs. (
35
) and (
52
), and

B^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3})=\frac{q_{1\perp}^{*}q_{3\perp}}{p_{4\perp}p_{5\perp}}=-\frac{q_{1\perp}^{*}}{q_{1\perp}}(w-1)(z-1).

(55)

To demonstrate the relationship given in eq. (
54
) for the opposity-helicity case, it will be easier to first obtain a representation of
B^{gg(0)}
in the minimal variables.
At tree level, there are therefore only two independent colour orderings in NMRK, as in eq. (
25
). However, in the one-loop MHV amplitudes of
{\cal N}=4
SYM, the tree-level amplitudes for
\sigma_{A}
,
\sigma_{A^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{B}
will all be multiplied by distinct transcendental functions, so these colour orderings are independent beyond tree level. Furthermore, in the one-loop NMHV amplitudes for
{\cal N}=4
SYM, we encounter rational coefficients of transcendental functions which are not simply tree-level amplitudes. However, as mentioned in the outset of section
3
, these rational coefficients consist of the individual terms in the BCFW representations of the tree amplitudes. For this reason, we investigate the BCFW-based representation of the tree-level amplitudes for the
\sigma_{B}
ordering in this section, with an emphasis on the individual rational terms.

We now perform a study analogous to section
3.2
for the ordering
\sigma_{B}
. We first list the cross ratios for this colour ordering,

\displaystyle u_{B}

\displaystyle\,\,=\,\,

\displaystyle\frac{s_{14}s_{35}}{s_{142}s_{235}}

\displaystyle\,\,=\,\,

\displaystyle\frac{p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-}}{|q^{\prime}_{2\perp}|^{2}+p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-}}

\displaystyle\,\,=\,\,

\displaystyle\frac{X|z|^{2}}{|w+z-wz|^{2}+X|z|^{2}}

\displaystyle v_{B}

\displaystyle\,\,=\,\,

\displaystyle\frac{s_{42}s_{56}}{s_{423}s_{142}}

\displaystyle\,\,=\,\,

\displaystyle\frac{p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+}}{|q_{2\perp}|^{2}+p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+}}

\displaystyle\,\,=\,\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+X|z|^{2}}

(56)

\displaystyle w_{B}

\displaystyle\,\,=\,\,

\displaystyle\frac{s_{23}s_{61}}{s_{235}s_{423}}

\displaystyle\,\,=\,\,

\displaystyle\frac{(-t_{1})(-t_{3})}{(|q_{2\perp}|^{2}+p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+})(|q^{\prime}_{2\perp}|^{2}+p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-})}

\displaystyle\,\,=\,\,

\displaystyle\frac{|w-1|^{2}|z-1|^{2}X|z|^{2}}{(1+X|z|^{2})(|w+z-wz|^{2}+X|z|^{2})}

which, like eq. (
42
), lie in the range
[0,1]
for our physical kinematic region.
Returning to our example amplitude, the NMRK limit of
M^{(0)}_{\text{(\ref {eq:BCFWexample})}}(p_{1}^{\oplus},p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{2}^{\ominus},p_{3}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus},p_{6}^{\ominus})
yields the representation,

\displaystyle\begin{split}B^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})=R^{B}_{uv}+R^{B}_{vw}+R^{B}_{wu}\,,\end{split}

(57)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{B}_{uv}&=\frac{z|w-1|^{2}|z-1|^{2}}{\bar{z}(w+z-wz)}\,,\\
R^{B}_{vw}&=-\frac{z(w-1)(z-1)}{\bar{z}(1+X|z|^{2})(w+z-wz+X|z|^{2})}\,,\\
R^{B}_{wu}&=-\frac{(\bar{w}-1)X^{2}z^{3}(\bar{z}-1)\bar{z}}{(w+z-wz)(w+z-wz+X|z|^{2})(|w+z-wz|^{2}+X|z|^{2})}\,,\end{split}

(58)

while the NMRK limit of
M^{(0)}_{{\text{(\ref {eq:nmhvmmmppp})}}}(p_{1}^{\ominus},p_{4}^{\ominus},p_{2}^{\ominus},p_{3}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\oplus},p_{6}^{\oplus})
yields the representation,

\displaystyle\begin{split}B^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})=R^{B}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}+R^{B}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}+R^{B}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}\,,\end{split}

(59)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{B}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}&=0\,,\\
R^{B}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}&=-\frac{(\bar{w}-1)(\bar{z}-1)|z|^{2}z^{2}X^{2}}{(1+X|z|^{2})(\bar{w}+\bar{z}-\bar{w}\bar{z}+X|z|^{2})}\,,\\
R^{B}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}&=-\frac{z(w-1)(z-1)(\bar{w}+\bar{z}-\bar{w}\bar{z})^{3}}{\bar{z}(\bar{w}+\bar{z}-\bar{w}\bar{z}+X|z|^{2})(|w+z-wz|^{2}+X|z|^{2})}\,.\end{split}

(60)

One can now verify the relation eq. (
54
) using the representations in eq. (
57
) or eq. (
59
) along with those obtained in section
3.2
.

Recalling eq. (
188
) and eq. (
196
), the physical-singularity surfaces of this ordering are

\begin{array}[]{rrrrrrr}s_{142}=0&\phantom{m}\leftrightarrow&p_{3}^{+}p_{6}^{-}=0\,,&&\\
s_{234}=0&\leftrightarrow&|q_{2\perp}|^{2}+p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+}=0&\phantom{m}\leftrightarrow&1+X|z|^{2}=0\,,\\
s_{235}=0&\leftrightarrow&|q_{2\perp}^{\prime}|^{2}+p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-}=0&\leftrightarrow&|w+z-wz|^{2}+X|z|^{2}=0\,,\end{array}

(61)

where we note that there is no singularity in the collinear limit, eq. (
37
). In the NMRK limit
s_{142}\sim s_{12}
which does not depend on the central degrees of freedom of the process. We therefore have not listed an entry for this Mandelstam invariant in terms of the minimal variables.
With reference to eqs. (
193
) and (
198
), the unphysical-singularity surfaces are
7
7

7

In the minimal coordinates, the limit
q_{2\perp}^{*}\to 0
is equivalent to taking
\bar{w}
and
\bar{z}
to zero at the same rate. The ratio
\bar{w}/\bar{z}
is well defined in this limit.

\begin{array}[]{rrrrrrr}\langle 1|4+2|3]=0&\,\,\,\leftrightarrow&P^{B}_{v}=0&\,\,\,\leftrightarrow&q_{2\perp}^{*}=0\,,&&\\
\langle 2|3+5|6]=0&\,\,\,\leftrightarrow&P^{B}_{u}=0&\,\,\,\leftrightarrow&q^{\prime}_{2\perp}=0&\,\,\,\leftrightarrow&w+z-wz=0\,,\\
\langle 5|6+1|4]=0&\,\,\,\leftrightarrow&P^{B}_{w}=0&\,\,\,\leftrightarrow&{\displaystyle\frac{q_{2\perp}^{*}}{p_{4\perp}^{*}}}+{\displaystyle\frac{q_{2\perp}^{\prime}}{p_{5\perp}}\frac{p_{5}^{+}}{p_{4}^{+}}}=0&\,\,\,\leftrightarrow&w+z-wz+X|z|^{2}=0\,.\\
\end{array}

(62)

We note that under the transformation in eq. (
50
) one obtains

\displaystyle u_{B}{\underset{p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}}{\longleftrightarrow}}v_{B}\,,\qquad w_{B}{\underset{p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}}{\longleftrightarrow}}w_{B}\,,

(63)

and

\displaystyle R^{B}_{uv}{\underset{p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}}{\longleftrightarrow}}(R^{B}_{uv})^{*},

\displaystyle R^{B}_{vw}{\underset{p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}}{\longleftrightarrow}}\left(R^{B}_{wu}\right)^{*},

\displaystyle R^{B}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}{\underset{p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}}{\longleftrightarrow}}(R^{B}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}})^{*}=0,

\displaystyle R^{B}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}{\underset{p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}}{\longleftrightarrow}}\left(R^{B}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}\right)^{*}.

(64)

These properties will be useful for organising the one-loop
opposite-helicity amplitude in section
4.2
.

3.4
The target-projectile exchange

The amplitude in eq. (
25
) is manifestly symmetric under the target-projectile exchange.
The manifest symmetry is broken on the right-hand side of eq. (
26
) by the choice of impact factors in eq. (
2
), which is dictated by the momentum representation, eq. (
166
),
of the spinor products. However, in transverse-momentum space the impact factors in (
2
) are just phases, and hence the central-emission vertex for the emission of two gluons is invariant under target-projectile exchange up to an overall phase. We may consider then the target-projectile map
F
, which interchanges

\displaystyle p_{1}\underset{F}{\leftrightarrow}p_{2}\,,\qquad p_{3}\underset{F}{\leftrightarrow}p_{6}\,,\qquad p_{4}\underset{F}{\leftrightarrow}p_{5}\,.

(65)

In NMRK the target-projectile map acts as

\displaystyle q_{1\perp}\underset{F}{\leftrightarrow}-q_{3\perp}\,,\qquad p_{4\perp}\underset{F}{\leftrightarrow}p_{5\perp}\,,\qquad p_{4}^{\pm}\underset{F}{\leftrightarrow}p_{5}^{\mp}\,.

(66)

In terms of the minimal set
\{w,z,X\}
these flip relations can be expressed as

\displaystyle w\underset{F}{\leftrightarrow}z\,,\qquad X\underset{F}{\mapsto}\frac{|z|^{2}}{|w|^{2}}X\,.

(67)

Under these flips, the cross ratios for the
\sigma_{A}
ordering, eq. (
42
), change as

u_{A}\underset{F}{\leftrightarrow}u_{A}\,,\qquad v_{A}\underset{F}{\leftrightarrow}w_{A}\,,

(68)

while the spurious poles change as

\displaystyle w+X|z|^{2}\xrightarrow[F]{}z(1+X\bar{z})\,,\qquad w-z\xrightarrow[F]{}-(w-z)\,,\qquad 1+X\bar{z}\xrightarrow[F]{}\frac{w+X|z|^{2}}{w}\,,

(69)

i.e. the first and third spurious-pole surfaces in eq. (
44
) are swapped. Furthermore, the rational coefficients (
46
) change as

\displaystyle R^{A}_{uv}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[F]{}\frac{w\bar{z}}{\bar{w}z}R^{A}_{wu}\,,\qquad R^{A}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}\xrightarrow[F]{}\frac{w\bar{z}}{\bar{w}z}R^{A}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}\,,

\displaystyle R^{A}_{vw}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[F]{}\frac{w\bar{z}}{\bar{w}z}R^{A}_{vw}\,,\qquad R^{A}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}\xrightarrow[F]{}\frac{w\bar{z}}{\bar{w}z}R^{A}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}\,,

(70)

\displaystyle R^{A}_{wu}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[F]{}\frac{w\bar{z}}{\bar{w}z}R^{A}_{uv}\,,\qquad R^{A}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}\xrightarrow[F]{}\frac{w\bar{z}}{\bar{w}z}R^{A}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}\,,

from which we see that both eqs. (
45
) and (
47
) are invariant under the target-projectile map
F
, up to an overall phase.
By contrast, for the
\sigma_{B}
ordering we find that each cross ratio (eq. (
56
)) is invariant under this map,

u_{B}\underset{F}{\leftrightarrow}u_{B}\,,\qquad v_{B}\underset{F}{\leftrightarrow}v_{B}\,,\qquad w_{B}\underset{F}{\leftrightarrow}w_{B}\,,

(71)

the spurious singularity surfaces (
62
) are likewise invariant, while each rational term in eqs. (
57
) and (
59
) is invariant up to an overall phase,

\displaystyle R^{B}_{xy}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[F]{}\frac{w\bar{z}}{\bar{w}z}R^{B}_{xy}\,,\qquad R^{B}_{\bar{x}\bar{y}}\xrightarrow[F]{}\frac{w\bar{z}}{\bar{w}z}R^{B}_{\bar{x}\bar{y}}\,,\qquad x,y\in\{u,v,w\},x\neq y\,.

Having investigated the tree-level CEV, and in particular the rational terms
R_{xy}^{I}
which comprise the BCFW representations of this CEV, we proceed to the study of this vertex at one loop.

4
The two-gluon central-emission vertex at one loop

In this section we will extract the two-gluon central-emission vertex at one loop in
{\cal N}=4
SYM. The plan is analogous to the extraction of the tree-level vertex in section
3
. We will begin with the one-loop amplitude in general kinematics, and find the leading behaviour in the central NMRK limit defined in appendix
A.3
. We expect that in this limit, the amplitude will factorise into the known one-loop impact factors and Regge trajectories associated with the two large rapidity spans, and a new piece which depends only on the central degrees of freedom, i.e. the gluons 4 and 5 and the transverse
t
-channel momenta. We will identify this new central piece with the one-loop CEV.

The study of the two-gluon CEV in
{\cal N}=4
SYM is not only of interest in its own right; via the supersymmetric decomposition of QCD amplitudes at one loop
Bern:1993mq
;
Bern:1994zx
;
Bern:1994cg
, it is also an ingredient for the two-gluon CEV in QCD. As such, we will begin by clarifying the connection between the two.

The colour decomposition we use for the QCD one-loop six-gluon amplitude is
DelDuca:1999rs
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mbox{{\tiny QCD}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{5}/\mathcal{R}}\Big[\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{a_{\sigma_{1}}}\cdots F^{a_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)M^{(1)[\mathbf{8}]}_{\mbox{{\tiny QCD}}}\left(p_{\sigma_{1}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{1}}},\dots,p_{\sigma_{6}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)

(72)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+N_{f}\mbox{tr\,}\left(T^{a_{\sigma_{1}}}\cdots T^{a_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)M^{(1)[\mathbf{3}]}_{\mbox{{\tiny QCD}}}\left(p_{\sigma_{1}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{1}}},\dots,p_{\sigma_{6}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)\Big]\,,

where
S_{5}=S_{6}/\mathbb{Z}_{6}
is the group of non-cyclic permutations and
\mathcal{R}
is the total reflection, e.g.
\mathcal{R}(1,2,3,4,5,6)=(6,5,4,3,2,1)
. The superscript
[\mathbf{R}]
on colour-ordered amplitudes denotes the colour representation of the particle circulating in the loop. We remind the reader that
F^{a_{i}}
and
T^{a_{i}}
are generators in the adjoint and fundamental representations respectively. For convenience we define separate colour-dressed amplitudes for the gluon-loop and quark-loop contributions,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(1)[\mathbf{8}]}_{\mbox{{\tiny QCD}}}

\displaystyle=g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{5}/\mathcal{R}}\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{a_{\sigma_{1}}}\cdots F^{a_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)M^{(1)[\mathbf{8}]}_{\mbox{{\tiny QCD}}}\left(p_{\sigma_{1}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{1}}},\dots,p_{\sigma_{6}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)\,,

(73)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(1)[\mathbf{3}]}_{\mbox{{\tiny QCD}}}

\displaystyle=g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{5}/\mathcal{R}}\mbox{tr\,}\left(T^{a_{\sigma_{1}}}\cdots T^{a_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)M^{(1)[\mathbf{3}]}_{\mbox{{\tiny QCD}}}\left(p_{\sigma_{1}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{1}}},\dots,p_{\sigma_{6}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)\,,

(74)

such that the QCD amplitude of eq. (
72
), with
N_{f}
Dirac fermions in the fundamental representation, is obtained by

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mbox{{\tiny QCD}}}=\mathcal{M}^{(1)[\mathbf{8}]}_{\mbox{{\tiny QCD}}}+N_{f}\mathcal{M}^{(1)[\mathbf{3}]}_{\mbox{{\tiny QCD}}}\,.

(75)

Colour-ordered gluon amplitudes in QCD at one loop may be further decomposed into supersymmetric multiplets circulating in the loop,

\displaystyle M^{(1)[\mathbf{8}]}_{\mbox{{\tiny QCD}}}

\displaystyle=M^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}-4M^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=1\chi}+M^{(1)}_{\mathrm{scalar}}\,,

(76a)

\displaystyle M^{(1)[\mathbf{3}]}_{\mbox{{\tiny QCD}}}

\displaystyle=M^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=1\chi}-M^{(1)}_{\mathrm{scalar}}\,.

(76b)

To bring this supersymmetric decomposition to the level of colour-dressed QCD amplitudes we define the colour-dressed contribution from the
\mathcal{N}=4
multiplet,

\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}=g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{5}/\mathcal{R}}\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{a_{\sigma_{1}}}\cdots F^{a_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)M^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}\left(p_{\sigma_{1}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{1}}},\dots,p_{\sigma_{6}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)\,.

(77)

For the colour-dressed contributions from the
\mathcal{N}=1
multiplet and the scalar circulating in the loop, we separately consider the cases where these are in the adjoint or fundamental representations,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(1)[\mathbf{8}]}_{\mathcal{N}=1\chi}

\displaystyle=g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}\,\sum_{\sigma\in S_{5}/\mathcal{R}}\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{a_{\sigma_{1}}}\cdots F^{a_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)M^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=1\chi}\left(p_{\sigma_{1}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{1}}},\dots,p_{\sigma_{6}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)\,,

(78a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(1)[\mathbf{3}]}_{\mathcal{N}=1\chi}

\displaystyle=g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}\,\sum_{\sigma\in S_{5}/\mathcal{R}}\mbox{tr\,}\left(T^{a_{\sigma_{1}}}\cdots T^{a_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)M^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=1\chi}\left(p_{\sigma_{1}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{1}}},\dots,p_{\sigma_{6}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)\,,

(78b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(1)[\mathbf{8}]}_{\mathrm{scalar}}
