\displaystyle=g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}\,\sum_{\sigma\in S_{5}/\mathcal{R}}\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{a_{\sigma_{1}}}\cdots F^{a_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)M^{(1)}_{\mathrm{scalar}}\left(p_{\sigma_{1}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{1}}},\dots,p_{\sigma_{6}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)\,,

(78c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(1)[\mathbf{3}]}_{\mathrm{scalar}}

\displaystyle=g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}\,\sum_{\sigma\in S_{5}/\mathcal{R}}\mbox{tr\,}\left(T^{a_{\sigma_{1}}}\cdots T^{a_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)M^{(1)}_{\mathrm{scalar}}\left(p_{\sigma_{1}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{1}}},\dots,p_{\sigma_{6}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{6}}}\right)\,,

(78d)

such that we can write the QCD one-loop colour-dressed amplitude as

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mbox{{\tiny QCD}}}=\left(\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}-4\mathcal{M}^{(1)[\mathbf{8}]}_{\mathcal{N}=1\chi}+\mathcal{M}^{(1)[\mathbf{8}]}_{\mathrm{scalar}}\right)+N_{f}\left(\mathcal{M}^{(1)[\mathbf{3}]}_{\mathcal{N}=1\chi}-\mathcal{M}^{(1)[\mathbf{3}]}_{\mathrm{scalar}}\right).

(79)

At one loop, this decomposition holds also at the level of the Regge-factorised expressions. For example, we can write the one-loop coefficient of the impact factor in eq. (
18
) as

\displaystyle c^{g(1)}_{\mbox{{\tiny QCD}}}=\left(c^{g(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}-4c^{g(1)[\mathbf{8}]}_{\mathcal{N}=1\chi}+c^{g(1)[\mathbf{8}]}_{\mathrm{scalar}}\right)+N_{f}\left(c^{g(1)[\mathbf{3}]}_{\mathcal{N}=1\chi}-c^{g(1)[\mathbf{3}]}_{\mathrm{scalar}}\right).

(80)

In this paper we focus on the
{\cal N}=4
component,
\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}
, of the one-loop amplitude.
Let us begin by discussing the relevant helicity configurations. As at tree level, there are two distinct helicity configurations for the one-loop two-gluon central-emission vertex.
In order to derive this CEV we take the central NMRK limit, as defined in eq. (
184
), of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{N}=4}^{(1)}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{-\nu_{2}},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},p_{6}^{-\nu_{1}})
.
Thus, to obtain the same-helicity vertex, i.e.
\nu_{4}=\nu_{5}
, the six-gluon amplitude we begin with must necessarily be an
(anti-)MHV
amplitude. In section
4.1
we take the central NMRK limit of
(anti-)MHV
one-loop six-gluon amplitudes in
{\cal N}=4
SYM to obtain the one-loop two-gluon CEV of this theory.
In turn, in order to extract the one-loop opposite-helicity vertex we must instead take the NMRK limit of a helicity configuration with
\nu_{4}\neq\nu_{5}
, which is necessarily an NMHV amplitude. This study is performed in section
4.2
.

In both cases we can simplify the procedure by recalling some results from section
3
. Although eq. (
77
) consists of a sum of 60 colour orderings, only 12 of these are not power suppressed by the rational terms in the central NMRK limit. This is straightforward for the MHV amplitudes, which are proportional to the tree-level result, up to logarithmically varying functions, as we will see in eq. (
88
). For the NMHV amplitudes, eqs. (
216
)–(
218
),
this is not the case. However, each of the pairs of individual rational terms in these amplitudes are power suppressed in the NMRK limit for colour orderings that do not have both
p_{2}
and
p_{3}
adjacent, and
p_{6}
and
p_{1}
adjacent. This means we need only study the 12 colour orderings that are leading at tree-level also for the one-loop NMHV amplitudes.
It will be useful to consider the representative colour orderings,

\displaystyle\sigma_{A}=\left\{1,2,3,4,5,6\right\},\quad\sigma_{A^{\prime}}=\left\{1,2,3,5,4,6\right\},\quad\sigma_{B}=\left\{1,4,2,3,5,6\right\},

(81)

that we introduced in section
3
.
We further introduce the notation
\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma}
to indicate a kinematic (momentum and spin) interchange of
2\leftrightarrow 3
, and
\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma}
to indicate a kinematic interchange
of
1\leftrightarrow 6
. As an example of this notation,

\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}}}=\left\{6,3,2,4,5,1\right\}.

(82)

The amplitude can thus be written as a decomposition into symmetric (
+
) and antisymmetric (
-
) components, as done in eq. (
19
),

\mathcal{M}^{(1)}=\mathcal{M}^{(1)(+,+)}+\mathcal{M}^{(1)(+,-)}+\mathcal{M}^{(1)(-,+)}+\mathcal{M}^{(1)(-,-)}.

(83)

Owing to Bose symmetry for gluon amplitudes, these kinematic interchanges translate directly into interchanges of the corresponding colour indices, so for example
\mathcal{M}^{(1)(-,-)}
corresponds to
the component of the amplitude in which an antisymmetric colour representation is exchanged in both the
t_{1}
and
t_{3}
channels,
\mathcal{M}^{(1)(+,-)}
corresponds to one in which a symmetric representation is exchanged in the
t_{1}
channel, while an antisymmetric one in the
t_{3}
channel, etc. In the central NMRK limit it will be useful to organise the colour-dressed amplitudes according to this symmetry. For example, when there is an adjoint representation circulating in the loop we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{M}^{(1)(-,-)}&=g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}\,F^{a_{3}a_{2}c_{1}}F^{a_{6}a_{1}c_{3}}\\
\times\frac{1}{4}\bigg\{&\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}}F^{c_{3}}\right)\times\left(M^{(1)}(\sigma_{A})-M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})-M^{(1)}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})+M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}}})\right)\\
+&\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{5}}F^{a_{4}}F^{c_{3}}\right)\times\left(M^{(1)}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})-M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A^{\prime}}})-M^{(1)}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A^{\prime}}})+M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A^{\prime}}}})\right)\\
+&\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{4}}F^{c_{3}}F^{a_{5}}\right)\times\left(M^{(1)}(\sigma_{B})-M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})-M^{(1)}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})+M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}}})\right)\bigg\}\,,\end{split}

(84)

which in particular is valid for one-loop gluon amplitudes in
{\cal N}=4
SYM, eq. (
77
).
The analogous expressions for the other signatures are listed in eqs. (
232
)–(
234
).

Before extracting the same-helicity CEV in section
4.1
and the opposite-helicity CEV in section
4.2
, we list here the known one-loop coefficients of the impact factor and Lipatov vertex in
{\mathcal{N}=4}
SYM, which will be of use in our extraction of both helicity configurations of the two-gluon CEV. The one-loop coefficient, eq. (
18
), of the impact factor in
{\mathcal{N}=4}
SYM is

\displaystyle c^{g(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}(t)=N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{-t}\right)^{\epsilon}\left(-\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\left(\frac{\tau}{-t}\right)+\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}-\frac{\delta_{R}}{6}\right)\,,

(85)

where
\delta_{R}=1
in conventional dimensional regularisation (CDR) and in ’t-Hooft-Veltman (HV) schemes,
while
\delta_{R}=0
in dimensional reduction. The QCD one-loop impact factor, from which eq. (
85
) may be understood through the decomposition of eq. (
80
), was computed for
\delta_{R}=1
in
Fadin:1992zt
;
Fadin:1993wh
;
DelDuca:1998cx
;
Bern:1998sc
, and for
\delta_{R}=0
in
DelDuca:1998cx
;
Bern:1998sc
.
Secondly, the single-gluon CEV to one-loop accuracy, eq. (
23
),
in
{\mathcal{N}=4}
SYM is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}v^{g(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}(t_{1},|p_{4\perp}|^{2},t_{2})=&N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}\Bigg(-\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\,\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|p_{4\perp}|^{2}}\right)^{\epsilon}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\,\left[\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{-t_{1}}\right)^{\epsilon}+\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{-t_{2}}\right)^{\epsilon}\right]\log\left(\frac{\tau}{|p_{4\perp}|^{2}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\log^{2}\left(\frac{t_{1}}{t_{2}}\right)\Bigg)\,.\end{split}

(86)

Note that eq. (
86
) is independent of the helicity of the produced particle, and of the regularisation parameter
\delta_{R}
. The one-loop coefficient was computed in QCD
in
Fadin:1993wh
;
Fadin:1994fj
;
Fadin:1996yv
;
DelDuca:1998cx
;
Bern:1998sc
, from which the
{\mathcal{N}=4}
part, eq. (
86
), may be extracted. We note that since in
{\mathcal{N}=4}
SYM the beta function vanishes, eqs. (
85
) and (
86
) do not need to be renormalised. In this paper we use the dimensional reduction choice with
\delta_{R}=0
,
preserving the uniform transcendentality property of the
{\cal N}=4
SYM amplitude.

4.1
Same-helicity vertex

In this section we take the NMRK limit of the MHV amplitude,

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{N}=4}^{(1)}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{-\nu_{2}},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},p_{6}^{-\nu_{1}}).

(87)

After the CEV for
\nu_{4}=\nu_{5}=\oplus
has been obtained, the CEV for
\nu_{4}=\nu_{5}=\ominus
may be obtained by complex conjugation, just as for the tree-level vertex, eq. (
30
).
The colour-ordered amplitudes for one-loop six-gluon MHV amplitudes in
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM are
Bern:1994zx

\displaystyle M^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}(p_{\sigma_{1}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{1}}},\cdots,p_{\sigma_{6}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{6}}})=\frac{\kappa_{\Gamma}}{(4\pi)^{2}}M^{(0)}(p_{\sigma_{1}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{1}}},\cdots,p_{\sigma_{6}}^{\nu_{\sigma_{6}}})V_{6}(\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{6})\,,

(88)

where the transcendental terms are given by the function,

\displaystyle\begin{split}V_{6}(\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{6})&=\sum_{i=1}^{6}-\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{-t_{i}^{[2]}}\right)^{\epsilon}-\sum_{i=1}^{6}\log\left(\frac{-t_{i}^{[2]}}{-t_{i}^{[3]}}\right)\log\left(\frac{-t_{i+1}^{[2]}}{-t_{i}^{[3]}}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\log^{2}\left(\frac{-t_{i}^{[3]}}{-t_{i+1}^{[3]}}\right)-\sum_{i=1}^{3}{\rm Li_{2}}\left[1-\frac{(-t_{i}^{[2]})(-t_{i+3}^{[2]})}{(-t_{i}^{[3]})(-t_{i-1}^{[3]})}\right]+\pi^{2},\end{split}

(89)

which does not depend on the helicities of the gluons.
Here we have used the notation,

t_{i}^{[r]}=(p_{\sigma_{i}}+\cdots+p_{\sigma_{i+r-1}})^{2}.

(90)

When analytically continuing to the physical region, as discussed in appendix
A.1
, we give all Mandelstam invariants a small positive imaginary part,
t_{i}^{[m]}\to t_{i}^{[m]}+i0
. For logarithms of ratios of invariants, this prescription yields

\log\left(\frac{-t_{i}^{[m]}}{-t_{j}^{[n]}}\right)=\log\left(\left|\frac{t_{i}^{[m]}}{t_{j}^{[n]}}\right|\right)-i\pi\left(\Theta(t_{i}^{[m]})-\Theta(t_{j}^{[n]})\right),

(91)

where the Heaviside function
\Theta(x)
is defined as
\Theta(x)=1
for
x>0
and
\Theta(x)=0
otherwise.
Care must be taken when performing the analytic continuation of the arguments of the dilogarithms in eq. (
89
) to the physical region, as discussed in appendix
F
.

From eq. (
88
) we see that the one-loop amplitudes in
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM in the same-helicity configuration
are given by the tree-level gluon amplitude times the transcendental function
V_{6}
. As a step towards taking the full NMRK limit of eq. (
87
), let us keep only the leading power terms of the rational functions. This means we neglect all but the 12 leading colour orderings in eq. (
87
), and for these 12 leading colour orderings, we take the NMRK limit of the tree-level colour-ordered amplitudes that appear in eq. (
88
). We recall that in the NMRK limit, an interchange of
p_{2}^{\nu_{2}}\leftrightarrow p_{3}^{\nu_{3}}
or
p_{6}^{\nu_{6}}\leftrightarrow p_{1}^{\nu_{1}}
leads only to a sign change of the leading tree-level amplitude. This lets us write, for example, the
(-,-)
component of the colour-dressed amplitude in the NMRK limit as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{M}^{(1)(-,-)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}&(p_{4}^{\nu},p_{5}^{\nu})\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}\frac{\kappa_{\Gamma}}{(4\pi)^{2}}\,F^{a_{3}a_{2}c_{1}}F^{a_{6}a_{1}c_{3}}\\
\times\frac{1}{4}\bigg\{&\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}}F^{c_{3}}\right)\left.M^{(0)}(\sigma_{A})\right|_{\mathrm{NMRK}}\left(V_{6}(\sigma_{A})+V_{6}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})+V_{6}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})+V_{6}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}}})\right)\\
+&\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{5}}F^{a_{4}}F^{c_{3}}\right)\left.M^{(0)}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})\right|_{\mathrm{NMRK}}\left(V_{6}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})+V_{6}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A^{\prime}}})+V_{6}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A^{\prime}}})+V_{6}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A^{\prime}}}})\right)\\
+&\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{4}}F^{c_{3}}F^{a_{5}}\right)\left.M^{(0)}(\sigma_{B})\right|_{\mathrm{NMRK}}\left(V_{6}(\sigma_{B})+V_{6}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})+V_{6}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})+V_{6}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}}})\right)\bigg\},\end{split}

(92)

where we factorised the tree amplitude for each colour ordering. The positive relative signs in this equation, in contrast to eq. (
84
), are due to the sign flips in the tree amplitude
M^{(0)}(\sigma)
.
Here, and in the following, we suppress the momentum and helicity arguments of
{\cal M}_{{\cal N}=4}^{(1)}
, apart from the two central gluons 4 and 5.
Next, we write the tree-level colour-ordered amplitudes in the central NMRK in a factorized form according to eq. (
26
), e.g. for the
A
colour ordering we use

\left.M^{(0)}(\sigma_{A})\right|_{\mathrm{NMRK}}=C^{g(0)}\left(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{-\nu_{2}}\right)\frac{1}{t_{1}}A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3})\frac{1}{t_{3}}C^{g(0)}\left(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{6}^{-\nu_{1}}\right)

(93)

and similarly for the
A^{\prime}
and
B
orderings.

Turning our attention to the transcendental functions, we
observe a major simplification in the central NMRK limit upon considering
the dispersive part of the amplitude
, defined by taking the real part of the transcendental functions.
We find that the latter
is given solely by the
(-,-)
component, eq. (
92
), as the dispersive parts of the remaining components of eq. (
83
), quoted in eqs. (
232
)–(
234
), vanish.
This simplification is due to the fact that, for the 12 leading configurations (shown in e.g. eq. (
92
)), the real part of the transcendental functions
V_{6}
in the central NMRK limit is unchanged by an interchange of
p_{2}\leftrightarrow p_{3}
or
p_{6}\leftrightarrow p_{1}
. In conclusion, the dispersive part of the colour-dressed amplitude in the central NMRK limit is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathrm{Disp}\left[{\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}(p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\oplus})}\right]}\,\,\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}\\
&\hskip 30.0pt\,g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}\,\frac{\kappa_{\Gamma}}{(4\pi)^{2}}s_{12}F^{a_{3}a_{2}c_{1}}C^{g(0)}\left(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{-\nu_{2}}\right)\frac{1}{t_{1}}\frac{1}{t_{3}}F^{a_{6}a_{1}c_{3}}C^{g(0)}\left(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{6}^{-\nu_{1}}\right)\,\,\times\\
&\hskip 30.0pt\bigg\{\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}}F^{c_{3}}\right)A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3}){\mathrm{Re}\left[{\left.V_{6}(\sigma_{A})\right|_{\rm NMRK}}\right]}\\
&\hskip 30.0pt\hphantom{\Big\{}+\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{5}}F^{a_{4}}F^{c_{3}}\right)A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3}){\mathrm{Re}\left[{\left.V_{6}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})\right|_{\rm NMRK}}\right]}\\
&\hskip 30.0pt\hphantom{\Big\{}+\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{5}}F^{c_{3}}F^{a_{4}}\right)B^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3}){\mathrm{Re}\left[{\left.V_{6}(\sigma_{B})\right|_{\rm NMRK}}\right]}\bigg\}.\end{split}

(94)

By contrast, the absorptive part of the amplitude, defined by taking the imaginary part of the transcendental functions, in NMRK receives contribution from all terms in the decomposition of eq. (
83
). The absorptive part of the amplitude is discussed further in appendix
F
, and the four signature components are listed in eqs. (
239
)–(
242
). In section
4.3
we show that this absorptive part does not contribute to the colour-summed NLO
gg\to gggg
squared matrix element in the central NMRK limit. This means that, in particular, only the dispersive part of the one-loop amplitude contributes to the dijet cross section at NNLL accuracy. We now move to the study of the real part of
V_{6}
for the relevant colour orderings, leaving the analogous discussion of the imaginary parts of
V_{6}
to appendix
F
.

As noted in ref.
Bartels:2008ce
, the real part of
V_{6}
can be written,
without any approximation
, as a combination of one-loop functions that describe amplitudes in MRK – the one-loop Regge trajectory of eq. (
5
), the impact factor of eq. (
85
) and the Lipatov vertex, eq. (
86
) – plus a remainder term that vanishes in the MRK limit. We will refer to this term as the NMRK remainder function (
\Delta V
below). The arguments of the one-loop MRK functions can be chosen such that the remainder function is free from infrared (IR) divergences, and is a function of conformally-invariant cross ratios alone. For future convenience we choose to group these one-loop MRK functions into two components. One component is
a function which collects the ‘central’ behaviour that will be relevant for our central NMRK limit,

U(\tilde{t}_{1},\tilde{\eta}_{12},\tilde{t}_{2},\tilde{s}_{2},\tilde{\eta}_{23},\tilde{t}_{3})=v^{g(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}\left(\tilde{t}_{1},\tilde{\eta}_{12},\tilde{t}_{2}\right)+\alpha^{(1)}(\tilde{t}_{2})\log\left(\frac{\tilde{s}_{2}}{\tau}\right)+v^{g(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}\left(\tilde{t}_{2},\tilde{\eta}_{23},\tilde{t}_{3}\right)\,.

(95)

The other component collects the remaining ‘non-central’ behaviour,

E(\tilde{t}_{1},\tilde{s}_{1};\tilde{t}_{3},\tilde{s}_{3})=c^{g(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}(\tilde{t}_{1};\tau)+\alpha^{(1)}(\tilde{t}_{1})\log\left(\frac{\tilde{s}_{1}}{\tau}\right)+\alpha^{(1)}(\tilde{t}_{3})\log\left(\frac{\tilde{s}_{3}}{\tau}\right)+c^{g(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}(\tilde{t}_{3};\tau)\,.

(96)

The arguments decorated with tildes in eq. (
95
) and eq. (
96
) are placeholders: the arguments assigned will differ for each colour ordering in eq. (
94
). However, we will always make the natural choice of assigning
\tilde{t}_{1}=t_{1}=s_{23}
and
\tilde{t}_{3}=t_{3}=s_{61}
, and we will assign
\tilde{t}_{2}=t_{2}=s_{234}
when considering the MRK limit, and
\tilde{t}_{2}=t^{\prime}_{2}=s_{235}
when considering the
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limit. Based on the structure of
V_{6}
in eq. (
89
), for a given colour ordering we will also choose the remaining arguments to be constructed from colour-ordered two- and three-particle Mandelstam invariants. This choice will be informed by our knowledge of the MRK and
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limit of this amplitude. However, these criteria do not lead to a unique assignment of the arguments of eqs. (
95
) and (
96
):
the remaining freedom will be discussed separately for each colour ordering.

As a first example, let us see how this re-writing can be performed for the real part of
V_{6}(\sigma_{A})
. We first separate the non-central function
E
, making the natural choice of Mandelstam variables for its arguments,

\begin{split}N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{V_{6}(\sigma_{A})}\right]}&=E(s_{23},s_{34};s_{61},s_{56})+V_{A}\,.\end{split}

(97)

This choice of arguments for
E
is the simplest choice which is compatible with the known MRK limit
8
8

8

As this function does not depend on the central degrees of freedom in the NMRK, no further simplification is obtained by taking the further MRK limit.
,

E(s_{23},s_{34};s_{61},s_{56})\xrightarrow[\mathrm{(N)MRK}]{}E(-|q_{1\perp}|^{2},p_{3}^{+}p_{4}^{-};-|q_{3\perp}|^{2},p_{5}^{+}p_{6}^{-})\,.

(98)

The function
V_{A}
in eq. (
97
) collects the remaining ‘central’ behaviour and it can be identified as the transcendental function associated with the two-gluon CEV for the
A
colour ordering.
As anticipated, this function itself can be neatly written as

\begin{split}V_{A}=U_{A}+\Delta V_{A}(u_{A},v_{A},w_{A})\,,\end{split}

(99)

where, again guided by the MRK limit, we fix
U_{A}
to equal the function
U
, eq. (
95
), with arguments assigned as follows

U_{A}=U\left(s_{23},\frac{s_{34}s_{45}}{s_{345}\sqrt{u_{A}}},s_{234},s_{45},\frac{s_{45}s_{56}}{s_{456}\sqrt{u_{A}}},s_{61}\right)\,.

(100)

This choice of kinematic arguments leads to a simple NMRK remainder function
\Delta V_{A}
, which is IR-finite, vanishes in the MRK limit and depends only on the cross ratios, eq. (
42
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Delta V_{A}(u_{A},v_{A},w_{A})=N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}\bigg(&\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}-\frac{1}{4}\log^{2}(u_{A})-\frac{1}{2}\log(u_{A})\log(v_{A}w_{A})\\
&-{\rm Li_{2}}\left(1-u_{A}\right)-{\rm Li_{2}}\left(1-v_{A}\right)-{\rm Li_{2}}\left(1-w_{A}\right)\bigg)\,.\end{split}

(101)

The MRK limit of the cross ratios is
u_{A}\to 1
,
v_{A}\to 0
,
w_{A}\to 0
(see eq. (
253
)). It is easy to see that eq. (
101
) vanishes in this limit.

As mentioned above, eq. (
100
) is not the unique choice which leads to an IR-finite NMRK remainder. In eq. (
100
) we made the further choice of requiring
U_{A}
to be invariant under target-projectile exchange, eq. (
65
). Yet even this physically-motivated requirement does not uniquely specify arguments for
U_{A}
: a rescaling of the
\tilde{t}_{2}
variable in eq. (
100
) by an arbitrary power of
u_{A}
, i.e.
s_{234}\to s_{234}\times(u_{A})^{p}
for real
p
, yields a NMRK remainder function which is an IR-finite function of cross ratios and is invariant under target-projectile exchange.
Our ultimate choice of arguments in eq. (
100
) was guided by the intuition that the trajectory should be evaluated exactly at the
t
-channel invariant
t_{2}=s_{234}
also away from the strict MRK limit. It would be interesting to explore this same procedure at higher orders in
\epsilon
, or at two loops, where, if such an organisation proves possible, there may be additional constraints on the kinematic arguments.

We stress that eq. (
97
) holds in general kinematics if no approximation is made to the kinematic invariants.
However, we will proceed to make the kinematic approximations valid in the central NMRK limit, in order to exploit the invariance of the real part under the interchanges
p_{2}\leftrightarrow p_{3}
or
p_{6}\leftrightarrow p_{1}
in this limit, as in eq. (
94
). Thus, we specify eq. (
99
) to the
NMRK limit, by making the approximation,

\begin{split}U_{A}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}U\left(-|q_{1\perp}|^{2},\frac{p_{4}^{-}\ s_{45}}{p_{4}^{-}+p_{5}^{-}},-|q_{2\perp}|^{2}-p_{5}^{+}p_{4}^{-},s_{45},\frac{s_{45}\ p_{5}^{+}}{p_{4}^{+}+p_{5}^{+}},-|q_{3\perp}|^{2}\right)\,,\end{split}

(102)

and also taking the NMRK approximation of the cross ratios, as listed in eq. (
42
), for the arguments of eq. (
101
). This NMRK approximation of
V_{A}
will later form one of the building blocks of the colour-dressed two-gluon CEV. The structure of the NMRK limit of the dispersive part of
M^{(1)}_{{\cal N}=4}
is depicted in fig.
6
.

Figure 6:
Schematic illustration of the behaviour of the dispersive part of the one-loop partial amplitude
M^{(1)}_{{\cal N}=4}(\sigma_{A})
in the central NMRK limit. The first diagram on the right-hand side represents the transcendental functions that depend on the central degrees of freedom,
V_{A}
, which we identify as the one-loop correction to the two-gluon CEV for this colour ordering. The remaining four diagrams on the right-hand side are given by the known one-loop impact factors and gluon Regge trajectory contributions, and are collected in the function
E
of eq. (
96
).

The function
V_{6}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})
is simply related to
V_{6}(\sigma_{A})
by an interchange of
p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}
. We now use the known
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limit of the amplitude as our guide, writing

\displaystyle N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{\left.V_{6}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})\right|_{\rm NMRK}}\right]}

\displaystyle=E(s_{23},s_{35};s_{61},s_{46})+V_{A^{\prime}}\,,

(103)

\displaystyle V_{A^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=U_{A^{\prime}}+\Delta V_{A}(u_{A^{\prime}},w_{A^{\prime}},v_{A^{\prime}})\,,

(104)

where
U_{A^{\prime}}
is related to eq. (
100
) by the interchange of
p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}
. As was true for the
A
ordering, in order to use the compact form of eq. (
94
), we will need to make the NMRK approximations to the arguments of
\Delta V_{A}
in eq. (
104
), and similarly we make the approximation,

\begin{split}U_{A^{\prime}}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}U\left(-|q_{1\perp}|^{2},\frac{p_{5}^{-}\ s_{45}}{p_{4}^{-}+p_{5}^{-}},-|q^{\prime}_{2\perp}|^{2}-p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-},s_{45},\frac{s_{45}\ p_{4}^{+}}{p_{4}^{+}+p_{5}^{+}},-|q_{3\perp}|^{2}\right)\,.\end{split}

(105)

Note that the NMHV remainder function for the
A^{\prime}
ordering is the same as for the
A
ordering, because
\Delta V_{A}(u,w,v)=\Delta V_{A}(u,v,w)
based on eq. (
101
).

We now turn to study of the real part of
V_{6}(\sigma_{B})
. The rational coefficient of this transcendental function,
M^{(0)}(\sigma_{B})
, has a leading contribution in both the MRK and
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limits. It will be useful therefore to re-write this transcendental function in two ways: one guided by the known MRK limit of the amplitude, and the other by the known
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limit. As usual, we first separate the central and non-central functions,

\displaystyle N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{V_{6}(\sigma_{B})}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E(s_{23},-s_{42};s_{61},s_{56})+V_{B}=E(s_{23},s_{35};s_{61},-s_{14})+V_{B^{\prime}}\,.

(106)

The forms of the central functions
V_{B}
and
V_{B^{\prime}}
are guided, respectively, by the known MRK and
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limits of this function – note that both refer to the same colour ordering.
These central functions are given by

V_{B}=U_{B}+\Delta V_{B}(u_{B},v_{B},w_{B}),\qquad\qquad V_{B^{\prime}}=U_{B^{\prime}}+\Delta V_{B}(v_{B},u_{B},w_{B})\,,

(107)

where we have chosen to use the kinematic arguments,

\displaystyle\begin{split}U_{B}&=U\left(s_{23},\frac{s_{14}s_{42}}{s_{142}u_{B}},s_{234},-s_{235},\frac{s_{35}s_{56}}{s_{356}u_{B}},s_{61}\right)\,,\\
U_{B^{\prime}}&=U\left(s_{23},\frac{s_{35}s_{56}}{s_{356}v_{B}},s_{235},-s_{234},\frac{s_{14}s_{42}}{s_{142}v_{B}},s_{61}\right)\,,\end{split}

(108)

for the one-loop MRK functions.

Unlike the
A
colour ordering, target-projectile symmetry does not constrain the arguments of eq. (
108
): from eq. (
71
) we see that in NMRK, each cross ratio is invariant under target-projectile exchange.
Our choice of kinematic arguments in eq. (
108
) were instead motivated by the requirement that
U_{B}
and
U_{B^{\prime}}
are related by the map which interchanges
\{p_{2}\leftrightarrow p_{3},\ p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5},\ p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{6}\}
, corresponding to a symmetry of the real part of
V_{6}(\sigma_{B})
.
We will refer to this map as
G
. For the natural assignment of variables in eq. (
106
), the real parts of
E
are related by this map.
9
9

9

Note that we always choose the signs of the arguments of
E
such that it is in fact a real function.

Choosing
U_{B}
and
U_{B^{\prime}}
to be related by
G
therefore means the respective NMRK remainder functions are also related by this map. For the cross ratios, eq. (
56
), the map
G
leads to
u_{B}\,\,{\underset{G}{\leftrightarrow}}\,\,v_{B}
, while
w_{B}
is invariant under
G
. The NMRK remainder function that follows from the choices made in eq. (
108
) is

\displaystyle\Delta V_{B}(u_{B},v_{B},w_{B})=\Delta V_{B}(v_{B},u_{B},w_{B})

(109)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}\bigg(\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}-\log(u_{B})\log(v_{B})-{\rm Li_{2}}\left(1-u_{B}\right)-{\rm Li_{2}}\left(1-v_{B}\right)-{\rm Li_{2}}\left(1-w_{B}\right)\bigg)\,,

which is indeed invariant under
G
. We note, however, that by considering suitable rescaling of the arguments of
U_{B}
and
U_{B^{\prime}}
by powers of
u_{B}
or
v_{B}
respectively (the quantities which tend to unity in the MRK or
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limits respectively), there is one alternative set of kinematic assignments which lead to an NMRK remainder function that possesses the same desirable properties as eq. (
109
): as for the
\sigma_{A}
ordering, our final choice of arguments in eq. (
108
) was determined by requiring that
\tilde{t}_{2}
is identified with the relevant
t
-channel momentum even in general kinematics, namely
t_{2}
and
t_{2}^{\prime}
in the MRK and
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limits, respectively.

In order to use eq. (
106
) within the compact form of eq. (
94
), we must make the NMRK approximations

\displaystyle U_{B}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}

\displaystyle U\bigg(-|q_{1\perp}|^{2},-\frac{p_{4}^{-}}{p_{5}^{-}}t_{2}^{\prime},t_{2},-t_{2}^{\prime},-\frac{p_{5}^{+}}{p_{4}^{+}}t_{2}^{\prime},-|q_{3\perp}|^{2}\bigg)\,,

\displaystyle U_{B^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}

\displaystyle U\bigg(-|q_{1\perp}|^{2},-\frac{p_{5}^{-}}{p_{4}^{-}}t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime},-t_{2},-\frac{p_{4}^{+}}{p_{5}^{+}}t_{2},-|q_{3\perp}|^{2}\bigg)\,,

(110)

where it is understood that on the right hand side we use the NMRK limits of

t_{2}=s_{234}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}-\left(|q_{2\perp}|^{2}+p_{5}^{+}p_{4}^{-}\right),\,\qquad t_{2}^{\prime}=s_{235}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}-\left(|q^{\prime}_{2\perp}|^{2}+p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-}\right)\,.

(111)

Similarly, we apply the NMRK approximations listed in eq. (
56
) for the cross-ratio arguments of eq. (
109
). We see that in the NMRK limit,
U_{B}
and
U_{B^{\prime}}
(and therefore
V_{B}
and
V_{B^{\prime}}
) are simply related by an interchange of
p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}
.

Having studied the three colour orderings of eq. (
94
), we will now move to a basis of colour structures that more closely resembles the colour structure of the tree-level amplitude, eq. (
25
). This basis will let us identify one-loop corrections associated with each of the tree-level colour structures, as well as highlight the new colour structure that starts contributing at one-loop level.
Denoting the three traces by

\displaystyle T_{A}=\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}}F^{c_{3}}\right),\qquad T_{A^{\prime}}=\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{5}}F^{a_{4}}F^{c_{3}}\right),\qquad T_{B}=\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{5}}F^{c_{3}}F^{a_{4}}\right),

(112)

we consider the following rotation of the sum inside the curly brackets in eq. (
94
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}T_{A}M_{A}+T_{A^{\prime}}M_{A^{\prime}}+T_{B}M_{B}&=(T_{A}-T_{B})\times\frac{1}{3}(2M_{A}-M_{A^{\prime}}-M_{B})\\
&+(T_{A^{\prime}}-T_{B})\times\frac{1}{3}(2M_{A^{\prime}}-M_{A}-M_{B})\\
&+\frac{1}{3}(T_{A}+T_{A^{\prime}}+T_{B})\times(M_{A}+M_{A^{\prime}}+M_{B})\,.\end{split}

(113)

Using the identities,

\displaystyle\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{a}F^{b}F^{c}\right)=N_{c}F^{abc}\,,\qquad\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{a}F^{b}\left[F^{c},F^{d}\right]\right)=N_{c}F^{abe}F^{ecd}\,,

(114)

we relate the first two combinations of colour structure on the right-hand side of eq. (
113
) to the tree-level structures
(F^{a_{\sigma_{4}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{5}}})_{c_{1}c_{3}}
of eq. (
25
), and define the third colour factor in terms of the fully-symmetric trace:

\displaystyle d^{a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}a_{4}}_{A}=\frac{1}{4!}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{4}}\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{a_{\sigma_{1}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{2}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{3}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{4}}}\right)\,.

(115)

Making use of the additional relations that exist within NMRK, namely

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.E(t_{1},-s_{42};t_{3},s_{56})\right|_{{\mathrm{NMRK}}}&=\left.E(t_{1},s_{34};t_{3},s_{56})\right|_{{\mathrm{NMRK}}}\,,\\
\left.E(t_{1},s_{35};t_{3},-s_{14})\right|_{{\mathrm{NMRK}}}&=\left.E(t_{1},s_{35};t_{3},s_{46})\right|_{{\mathrm{NMRK}}}\,,\end{split}

(116)

we now express eq. (
94
) as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathrm{Disp}\left[\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{{\cal N}=4}(p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\oplus})\right]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}\frac{g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}}{(4\pi)^{2}}\,s_{12}F^{a_{3}a_{2}c_{1}}C^{g(0)}\left(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{-\nu_{2}}\right)\frac{1}{t_{1}}\times\\
&\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2}}\Bigg\{(F^{a_{\sigma_{4}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{5}}})_{c_{1}c_{3}}\left(A^{gg(1)}_{\vDash}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{\sigma_{4}},p^{\oplus}_{\sigma_{5}},q_{3})+A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{\sigma_{4}},p^{\oplus}_{\sigma_{5}},q_{3})E(t_{1},s_{3\sigma_{4}};t_{3},s_{\sigma_{5}6})\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{N_{c}}d_{A}^{c_{1}a_{\sigma_{4}}a_{\sigma_{5}}c_{3}}A^{gg(1)}_{d}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{\sigma_{4}},p^{\oplus}_{\sigma_{5}},q_{3})\Bigg\}\times\frac{1}{t_{3}}F^{a_{6}a_{1}c_{3}}C^{g(0)}\left(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{6}^{-\nu_{1}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(117)

where the totally symmetric colour structure
d_{A}
starts contributing at one-loop level, with

\displaystyle A^{gg(1)}_{d}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3})=A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3})\left(V_{A}-V_{B}\right)\,.

(118)

The symmetry of the
d_{A}
colour structure under the permutation
S_{2}
of eq. (
117
) means that after the sum over the
\sigma_{4},\sigma_{5}
permutations is performed, there are only three independent colour structures in this equation, as in eq. (
94
).

For the kinematic coefficient of the tree-level colour structures we find, using eqs. (
54
) and (
113
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}&A^{gg(1)}_{\vDash}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3})\\
&=\frac{1}{3}\left(A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3})\left(2V_{A}+V_{B}\right)-A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})\left(V_{A^{\prime}}-V_{B^{\prime}}\right)\right)\,.\end{split}

(119)

We interpret this term as the one-loop coefficient of the all-orders expansion of this colour structure,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&A^{gg}_{\vDash}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})=A^{gg(0)}_{\vDash}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})+\frac{\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}}{4\pi}A^{gg(1)}_{\vDash}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})+{\cal O}(\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{2})\,,\end{split}

(120)

with

\displaystyle A^{gg(0)}_{\vDash}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})=A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})\,.

(121)

As the structure
d_{A}
first appears at one-loop level we write

\displaystyle\begin{split}A^{gg}_{d}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})&=\frac{\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}}{4\pi}A^{gg(1)}_{d}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})+{\cal O}(\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{2})\,,\end{split}

(122)

in anticipation of higher-order corrections to this colour structure. We note that
A^{gg(1)}_{d}
and
A^{gg(1)}_{\vDash}
both depend on
\mu^{2}
and
\tau
, but as for the one-loop coefficients of the impact factor and Lipatov vertex, we omit this dependence for brevity.

Figure 7:
Schematic illustration of the further factorisation of the one-loop two-gluon CEV for the ordering
\sigma_{A}
in the MRK limit.

The notation introduced in this section makes it straightforward to verify that the eqs. (
119
) and (
118
) yield the expected MRK limit. We first note that by construction, eqs. (
99
) and (
107
) both tend to the same MRK limit,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.U_{A}\right|_{\mathrm{MRK}}=\left.U_{B}\right|_{\mathrm{MRK}}=U_{\mathrm{MRK}}=&U(-|q_{1\perp}|^{2},|p_{4\perp}|^{2},-|q_{2\perp}|^{2},p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-},|p_{5\perp}|^{2},-|q_{3\perp}|^{2}).\end{split}

(123)

Using the MRK limits of the relevant cross ratios listed in eq. (
253
) together with the values of the dilogarithm,

{\rm Li_{2}}\left(0\right)=0,\qquad{\rm Li_{2}}\left(1\right)=\frac{\pi^{2}}{6},

(124)

we see that both
\Delta V_{A}(u_{A},v_{A},w_{A})
and
\Delta V_{B}(u_{B},v_{B},w_{B})
vanish in the MRK limit as desired. This leads to the simple results,

\displaystyle A^{gg(1)}_{\vDash}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p_{4}^{\oplus},q_{2})\frac{1}{t_{2}}V^{g(0)}(q_{2},p_{5}^{\oplus},q_{3})\,U_{\mathrm{MRK}}\,,

(125)

\displaystyle A^{gg(1)}_{d}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}0\,.

(126)

Equation (
125
) is depicted schematically in fig.
7
. The function
U_{\mathrm{MRK}}
generates all three of the loop corrections on the right-hand side of the figure, thanks to the definition (
95
).
A similar analysis shows that

\displaystyle A^{gg(1)}_{\vDash}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p_{5}^{\oplus},q_{2}^{\prime})\frac{1}{t_{2}^{\prime}}V^{g(0)}(q_{2}^{\prime},p_{4}^{\oplus},q_{3})U_{{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}}\,,

(127)

\displaystyle A^{gg(1)}_{d}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}0\,,

(128)

where

\begin{split}\left.U_{A^{\prime}}\right|_{{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}}=\left.U_{B^{\prime}}\right|_{{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}}=U_{\mathrm{MRK^{\prime}}}=U(-|q_{1\perp}|^{2},|p_{5\perp}|^{2},-|q^{\prime}_{2\perp}|^{2},p_{5}^{+}p_{4}^{-},|p_{4\perp}|^{2},-|q_{3\perp}|^{2})\,.\end{split}

(129)

In appendix
G
we investigate the soft and collinear limits of eq. (
117
).
In section
5
we conjecture how the one-loop results of this section may be extended to all orders. In the next subsection we obtain expressions analogous to eqs. (
119
) and (
118
)
but for the case where gluons 4 and 5 have opposite helicities.

4.2
Opposite-helicity vertex

We now perform an analogous study to section
4.1
for the opposite-helicity case, where we must start from an NMHV helicity configuration.
We present the procedure for a configuration of the form
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{N}=4}^{(1)}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{-\nu_{2}},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},p_{6}^{-\nu_{1}})
. As for the tree-level vertex, after the CEV for
\nu_{4}=-\nu_{5}=\oplus
has been obtained, the CEV for
\nu_{4}=-\nu_{5}=\ominus
may be obtained by complex conjugation.
The colour-ordered one-loop six-gluon NMHV amplitudes in
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM are given in eqs. (
216
)–(
218
). Unlike eq. (
88
), these NMHV amplitudes are not proportional to the tree-level amplitude. Instead, the transcendental functions of the NMHV amplitude,
W_{i}
are multiplied by pairs of the rational functions that appear in the BCFW representations of the tree-level amplitude listed in appendix
D.1
. In the NMRK, after dividing by the appropriate impact factors and
t
-channel propagators, these rational coefficients are simply given by sums of pairs of the rational functions which we have studied in sections
3.2
and
3.3
,

S_{xy}^{I}=R_{xy}^{I}+R_{\bar{x}\bar{y}}^{I}\qquad x,y\in\{u,v,w\},\quad I\in\{A,A^{\prime},B\}.

(130)

Like the tree-level amplitudes, these rational coefficients of the transcendental functions
W_{i}
change only by a sign under exchange of
p_{2}^{\nu_{2}}\leftrightarrow p_{3}^{\nu_{3}}
or
p_{6}^{\nu_{6}}\leftrightarrow p_{1}^{\nu_{1}}
. For the 12 leading colour orderings, we further find that an exchange of
p_{2}\leftrightarrow p_{3}
or
p_{6}\leftrightarrow p_{1}
does not alter the real part of the functions
W_{i}
. These properties collectively allow the dispersive part of the amplitude to be written as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathrm{Disp}\left[{\cal M}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}(p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus})\right]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}\\
&\hskip 10.0ptg_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}\frac{\kappa_{\Gamma}}{(4\pi)^{2}}\,s_{12}\,F^{a_{3}a_{2}c_{1}}\,C^{g(0)}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{-\nu_{2}})\,\frac{1}{t_{1}}\,\frac{1}{t_{3}}\,F^{a_{6}a_{1}c_{3}}C^{g(0)}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{6}^{-\nu_{1}})\,\,\\
&\hskip 10.0pt\times\bigg\{\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}}F^{c_{3}}\right)\Big[S^{A}_{uv}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{1}(\sigma_{A})}\right]}+S^{A}_{vw}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{2}(\sigma_{A})}\right]}+S^{A}_{wu}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{3}(\sigma_{A})}\right]}\Big]\\
&\hskip 10.0pt\quad+\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{5}}F^{a_{4}}F^{c_{3}}\right)\Big[S^{A^{\prime}}_{uv}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{1}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})}\right]}+S^{A^{\prime}}_{vw}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{2}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})}\right]}+S^{A^{\prime}}_{wu}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{3}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})}\right]}\Big]\\
&\hskip 10.0pt\quad+\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{5}}F^{c_{3}}F^{a_{4}}\right)\Big[S^{B}_{uv}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{1}(\sigma_{B})}\right]}+S^{B}_{vw}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{2}(\sigma_{B})}\right]}+S^{B}_{wu}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{3}(\sigma_{B})}\right]}\Big]\bigg\}\,,\end{split}

(131)

analogously to eq. (
94
). On the right-hand side of eq. (
131
), the transcendental functions are understood to be taken in the NMRK limit, but for brevity we do not indicate this explicitly.
As in section
4.1
, we find that the dispersive part of the amplitude is given solely by
(-,-)
exchange. This is not true for the absorptive part, which is studied in appendix
F
. We will see in section
4.3
that the absorptive part does not contribute to the helicity- and colour-summed NLO squared matrix element for
gg\to gggg
, or to the dijet cross section at NNLL. The absorptive part will only contribute at N
3
LL. For the remainder of this section we focus on the dispersive part of the amplitude, eq. (
131
).

Unlike the tree-level expressions (e.g. eq. (
45
)), the sums of two rational terms, eq. (
130
), do contain genuine unphysical poles. To demonstrate that the dispersive part of the NMRK amplitude, eq. (
131
), is nonetheless free from unphysical singularities it is useful to use the fact that the sum of the three
S_{xy}^{I}
is twice the tree-level CEV to rewrite eq. (
131
) as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathrm{Disp}\left[\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}(p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus})\right]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}\frac{\kappa_{\Gamma}}{(4\pi)^{2}}\,s_{12}\,F^{a_{3}a_{2}c_{1}}\,C^{g(0)}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{-\nu_{2}})\,\frac{1}{t_{1}}\,\\
&\times\bigg\{\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}}F^{c_{3}}\right)\Big[2A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus},q_{3}){\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{2}(\sigma_{A})}\right]}\\
&\hskip 120.00018pt+S^{A}_{uv}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{1}(\sigma_{A})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A})}\right]}+S^{A}_{wu}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{3}(\sigma_{A})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A})}\right]}\Big]\\
&\,+\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{5}}F^{a_{4}}F^{c_{3}}\right)\Big[2A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p_{5}^{\ominus},p_{4}^{\oplus},q_{3}){\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{2}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})}\right]}\\
&\hskip 120.00018pt+S^{A^{\prime}}_{uv}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{1}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})}\right]}+S^{A^{\prime}}_{wu}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{3}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})}\right]}\Big]\\
&\,+\mbox{tr\,}\left(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{5}}F^{c_{3}}F^{a_{4}}\right)\Big[2B^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus},q_{3}){\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{1}(\sigma_{B})}\right]}\\
&\hskip 120.00018pt+S^{B}_{vw}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{2}(\sigma_{B})-W_{1}(\sigma_{B})}\right]}+S^{B}_{wu}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{3}(\sigma_{B})-W_{1}(\sigma_{B})}\right]}\Big]\bigg\}\\
&\times\frac{1}{t_{3}}\,F^{a_{6}a_{1}c_{3}}C^{g(0)}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{6}^{-\nu_{1}})\,.\end{split}

(132)

This rewriting simplifies our task for two reasons:

•

From section
3
we know the functions
A^{gg(0)}
and
B^{gg(0)}
are free from unphysical singularities.

•

The difference between two cyclic permutations of
W
is much simpler than a given permutation of
W
.

To expand on the second point, the difference between two cyclic permutations of
W
can be expressed as a single product of logarithms whose arguments are simple functions of the cross ratios. This property holds even in general kinematics, although in the following we implicitly consider the cross ratios to be taken in the NMRK limit.
Let us first consider these terms in the
\sigma_{A}
ordering,

\displaystyle\begin{split}W_{1}(\sigma_{A})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A})&=\log\left(v_{A}\right)\log\left(\frac{w_{A}}{u_{A}}\right)\,,\\
W_{3}(\sigma_{A})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A})&=\log\left(w_{A}\right)\log\left(\frac{v_{A}}{u_{A}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(133)

We note in passing that these functions are related by the interchange of
u_{A}\leftrightarrow v_{A}
, which has the physical interpretation of target-projectile exchange, eq. (
68
). This provides our motivation for arranging eq. (
132
) as we have done, where

W_{2}(\sigma_{A})
multiplies the corresponding tree-level CEV, while the remaining terms are related by

\displaystyle\begin{split}S^{A}_{uv}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{1}(\sigma_{A})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A})}\right]}\xrightarrow[F]{}\frac{w\bar{z}}{\bar{w}z}S^{A}_{wu}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{3}(\sigma_{A})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A})}\right]}.\end{split}

(134)

Table
1
lists the behaviour of the cross ratios at each unphysical-singularity surface. From this table we see that at
P^{A}_{v}=0
or
P^{A}_{w}=0
, the transcendental terms which multiply the divergent rational terms in eq. (
132
) vanish. For the remaining unphysical singularity at
P^{A}_{u}=0
, we find

\displaystyle\begin{split}S^{A}_{uv}\left(W_{1}(\sigma_{A})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A})\right)+&S^{A}_{wu}\left(W_{3}(\sigma_{A})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A})\right)\\
&\xrightarrow[P^{A}_{u}\to 0]{}\left(S^{A}_{uv}+S^{A}_{wu}\right)\log^{2}\left(\frac{|z-1|^{2}X}{(1+X)(1+|z|^{2}X)}\right),\end{split}

(135)

which is finite at
P^{A}_{u}=0
, because in the sum
S^{A}_{uv}+S^{A}_{wu}=2A^{gg(0)}-S^{A}_{vw}
, the apparent pole at
P^{A}_{u}=0
is a removable singularity.

P^{A}_{u}=0

P^{A}_{v}=0

P^{A}_{w}=0

u_{A}

1

\frac{|w-1|^{2}X}{(1+X)(1+|w|^{2}X)}

\frac{|z-1|^{2}X}{(1+X)(1+|z|^{2}X)}

v_{A}

\frac{|z-1|^{2}X}{(1+X)(1+|z|^{2}X)}

1

\frac{|z-1|^{2}X}{(1+X)(1+|z|^{2}X)}

w_{A}

\frac{|z-1|^{2}X}{(1+X)(1+|z|^{2}X)}

\frac{|w-1|^{2}X}{(1+X)(1+|w|^{2}X)}

1

Table 1:
Behaviour of the cross ratios in the NMRK limit at the three unphysical-singularity surfaces of the rational terms of the ordering
\sigma_{A}
, listed in eq. (
44
).

The
\sigma_{A^{\prime}}
ordering is exactly analogous. For the
\sigma_{B}
ordering we have singled out in eq. (
132
) the transcendental function
W_{1}(\sigma_{B})
to multiply the tree-level amplitude. The other transcendental functions that appear in this colour structure are then

\displaystyle\begin{split}W_{2}(\sigma_{B})-W_{1}(\sigma_{B})&=\log\left(v_{B}\right)\log\left(\frac{u_{B}}{w_{B}}\right)\,,\\
W_{3}(\sigma_{B})-W_{1}(\sigma_{B})&=\log\left(u_{B}\right)\log\left(\frac{v_{B}}{w_{B}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(136)

which are related to each other by the map
G
introduced in section
4.1
. For the NMHV tree-level rational functions in the NMRK limit, this map is equivalent to the composition of interchanging
p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}
with complex conjugation, i.e. the remaining terms in eq. (
132
) have the property,

\displaystyle S^{B}_{vw}\left(W_{2}(\sigma_{B})-W_{1}(\sigma_{B})\right)\,\,{\underset{p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}}{\longleftrightarrow}}\,\,\left(S^{B}_{wu}\right)^{*}\left(W_{3}(\sigma_{B})-W_{1}(\sigma_{B})\right)\,,

(137)

where we have used eq. (
64
).
Using the properties summarised in table
2
, we see that this ordering is also free from unphysical singularities. At
P_{u}^{B}=0
or
P_{v}^{B}=0
, the rational terms in eq. (
132
) which diverge are multiplied by transcendental terms (eq. (
136
)) that vanish. At
P_{w}^{B}=0
we see that the transcendental terms in eq. (
136
) become equal, allowing us to write

\displaystyle\begin{split}S^{B}_{vw}(W_{2}(\sigma_{B})-W_{1}(\sigma_{B}))+S^{B}_{wu}(W_{3}(\sigma_{B})-W_{1}(\sigma_{B}))\xrightarrow[P^{B}_{w}\to 0]{}(S^{B}_{vw}+S^{B}_{wu})\log^{2}\left(\frac{1}{1+|z|^{2}X}\right).\end{split}

(138)

The sum of rational terms
S^{B}_{vw}+S^{B}_{wu}
is finite at
P_{w}^{B}=0
, so we have shown that the dispersive part of the amplitude eq. (
132
) is free from unphysical singularities, as expected.

P_{u}^{B}=0

P_{v}^{B}=0

P_{w}^{B}=0

u_{B}

1

\frac{1}{1+|1+\frac{w}{z}|^{2}\frac{1}{X}}

\frac{1}{1+|z|^{2}X}

v_{B}

\frac{1}{1+|z|^{2}X}

1

\frac{1}{1+|z|^{2}X}

w_{B}

\frac{1}{1+|z|^{2}X}

\frac{1}{1+|1+\frac{w}{z}|^{2}\frac{1}{X}}

1
