Behaviour of the cross ratios in the NMRK at the three unphysical-singularity surfaces of the rational terms of the ordering
\sigma_{B}
, listed in eq. (
62
).

As in section
4.1
, we can write the real part of the transcendental functions exactly in terms of the one-loop trajectory, impact factors and Lipatov vertex, plus a NMRK remainder term. We first separate the transcendental function into a ‘central’ and ‘non-central’ pieces,

\displaystyle\begin{split}2N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{2}(\sigma_{A})}\right]}=&E(t_{1},s_{34};t_{3},s_{56})+2W_{2}^{A}\,,\end{split}

(139)

with
E
given in eq. (
96
), and we further separate the central function into

2W_{2}^{A}=U_{A}+\Delta W_{A}(u_{A},v_{A},w_{A})\,.

(140)

The function
U_{A}
is given by eq. (
100
). In other words, this is a helicity-independent term. In contrast, for the opposite-helicity case the remainder function is given by

\Delta W_{A}(u_{A},v_{A},w_{A})=N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}\left(-\frac{1}{4}\log^{2}\left(u_{A}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\log(u_{A})\log(v_{A}w_{A})-\log(v_{A})\log(w_{A})\right)\,.

(141)

Note that, as for the same-helicity case, eq. (
101
), the variable assignment in eq. (
100
) leads to a NMRK remainder function which is IR-finite and symmetric under
v_{A}\leftrightarrow w_{A}
. However, we note that unlike eq. (
101
), eq. (
141
) does not vanish in the MRK limit. We will return to this point later. When working in NMRK, in order to exploit the equality of the real parts of the transcendental functions under an exchange of
p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{6}
or
p_{2}\leftrightarrow p_{3}
, we make the approximation eq. (
102
), and we make the NMRK approximation of the cross ratios in eq. (
141
).
For the
A^{\prime}
ordering we similarly write

\displaystyle\begin{split}2N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{2}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})}\right]}&=E(t_{1},s_{35};t_{3},s_{46})+2W_{2}^{A^{\prime}}\,,\\
2W_{2}^{A^{\prime}}&=U_{A^{\prime}}+\Delta W_{A}(u_{A^{\prime}},w_{A^{\prime}},v_{A^{\prime}}).\end{split}

(142)

where
U_{A^{\prime}}
is related to
U_{A}
by exchange of
p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}
.

As in section
4.1
, we can write the transcendental function of the
\sigma_{B}
ordering in terms of the one-loop factorised pieces in two different ways,

\displaystyle\begin{split}2N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{1}(\sigma_{B})}\right]}=E(t_{1},-s_{24};t_{3},s_{56})+2W_{1}^{B}=E(t_{1},s_{35};t_{3},-s_{14})+2W_{1}^{B^{\prime}}\,,\end{split}

(143)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}2W_{1}^{B}&=U_{B}+\Delta W_{B}(u_{B},v_{B},w_{B}),\qquad\qquad 2W_{1}^{B^{\prime}}=U_{B^{\prime}}+\Delta W_{B}(v_{B},u_{B},w_{B})\,.\end{split}

(144)

The helicity-independent functions
U_{B}
and
U_{B^{\prime}}
are given in eq. (
108
), and the NMRK remainder is

\displaystyle\Delta W_{B}(u_{B},v_{B},w_{B})=N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}\left(\log(v_{B})\log\left(\frac{w_{B}}{u_{B}}\right)+\log(u_{B})\log\left(\frac{w_{B}}{v_{B}}\right)\right)\,.

(145)

We note that, similar to eq. (
109
), eq. (
145
) is IR finite, symmetric under
u_{B}\leftrightarrow v_{B}
, but does not vanish in either MRK or
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limits. We emphasise that eq. (
143
) is exact, but in the following we make the NMRK approximation (
110
) for
U_{B}
or
U_{B^{\prime}}
, after which the functions
U_{B}
and
U_{B^{\prime}}
are related by
p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}
. We also make the NMRK approximation to the cross ratios in eq. (
145
), after which this function is symmetric under
p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}
.

Following the procedure of section
4.1
, we now wish to express the opposite-helicity amplitude
using the colour-structure basis of eq. (
117
). To this end, we apply the colour rotation of eq. (
113
) to the sum inside the curly brackets in eq. (
132
). We find that the structure of the kinematic coefficients is similar to the same-helicity case, motivating the following expression for general helicities, generalising eq. (
117
),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Disp}\left[{\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5}^{\nu_{5}})}\right]}\xrightarrow[\rm NMRK]{}

\displaystyle\frac{g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}}{(4\pi)^{2}}\,s_{12}F^{a_{3}a_{2}c_{1}}C^{g(0)}\left(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{-\nu_{2}}\right)\frac{1}{t_{1}}\,\times

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2}}\Bigg\{(F^{a_{\sigma_{4}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{5}}})_{c_{1}c_{3}}\left(A^{gg(1)}_{\vDash}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{4}}}_{\sigma_{4}},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{5}}}_{\sigma_{5}},q_{3})+A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{4}}}_{\sigma_{4}},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{5}}}_{\sigma_{5}},q_{3})E(t_{1},s_{3\sigma_{4}};t_{3},s_{\sigma_{5}6})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{N_{c}}d_{A}^{c_{1}a_{\sigma_{4}}a_{\sigma_{5}}c_{3}}A^{gg(1)}_{d}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{4}}}_{\sigma_{4}},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{5}}}_{\sigma_{5}},q_{3})\Bigg\}\times\frac{1}{t_{3}}F^{a_{6}a_{1}c_{3}}C^{g(0)}\left(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{6}^{-\nu_{1}}\right)\,.

(146)

For the opposite-helicity case we find that the kinematic coefficient of the tree-level colour structure is

\displaystyle A^{gg(1)}_{\vDash}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})

(147)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\Bigg[2A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\Big(2W^{A}_{2}+W^{B}_{1}\Big)-2A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})\Big(W^{A^{\prime}}_{2}-W^{B^{\prime}}_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+2\Big(S^{A}_{uv}(W^{A}_{1}-W^{A}_{2})+S^{A}_{wu}(W^{A}_{3}-W^{A}_{2})\Big)-\Big(S^{A^{\prime}}_{uv}(W^{A^{\prime}}_{1}-W^{A^{\prime}}_{2})+S^{A^{\prime}}_{wu}(W^{A^{\prime}}_{3}-W^{A^{\prime}}_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-\Big(S^{B}_{vw}(W^{B}_{2}-W^{B}_{1})+S^{B}_{wu}(W^{B}_{3}-W^{B}_{1})\Big)\Bigg]\,.

There is some freedom in how to partition the kinematic coefficient of
d_{A}
as a sum over colour orderings. Before discussing our choice of how we organise this term, let us investigate the MRK limit of eq. (
147
). We first drop the rational terms that are power suppressed in this limit,

\displaystyle A^{gg(1)}_{\vDash}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})

(148)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\Bigg[A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\Big(3U_{\mathrm{MRK}}+2\Delta W_{A}(u_{A},v_{A},w_{A})+\Delta W_{B}(u_{B},v_{B},w_{B})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+2\Big(S^{A}_{uv}(W^{A}_{1}-W^{A}_{2})+S^{A}_{wu}(W^{A}_{3}-W^{A}_{2})\Big)-S^{B}_{vw}\big(W^{B}_{2}-W^{B}_{1}\big)\Bigg]\,,

where
U_{\mathrm{MRK}}
is the helicity-independent MRK limit of the central transcendental functions, introduced in eq. (
123
). We now note that the nonvanishing terms of
\Delta W_{A}
and
\Delta W_{B}
cancel against the MRK limits of the terms proportional to
S^{A}
and
S^{B}
respectively, i.e.

\displaystyle\begin{split}A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\Delta W_{A}(u_{A},v_{A},w_{A})&\underset{\mathrm{MRK}}{=}-S^{A}_{uv}(W^{A}_{1}-W^{A}_{2})-S^{A}_{wu}(W^{A}_{3}-W^{A}_{2})\,,\\
A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\Delta W_{B}(u_{B},v_{B},w_{B})&\underset{\mathrm{MRK}}{=}S^{B}_{vw}(W^{B}_{2}-W^{B}_{1})\,.\end{split}

(149)

With this we see that the MRK limit of eq. (
148
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}A^{gg(1)}_{\vDash}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p_{4}^{\oplus},q_{2})\frac{1}{t_{2}}V^{g(0)}(q_{2},p_{5}^{\ominus},q_{3})\,U_{\mathrm{MRK}}\,,\end{split}

(150)

coincides with the MRK limit of the same-helicity amplitude, eq. (
125
), manifesting the helicity independence of the MRK limit.
A similar analysis shows that the
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limit of eq. (
148
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}A^{gg(1)}_{\vDash}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p_{5}^{\ominus},q_{2}^{\prime})\frac{1}{t_{2}^{\prime}}V^{g(0)}(q_{2}^{\prime},p_{4}^{\oplus},q_{3})\,U_{\mathrm{MRK^{\prime}}}\,,\end{split}

(151)

coincides with the
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limit of the same-helicity vertex, eq. (
127
).

Equation (
149
) also provides our motivation for writing the kinematic coefficient of the totally symmetric colour structure as

\displaystyle\begin{split}A^{gg(1)}_{d}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})&=2A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\left(W_{2}^{A}-W_{1}^{B}\right)\\
&+S^{A}_{uv}(W^{A}_{1}-W^{A}_{2})+S^{A}_{wu}(W^{A}_{3}-W^{A}_{2})+S^{B}_{vw}(W^{B}_{2}-W^{B}_{1}),\end{split}

(152)

with the aforementioned property eq. (
137
) leading to

\displaystyle\begin{split}A^{gg(1)}_{d}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})&=2A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})\left(W^{A^{\prime}}_{2}-W_{1}^{B^{\prime}}\right)\\
&+S^{A^{\prime}}_{uv}(W^{A^{\prime}}_{1}-W^{A^{\prime}}_{2})+S^{A^{\prime}}_{wu}(W^{A^{\prime}}_{3}-W^{A^{\prime}}_{2})+S^{B}_{wu}(W^{B}_{3}-W^{B}_{1}).\end{split}

(153)

This choice gives us the simple MRK and MRK
′
limits,

\displaystyle\begin{split}A^{gg(1)}_{d}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}0\,,\\
A^{gg(1)}_{d}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}0\,.\end{split}

(154)

similar to eqs. (
126
) and (
128
).

4.3
NLO
gg\to gggg
squared matrix element in the central NMRK limit

Having obtained the central NMRK limit of the six-gluon one-loop amplitude in
{{\cal N}=4}
SYM for all leading helicity configurations, we now consider the contribution of this amplitude to the squared matrix element for

gg\to gggg
at NLO, i.e. the interference of this amplitude with the tree-level amplitude given in eqs. (
25
) and (
26
), summed over all colour indices:

2{\mathrm{Re}\left[{{\cal M}^{(0)*}_{6g}{\cal M}^{(1)}_{6g}}\right]}=2{\mathrm{Re}\left[{{\cal M}^{(0)*}_{6g}\mathrm{Disp}\left[{\cal M}^{(1)}_{6g}\right]}\right]}-2{\mathrm{Im}\left[{{\cal M}^{(0)*}_{6g}\mathrm{Absorp}\left[{\cal M}^{(1)}_{6g}\right]}\right]}\,.

(155)

We consider these two contributions in turn.

Dispersive part

We first consider the contribution of the dispersive part of the one-loop amplitude, eq. (
146
), to eq. (
155
).
The colour structures contributing to
{\cal M}^{(0)*}_{6g}\mathrm{Disp}\big[{\cal M}^{(1)}_{6g}\big]
are depicted as interference diagrams in figure
8
. For each of them, after summing over the colour indices
a_{2}
,
a_{3}
and
a_{1}
,
a_{6}
, the factors associated with the impact factors are proportional to the identity in the adjoint representation, i.e.

\mbox{tr\,}(F^{c_{1}}F^{\tilde{c}_{1}})=2N_{c}\delta^{c_{1}\tilde{c}_{1}}\,,\qquad\qquad\mbox{tr\,}(F^{c_{3}}F^{\tilde{c}_{3}})=2N_{c}\delta^{c_{3}\tilde{c}_{3}}\,.

(156)

Figure 8:
Three of the colour structures contributing to
{\cal M}^{(0)*}_{6g}\mathrm{Disp}\big[{\cal M}^{(1)}_{6g}\big]
using the basis of one-loop colour structures in eq. (
146
). The remaining three colour structures can be obtained by interchanging
a_{4}\leftrightarrow a_{5}
. A three-gluon vertex represents the adjoint generator
F
, while the
\otimes
symbol represents the fully symmetric trace
d_{A}
of eq. (
115
).

We then see that the colour structures involving the totally symmetric tensor
d_{A}
vanish after contraction with the partially antisymmetric tree-level colour-structure:

\displaystyle 4N_{c}^{2}\delta^{c_{1}\tilde{c}_{1}}\delta^{c_{3}\tilde{c}_{3}}d_{A}^{c_{1}a_{4}a_{5}c_{3}}(F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}})_{\tilde{c}_{1}\tilde{c}_{3}}=-8N_{c}^{2}d_{A}^{c_{1}a_{4}a_{5}c_{3}}f^{c_{1}a_{4}\tilde{c}_{2}}f^{\tilde{c}_{2}a_{5}c_{3}}=0.

(157)

The coefficient
A_{d}^{gg(1)}
of the two-gluon CEV in eq. (
146
) therefore does not contribute to the NLO
gg\to 4\,\text{jet}
cross section, or by extension, to the N
3
LO dijet cross section after phase-space integration. We emphasise that this conclusion applies to any helicity configuration of the one-loop two-gluon CEV.

By a similar argument, the symmetric colour structure of the two-gluon CEV does not contribute to the NNLL BFKL kernel.
To see this we note that at this logarithmic accuracy, the relevant interference, or ‘ladder’ diagrams describing an arbitrary number of real emissions along the ladder, including precisely one instance of the one-loop two-gluon CEV, have all other ‘rungs’ generated by the interference of tree-level impact factors and one-gluon CEVs (those appearing in fig.
1
(b)). The colour structure containing
d_{A}
of such a ladder diagram is depicted in figure
9
. Given that the colour structure of the tree-level impact factors are either a fundamental generator or adjoint structure constant, and that the colour-structure of any one-gluon CEV is simply a structure constant, it follows that upon summing over the colour indices, the entire ladder of rungs above and below the one-loop two-gluon CEV collapse with the result proportional the identity in the adjoint representation (with each rung yielding an extra factor of
2N_{c}
). At the final stage, the contraction between
d_{A}
and the structure constant yields a vanishing result, just as in eq. (
157
).

Figure 9:
A colour structure of an interference, or ‘ladder’ diagram at NNLL accuracy, containing one instance of the one-loop two-gluon CEV, and all other real emissions given by tree-level one-gluon CEVs. The
\otimes
symbol represents the fully symmetric trace
d_{A}
of the two-gluon CEV. This colour structure vanishes upon summing over all colour indices, as discussed in the main text.

We further note that the symmetric colour structure of the two-gluon CEV is expected to contribute to the cross section and to the BFKL kernel at N
3
LL accuracy: at this order one must consider the square of the one-loop two-gluon CEV, where the interference involving two
d_{A}
terms survives, and yields a quartic Casimir invariant.

Having seen that the symmetric trace of four generators,
d_{A}
, which appears in the two-gluon CEV, does not contribute to the cross section or the BFKL kernel at NNLL, it is interesting to ask whether such symmetric colour structures contribute at this logarithmic accuracy to
any
of the interference diagrams making up the BFKL kernel in fig.
3
. The answer is negative: upon summing over colour indices, the impact factors in any of these interference diagrams would be proportional to the identity in the adjoint representation. This argument holds for both gluon or quark impact factors. Having contracted the impact factors, the virtual loops and the central gluons only involve six colour vertices in total, which cannot form a quartic Casimir in any of the contributions in fig.
3
.
This may be contrasted with contributions to the
2\to 2
amplitude from triple Reggeon exchange, which do involve quartic Casimirs at NNLL accuracy
Falcioni:2020lvv
;
Falcioni:2021buo
;
Falcioni:2021dgr
.

Absorptive part

We now consider the contribution of the absorptive part of the one-loop amplitude to eq. (
155
). As mentioned above, for the absorptive part of the one-loop amplitude, all signature components contribute at leading power in the NMRK limit, although they do not have logarithmic dependence on the large invariants
s_{34}
or
s_{56}
. The four signature components are listed in eqs. (
239
)–(
242
) for
\nu_{4}=\nu_{5}
and in eqs. (
243
)–(
246
) for
\nu_{4}=-\nu_{5}
. However, the leading-power behaviour of the tree-level amplitude is given by the
(-,-)
component alone, and this projects out the
(-,-)
component of the absorptive one-loop amplitude
in
{\cal M}^{(0)*}_{6g}\mathrm{Absorp}\big[{\cal M}^{(1)}_{6g}\big]
upon summing over the external colour indices. For the
(-,-)
component of the absorptive part of the amplitude (eq. (
239
) for the
\nu_{4}=\nu_{5}
case and eq. (
243
) for the
\nu_{4}=-\nu_{5}
case), we can factor out an adjoint generator associated to the tree-level impact factors. Upon considering the interference between these absorptive parts and the tree-level amplitude, we therefore find that the colour factors associated with the impact factors are identical to the dispersive case above, eq. (
156
).

To proceed and evaluate the imaginary part of
{\cal M}^{(0)*}_{6g}\mathrm{Absorp}\big[{\cal M}^{(1)}_{6g}\big]
we now distinguish between the same-helicity case, where the relevant absorptive part is given in eq. (
239
), and the opposite-helicity case, where the relevant absorptive part is given in eq. (
243
). For the former case, the colour factors which contain
\mbox{tr\,}(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}}F^{c_{3}})
or
\mbox{tr\,}(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{5}}F^{a_{4}}F^{c_{3}})
do not contribute to the absorptive interference term, due to either the vanishing of the colour factor or the reality of its kinematic coefficient. The remaining contribution is proportional to

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mbox{tr\,}(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{5}}F^{c_{3}}F^{a_{4}})(F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}})_{c_{1}c_{3}}\mathrm{Im}\left[B^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3})A^{gg(0)*}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3})\right]\\
+\,&\mbox{tr\,}(F^{c_{1}}F^{a_{5}}F^{c_{3}}F^{a_{4}})(F^{a_{5}}F^{a_{4}})_{c_{1}c_{3}}\mathrm{Im}\left[B^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5},q_{3})A^{gg(0)*}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})\right]=0.\end{split}

(158)

To show that this quantity is zero we first note that the two colour factors are equal, which can be shown using the reversal-invariance property of the trace of four adjoint generators, along with a relabelling of the indices
a_{4}
and
a_{5}
. After factoring out the common colour factor one is left with the imaginary part of a kinematic factor which can be shown to be real using eq. (
54
). We conclude that the absorptive part of the amplitude does not contribute to the squared matrix element for
gg\to gggg
at NLO in the central NMRK limit for the helicity configurations with
\nu_{4}=\nu_{5}
.

Repeating the above analysis for the opposite-helicity case we find a non-vanishing result. Specifically, for
\nu_{4}=\oplus
and
\nu_{5}=\ominus
we find that

\displaystyle\begin{split}2\mathrm{Im}\Big[{\cal M}^{(0)*}_{6g}(p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5})&\,\mathrm{Absorp}\left[{\cal M}^{(1)}_{6g}(p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5})\right]\Big]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}2\,g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{8}\frac{\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}}{4\pi}\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{s^{2}}{t_{1}^{2}t_{3}^{2}}\left|C^{g(0)}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{-\nu_{2}})\right|^{2}\\
\times 8\,N_{c}^{5}(N_{c}^{2}-1)\bigg\{&\mathrm{Im}\left[\Big(S_{uv}^{A}\log(v_{A})+S_{wu}^{A}\log(w_{A})\Big)A^{gg(0)*}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\right]\\
+&\mathrm{Im}\left[\left(S_{uv}^{A^{\prime}}\log(v_{A^{\prime}})+S_{wu}^{A^{\prime}}\log(w_{A^{\prime}})\right)A^{gg(0)*}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})\right]\\
-&\mathrm{Im}\left[S_{uv}^{B}B^{gg(0)*}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\right]\log\left(\frac{w_{B}}{u_{B}v_{B}}\right)\bigg\}\times\left|C^{g(0)}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{6}^{-\nu_{1}})\right|^{2}\,,\end{split}

(159)

which is generically non-zero.

Next we observe that the absorptive interference term for
\nu_{4}=\ominus
and
\nu_{5}=\oplus
is equal to the complex conjugate of eq. (
159
). Therefore, the absorptive part of the one-loop amplitude in NMRK does not contribute to the helicity-summed squared matrix element for
gg\to gggg
at NLO. Thus, the contribution of the one-loop six-gluon amplitude in the central NMRK limit to the cross section at this logarithmic accuracy is entirely driven by the dispersive part.

5
All-orders conjectures for amplitudes in a central NMRK limit

In section
4
we discussed the six-gluon
{\cal N}=4
SYM amplitude to one-loop accuracy. However, the results of section
4.1
and
4.2
are suggestive of the factorised form that amplitudes may take in the central NMRK limit at higher orders, and potentially at higher multiplicity. In this section we therefore make conjectures for the form that the dispersive part of amplitudes may take beyond one loop and beyond the six-gluon case.

The separation of the non-central
E
functions in eq. (
146
), multiplying the tree-level two-gluon CEV, strongly suggests a factorised picture, analogous to eq. (
21
), for the production of two gluons in the central NMRK limit. The one-loop two-gluon CEV extracted this way is a component of BFKL at NNLL accuracy.
As noted in ref.
Canay:2021yvm
, one can place limits on the pattern of Reggeization by requiring that the MRK limit of said CEV agrees with the known behaviour of amplitudes in MRK.
The following Reggeization ansatz for the production of two gluons in the central NMRK limit is compatible with both the known MRK limit and with the structure of the dispersive part of the one-loop amplitude eq. (
146
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathrm{Disp}\left[{{\cal M}^{(-,-)}_{6g}}\right]}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}s\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{3}})_{a_{2}c_{1}}\,C^{g}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}})\right]\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2}}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{t_{1}}\,\frac{1}{2}\left[\left(\frac{s_{3\sigma_{4}}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{1})}+\left(\frac{-s_{3\sigma_{4}}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{1})}\right]\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{2}(F^{a_{\sigma_{4}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{5}}})_{c_{1}c_{3}}\,A^{gg}_{\vDash}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{4}}}_{\sigma_{4}},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{5}}}_{\sigma_{5}},q_{3})+\frac{1}{N_{c}}d^{c_{1}a_{\sigma_{4}}a_{\sigma_{5}}c_{3}}_{A}A^{gg}_{d}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{4}}}_{\sigma_{4}},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{5}}}_{\sigma_{5}},q_{3})\right]\,\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\frac{1}{t_{3}}\,\frac{1}{2}\left[\left(\frac{s_{{\sigma_{5}}6}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{3})}+\left(\frac{-s_{{\sigma_{5}}6}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{3})}\right]\Bigg\}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{6}})_{a_{1}c_{3}}\,C^{g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}})\right]\,.\end{split}

(160)

This factorization is illustrated schematically in figure
10
(a). The first thing to note is that, unlike amplitudes at LL, or signature-odd amplitudes at NLL, the amplitude is not fully factorised. Rather, eq. (
160
) is rather a sum over two colour orderings, each of which is factorised in the familiar form of impact factors, Reggeized gluons and a two-gluon CEV.

Further to this point are the assumptions we have made regarding the colour structure
d_{A}
beyond one-loop accuracy. In eq. (
160
) we have assumed that this
totally-symmetric colour factor couples to Reggeized gluons at higher orders in
\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle S}
. Specifically,
eq. (
160
) assumes a particular form for this Reggeization, based on the partitioning of the kinematic coefficient of
d_{A}
, sections
4.1
and
4.2
. While this partitioning seems natural from the point of view of requiring compatibility with the known MRK limit, the nature of Reggeization of this term of course cannot be determined from the one-loop amplitude studied in this paper.
It may be constrained by taking the central NMRK limit of the two-loop six-gluon amplitude. A similar structure is accessible through the non-central NMRK limit of the two-loop five-gluon amplitude.

Figure 10:
(a) The expected factorised picture of the dispersive part of a six-gluon amplitude in the central NMRK limit. (b) The expected factorization of an
n
-gluon amplitude in a central NMRK limit. The coloured blobs represent impact factors, and one- and two gluon CEVs, defined to all orders in perturbation theory, similarly to figure
4
.

With these assumptions in mind, we may consider how eq. (
160
) may be generalised to the case of
2\to n
scattering in any of the central NMRK limits where the rapidity separation between a pair of consecutive central gluons denoted by
m
and
m+1
(with
4\leq m\leq n-2
) is not necessarily large, while all other rapidity separations are large.
We propose the following ansatz for an arbitrary number of legs:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathrm{Disp}\left[{{\cal M}^{[\mathbf{8}_{a}]}_{ng}}\right]}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}s\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{3}})_{a_{2}c_{1}}\,C^{g}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}})\right]\\
&\times\prod_{i=4}^{m-1}\left(\frac{1}{t_{i-3}}\,\frac{1}{2}\left[\left(\frac{s_{i-1,i}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{i-3})}+\left(\frac{-s_{i-1,i}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{i-3})}\right]\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{i}})_{c_{i-3}c_{i-2}}V^{g}(q_{i-3},p_{i}^{\nu_{i}},q_{i-2})\right]\right)\\
&\times\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2}}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{t_{m-3}}\,\frac{1}{2}\left[\left(\frac{s_{m-1,\sigma_{m}}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{m-3})}+\left(\frac{-s_{m-1,\sigma_{m}}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{m-3})}\right]\\
&\times\bigg[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{2}(F^{a_{\sigma_{m}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{m+1}}})_{c_{m-3}c_{m-1}}\,A^{gg}_{\vDash}(q_{m-3},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{m}}}_{\sigma_{m}},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{m+1}}}_{\sigma_{m+1}},q_{m-1})\\
&\hskip 150.0pt+\frac{1}{N_{c}}d^{c_{m-3}a_{\sigma_{m}}a_{\sigma_{m+1}}c_{m-1}}_{A}A^{gg}_{d}(q_{m-3},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{m}}}_{\sigma_{m}},p^{\nu_{\sigma_{m+1}}}_{\sigma_{m+1}},q_{m-1})\bigg]\,\\
&\times\frac{1}{t_{m-1}}\,\frac{1}{2}\left[\left(\frac{s_{\sigma_{m+1},m+2}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{m-1})}+\left(\frac{-s_{\sigma_{m+1},m+2}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{m-1})}\right]\Bigg\}\\
&\times\prod_{i=m+2}^{n-1}\left(\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{i}})_{c_{i-3}c_{i-2}}V^{g}(q_{i-3},p_{i}^{\nu_{i}},q_{i-2})\right]\frac{1}{t_{i-2}}\,\frac{1}{2}\left[\left(\frac{s_{i,i+1}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{i-2})}+\left(\frac{-s_{i,i+1}}{\tau}\right)^{\alpha(t_{i-2})}\right]\right)\\
&\times\left[g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}(F^{a_{n}})_{a_{1}c_{n-3}}\,C^{g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{n}^{\nu_{n}})\right]\,,\end{split}

(161)

which is written entirely in terms of the components extracted from
n\leq 6
amplitudes. This factorised structure is depicted schematically in figure
10
(b), where real emissions that are strongly ordered in rapidity are described in a similar way to the MRK structure depicted in figure
4
.

6
Conclusions

Taking the one-loop six-gluon amplitudes of
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM
Bern:1994zx
;
Bern:1994cg
into the central NMRK limit, in this paper we have obtained the one-loop corrections to the two-gluon central-emission vertex (CEV) in this theory, in both the same-helicity and opposite-helicity configurations. This is a first step toward the determination of the same quantity in QCD.

In contrast with the one-gluon CEV,
we find that the two-gluon CEV, eq. (
84
), receives one-loop corrections involving a new colour structure consisting of a fully symmetric trace of four adjoint generators,
which is not present at tree level. We find, however, that this new structure does not contribute to the squared matrix element for
gg\to gggg
at NLO, so it does not enter the NNLL BFKL kernel. We note, however that this symmetric colour structure does contribute to the squared matrix element at N
3
LL accuracy, where the interference between two one-loop two-gluon CEVs gives rise to a quartic Casimir invariant.

As discussed in section
4.3
, none of the NNLL contributions to the BFKL kernel, which are described by the interference diagrams in fig.
3
, gives rise to a quartic Casimir. This may be contrasted with contributions to the
2\to 2
amplitude from triple-Reggeon exchange, which do involve quartic Casimirs at NNLL accuracy
Falcioni:2020lvv
;
Falcioni:2021buo
;
Falcioni:2021dgr
.

In addition to the new colour structure, also the kinematic dependence of the two-gluon CEV is rather complex. In the same-helicity two-gluon CEV, which is extracted from an MHV amplitude, dilogarithms survive in the NMRK limit, eqs. (
101
) and (
109
). The dilogarithms depend on conformally-invariant cross ratios (
42
) and (
56
), the same variables which characterize six-gluon amplitudes in planar
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM
Drummond:2007au
.

Yet another layer of complexity arises in the case of the one-loop NMHV amplitude, from which the opposite-helicity two-gluon CEV is extracted.
Considering this amplitude in central NMRK, eq. (
146
), eq. (
147
) and eq. (
152
), we find rational coefficients which are not proportional to the tree amplitude, accompanied by different transcendental functions, eq. (
131
).
This structure involves an interplay between spurious poles of these rational coefficients and zeroes of the transcendental functions multiplying them, see eqs. (
132
)–(
136
) and tables
1
and
2
.
These features propagate into the one-loop opposite-helicity CEV.

Another consequence of the more complicated structure of the one-loop NMHV amplitude is that, in contrast with the MHV case, an absorptive part survives in the squared matrix element for
gg\to gggg
at NLO, after summing over colour indices, as shown in section
4.3
.
However, upon summing over gluon helicities, this absorptive contribution vanishes, and thus only the dispersive part of the one-loop six-gluon amplitude contributes to unpolarised cross sections at NNLL.

In ref.
Bartels:2008ce
, it was noted that the transcendental functions of the one-loop six-gluon MHV amplitude
can be written exactly in terms of the known one-loop Regge components, eq. (
97
), consisting of impact factors, Reggeized gluons and Lipatov vertices, plus a remainder term (
101
) written in terms of the conformally-invariant cross ratios (
42
).
In eqs. (
139
) and (
141
), we have shown that this holds also for the NMHV amplitude, and thus for the whole one-loop six-gluon amplitude in
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM.
This parallels the set-up of the
two-loop six-gluon MHV amplitude in planar
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM, which can be expressed in terms of two-loop Regge components
DelDuca:2008jg
and remainder functions depending on conformally-invariant cross ratios (
42
), and which can be evaluated in the central NMRK limit without any loss of generality
DelDuca:2009au
;
DelDuca:2010zg
. It would be interesting to extend this analysis to the two-loop six-gluon amplitude in full
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM.

It follows in particular that the transcendental functions making up the
one-loop two-gluon CEV can be expressed in terms of one-loop MRK components, evaluated at NMRK kinematics, plus a remainder function.
The former is independent of the helicity configuration, eq. (
95
), while the latter has a distinct functional form for each of the two helicity configurations.

Our one-loop analysis of the two-gluon CEV provides first hints with regards to the factorization and exponentiation properties of the six-gluon amplitude.
In eq. (
160
) we have proposed an exponentiation pattern of the six-gluon amplitude in the central NMRK limit in which rapidity ordering is partially linked with colour ordering. The guiding principles have been the locality of the two-gluon CEV in rapidity, and its factorisation in the MRK limit into two one-gluon CEVs connected by a Regge trajectory.
However, the link between rapidity and colour ordering in (
160
) is only partial, because of the presence of a fully-symmetric colour structure, which occurs first at the one-loop level, eq. (
146
): an evaluation of the two-loop amplitude in the NMRK limit is necessary to confirm or disprove this factorized structure. We leave the investigation of two-loop amplitudes in the NMRK limit to future work.

In the NLL corrections to the BFKL kernel (fig.
2
b), the one-loop one-gluon CEV is multiplied by the corresponding tree-level CEV, with the gluon
p_{4}
emitted centrally along the ladder which is
integrated over its phase space. No collinear divergences are allowed in MRK, eq. (
7
), however in the phase-space integration, the gluon
p_{4}
may become soft and yield a pole of
{\cal O}(1/\epsilon)
. In order to generate all the finite terms of the squared amplitude, the one-loop one-gluon CEV must be evaluated to
{\cal O}(\epsilon)
in the limit that the gluon
p_{4}
is soft
Fadin:1996yv
;
DelDuca:1998cx
, which requires the use of one-loop soft functions to
{\cal O}(\epsilon)

Bern:1998sc
.
In the NNLL corrections to the BFKL kernel, similar features are present: the one-loop two-gluon CEV is multiplied by the corresponding tree-level CEV with the gluons
p_{4}
and
p_{5}
emitted along the ladder, which are integrated over their phase space. In the phase-space integration, both gluons may become soft, as well as collinear to each other, yielding poles of up to
{\cal O}(1/\epsilon^{3})
. Accordingly, the one-loop two-gluon CEV must be evaluated to
{\cal O}(\epsilon^{3})
in the limit that gluons
p_{4}
and
p_{5}
are soft, and/or collinear to each other. To higher order in
\epsilon
, the one-loop splitting functions are known
Kosower:1999rx
;
Bern:1999ry
, but the one-loop double-soft functions are yet to be determined. We leave the analysis of the
higher-order terms of the one-loop two-gluon CEV to future work.

For generic gluon momenta, in order to obtain the two-gluon CEV in QCD using the supersymmetric decomposition of one-loop amplitudes, the next step is to perform a similar NMRK analysis of the contributions of a
\mathcal{N}=1
multiplet and of a scalar circulating in the loop. We also leave this analysis for the future.

Appendix A
Kinematic regions

A.1
General multi-parton kinematics

We consider the production of four partons of momenta
p_{i}
,
i=3,4,5,6
,
in the scattering between two partons of
momenta
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
, as depicted in figure
11
. By convention,
we consider the scattering in the unphysical region where all momenta
are taken as outgoing, and then we analytically continue to the
physical region where
p_{1}^{0}<0
and
p_{2}^{0}<0
. Thus
partons are incoming or outgoing depending on the sign
of their energy. Since the helicity of a positive-energy
(negative-energy) massless spinor has the same (opposite) sign as its
chirality, the helicities assigned to the partons
depend on whether they are incoming or outgoing.
We label outgoing (positive-energy) particles
with their helicity; if they are incoming the
actual helicity and charge are reversed, e.g. an incoming left-handed
parton is labelled as an outgoing right-handed anti-parton.

Figure 11:
Depiction of the production of four gluons of momenta
p_{i}
,
i=3,4,5,6
,
in the scattering between two partons of
momenta
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
. We take all momenta to be outgoing.

Using light-cone coordinates
p^{\pm}=p^{0}\pm p^{z}
, and
complex transverse coordinates
p_{\perp}=p^{x}+ip^{y}
, with scalar
product,

2p\cdot q=p^{+}q^{-}+p^{-}q^{+}-p_{\perp}q^{*}_{\perp}-p^{*}_{\perp}q_{\perp}\,,

(162)

the four-momenta are,

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{p_{1}^{-}}{2},0,0,\frac{-p_{1}^{-}}{2}\right)\equiv\left(0,p_{1}^{-};0,0\right)\,,

\displaystyle p_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{p_{2}^{+}}{2},0,0,\frac{p_{2}^{+}}{2}\right)\equiv\left(p_{2}^{+},0;0,0\right)\,,

(163)

\displaystyle p_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{p_{i}^{+}+p_{i}^{-}}{2},{\rm Re}[p_{i\perp}],{\rm Im}[p_{i\perp}],\frac{p_{i}^{+}-p_{i}^{-}}{2}\right)\equiv|p_{i\perp}|\left(e^{y_{i}},e^{-y_{i}};\cos{\phi_{i}},\sin{\phi_{i}}\right)\,\,,

where
y
is the rapidity, and
3\leq i\leq 6
. The first notation in eq. (
163
) is the
standard representation

p^{\mu}=(p^{0},p^{x},p^{y},p^{z})
, while in the second we have the
+
and
-

light-cone components on the left of the semicolon,
and the transverse components on the right.

From momentum conservation,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=3}^{6}p_{i\perp}\,,

\displaystyle p_{2}^{+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=3}^{6}p_{i}^{+}\,,

(164)

\displaystyle p_{1}^{-}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=3}^{6}p_{i}^{-}\,,

and using the scalar product eq. (
162
),
the Mandelstam invariants may be written as,

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2p_{1}\cdot p_{2}=\sum_{i,j=3}^{6}p_{i}^{+}p_{j}^{-}

\displaystyle s_{2i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2p_{2}\cdot p_{i}=-\sum_{j=3}^{6}p_{i}^{-}p_{j}^{+}

(165)

\displaystyle s_{1i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2p_{1}\cdot p_{i}=-\sum_{j=3}^{6}p_{i}^{+}p_{j}^{-}

\displaystyle s_{ik}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2p_{i}\cdot p_{k}=p_{i}^{+}p_{k}^{-}+p_{i}^{-}p_{k}^{+}-p_{i\perp}p^{\ast}_{k\perp}-p^{\ast}_{i\perp}p_{k\perp}\,,

with
3\leq i,k\leq 6
.

For the momenta in eq. (
163
), we find the following right-handed spinor products,

\langle k\,p\rangle=\overline{u}_{-}(k)u_{+}(p)
,
in the notation of Ref.
DelDuca:1999iql
,

\displaystyle\langle p_{i}\,p_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{i\perp}\sqrt{\frac{p_{j}^{+}}{p_{i}^{+}}}-p_{j\perp}\sqrt{\frac{p_{i}^{+}}{p_{j}^{+}}}\,,

\displaystyle\langle p_{2}\,p_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\sqrt{\frac{-p_{2}^{+}}{p_{i}^{+}}}\,p_{i\perp}\,,

(166)

\displaystyle\langle p_{i}\,p_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\sqrt{-p_{1}^{-}p_{i}^{+}}\,,

\displaystyle\langle p_{2}\,p_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{p_{2}^{+}p_{1}^{-}}\,,

where we have used the mass-shell condition,

|p_{i\perp}|^{2}=p_{i}^{+}p_{i}^{-}\,.

(167)

Left-handed spinor products,
[k\,p]=\overline{u}_{+}(k)u_{-}(p)
, are given by complex conjugation,

[k\,p]={\rm sign}(k^{0}p^{0})\langle p\,k\rangle^{\ast}\,.

(168)

Spinor products are antisymmetric,

\langle k\,p\rangle=-\langle p\,k\rangle\,,\qquad[k\,p]=-[p\,k]\,.

(169)

We also use the currents,
[p|\gamma^{\mu}|k\rangle
and
\langle p|\gamma^{\mu}|k]
,
which are related by charge conjugation,

[p|\gamma^{\mu}|k\rangle=\langle k|\gamma^{\mu}|p]\,,

(170)

and complex conjugation,

[p|\gamma^{\mu}|k\rangle^{*}={\rm sign}(k^{0}p^{0})[k|\gamma^{\mu}|p\rangle\,.

(171)

Through the Fierz rearrangement,

\langle k|\gamma^{\mu}|p]\langle v|\gamma^{\mu}|q]=2\langle k\,v\rangle[q\,p]\,,

(172)

and the Gordon identity,

[p|\gamma^{\mu}|p\rangle=\langle p|\gamma^{\mu}|p]=2p^{\mu}\,,

(173)

we obtain that

\displaystyle\langle k|\not{q}|p]=\langle k\,q\rangle[q\,p]\,,

\displaystyle[p|\not{q}|k\rangle=[p\,q]\langle q\,k\rangle\,.

(174)

A.2
Multi-Regge kinematics

In multi-Regge kinematics, we require that the light-cone momenta of the gluons are strongly ordered and have comparable transverse momentum. In the six-gluon case, we take

\displaystyle\begin{split}&p^{+}_{3}\gg p^{+}_{4}\gg p^{+}_{5}\gg p^{+}_{6}\,,\quad\qquad p^{-}_{3}\ll p^{-}_{4}\ll p^{-}_{5}\ll p^{-}_{6}\,,\\
&|p_{3\perp}|\simeq|p_{4\perp}|\simeq|p_{5\perp}|\simeq|p_{6\perp}|\,.\end{split}

(175)

The strong ordering of light-cone momenta in eq. (
175
) is equivalent to requiring a strong-ordering on the rapidities,

y_{3}\gg y_{4}\gg y_{5}\gg y_{6}\,.

(176)

Momentum conservation in eq. (
164
) then becomes

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=3}^{6}p_{i\perp}\,,

\displaystyle p_{2}^{+}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle-p_{3}^{+}\,,

(177)

\displaystyle p_{1}^{-}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle-p_{6}^{-}\,.

To leading accuracy, the Mandelstam invariants in eq. (
165
) are reduced to

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2p_{1}\cdot p_{2}\simeq p_{3}^{+}p_{6}^{-}\,,

\displaystyle s_{2i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2p_{2}\cdot p_{i}\simeq-p_{3}^{+}p_{i}^{-}\,,

(178)

\displaystyle s_{1i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2p_{1}\cdot p_{i}\simeq-p_{i}^{+}p_{6}^{-}\,,

\displaystyle s_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2p_{i}\cdot p_{j}\simeq p_{i}^{+}p_{j}^{-}\,,

with
3\leq i,j\leq 6
, and with
p^{+}_{i}>p^{+}_{j}
, for
i<j
.
Note that the mass-shell condition in eq. (
167
) implies that

s\,|p_{4\perp}|^{2}|p_{5\perp}|^{2}\simeq s_{34}s_{45}s_{56}\,,

(179)

which is an example of the general multi-Regge constraint,

s_{ij}\,\prod_{k=i+1}^{j-1}|p_{k\perp}|^{2}=\prod_{k=i}^{j-1}s_{k,k+1}\,.

(180)

The spinor products in eq. (
166
) become,

\displaystyle\langle p_{i}\,p_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\frac{p_{i}^{+}}{p_{j}^{+}}}\,p_{j\perp}\,\qquad{\rm for}\,y_{i}>y_{j}\;,

\displaystyle\langle p_{2}\,p_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle-i\sqrt{\frac{p_{3}^{+}}{p_{i}^{+}}}\,p_{i\perp}\,,

(181)

\displaystyle\langle p_{i}\,p_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle i\sqrt{p_{i}^{+}p_{6}^{-}}\,,

\displaystyle\langle p_{2}\,p_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle-\sqrt{p_{3}^{+}p_{6}^{-}}\,,

with
3\leq i\leq 6
. In eqs. (
178
) and (
181
) only the leading terms are displayed.
Here and henceforth, it is understood that, if so required by the computation,
sub-leading terms may need to be retained.

In the Euclidean region, where the Mandelstam invariants are taken as all negative, the ordering in eq. (
175
) becomes

-s_{12}\gg-s_{123},-s_{345}\gg-s_{34},-s_{45},-s_{56}\gg-s_{23},-s_{61},-s_{234}\,.

(182)

Introducing a parameter
\lambda\ll 1
, the hierarchy in eq. (
176
) above is equivalent to the rescaling,

\{s_{123},s_{345}\}={\cal O}(\lambda)\,,\qquad\{s_{34},s_{45},s_{56}\}={\cal O}(\lambda^{2})\,,\qquad\{s_{23},s_{61},s_{234}\}={\cal O}(\lambda^{3})\,.

(183)

A.3
Central next-to-multi-Regge kinematics

We now consider the production of four partons of momenta
p_{i}
, with
3\leq i\leq 6
,
with the new constraint that partons 4 and 5 are in the central region along the gluon ladder,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&p^{+}_{3}\gg p^{+}_{4}\simeq p^{+}_{5}\gg p^{+}_{6}\,,\quad\qquad p^{-}_{3}\ll p^{-}_{4}\simeq p^{-}_{5}\ll p^{-}_{6}\,\,,\\
&|p_{3\perp}|\simeq|p_{4\perp}|\simeq|p_{5\perp}|\simeq|p_{6\perp}|\,,\end{split}

(184)

and hence,

y_{3}\gg y_{4}\simeq y_{5}\gg y_{6}.

(185)

The leading contributions to momentum conservation are the same as in eq. (
177
).
The Mandelstam invariants of eq. (
165
) become

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle p_{3}^{+}p_{6}^{-}\,,

\displaystyle s_{2i}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle-p_{3}^{+}p_{i}^{-}\,,

\displaystyle s_{1i}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle-p_{i}^{+}p_{6}^{-}\,,

(186)

\displaystyle s_{jk}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle p_{j}^{+}p_{k}^{-}\,,\qquad j\neq 4\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,k\neq 5\,,

\displaystyle s_{45}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-}+p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+}-p_{4\perp}^{\ast}p_{5\perp}-p_{5\perp}^{\ast}p_{4\perp}\,,

with
3\leq i,j,k\leq 6
, with
p^{+}_{j}>p^{+}_{k}
for
j<k
.
Note that the mass-shell condition in eq. (
167
) and the Mandelstam invariants in eq. (
186
) imply that

\displaystyle\begin{split}s&\simeq\frac{s_{34}\,s_{56}}{p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+}}\\
&=\frac{s_{34}\,s_{56}\,p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-}}{|p_{4\perp}|^{2}|p_{5\perp}|^{2}}\,.\\
\end{split}

(187)

All three-particle Mandelstam invariants either simplify to a two-particle Mandelstam invariant, or are equal to one of the following, up to an overall sign,

\displaystyle\begin{split}s_{123}&\simeq(p_{4}^{+}+p_{5}^{+})p_{6}^{-}\,,\\
s_{234}&\simeq-|p_{3\perp}+p_{4\perp}|^{2}-p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+}\,,\\
s_{235}&\simeq-|p_{3\perp}+p_{5\perp}|^{2}-p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-}\,,\\
s_{345}&\simeq p_{3}^{+}(p_{4}^{-}+p_{5}^{-})\,.\end{split}

(188)

In the Euclidean region, where the Mandelstam invariants are taken as all negative, the ordering in eq. (
184
) becomes

-s_{12}\gg-s_{34},-s_{56},-s_{123},-s_{345}\gg-s_{23},-s_{45},-s_{61},-s_{234},-s_{235}\,.

(189)

Introducing a parameter
\lambda\ll 1
, the hierarchy in eq. (
184
) above is equivalent to the rescaling,

\{s_{34},s_{56},s_{123},s_{345}\}={\cal O}(\lambda)\,,\qquad\{s_{23},s_{45},s_{61},s_{234},s_{235}\}={\cal O}(\lambda^{2})\,.

(190)

The spinor products in eq. (
166
) become

\displaystyle\langle p_{2}\,p_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle-\sqrt{p_{3}^{+}p_{6}^{-}}\,,

\displaystyle\langle p_{2}\,p_{k}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\sqrt{\frac{-p_{2}^{+}}{p_{k}^{+}}}\,p_{k\perp}\simeq-i\sqrt{\frac{p_{3}^{+}}{p_{k}^{+}}}p_{k\perp}\,,

\displaystyle\langle p_{k}\,p_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\sqrt{-p_{1}^{-}p_{k}^{+}}\,\simeq i\sqrt{p_{k}^{+}p_{6}^{-}}\,,

(191)

\displaystyle\langle p_{j}\,p_{k}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{j\perp}\sqrt{\frac{p_{k}^{+}}{p_{j}^{+}}}-p_{k\perp}\sqrt{\frac{p_{j}^{+}}{p_{k}^{+}}}\simeq-p_{k\perp}\,\sqrt{\frac{p_{j}^{+}}{p_{k}^{+}}}\,,\qquad j\neq 4\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,k\neq 5

\displaystyle\langle p_{4}\,p_{5}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{4\perp}\sqrt{\frac{p_{5}^{+}}{p_{4}^{+}}}-p_{5\perp}\sqrt{\frac{p_{4}^{+}}{p_{5}^{+}}}\,,

with
3\leq j,k\leq 6
, with
p^{+}_{j}>p^{+}_{k}
.

The following spinor strings occur in the BCFW representation of NMHV tree-level amplitudes. We list the spinor strings for two representative colour orderings. For the colour ordering
\{1,2,3,4,5,6\}
the relevant quantities are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle p_{1}|\not{p}_{2}+\not{p}_{3}|p_{4}]&\simeq-i|q_{3\perp}|\sqrt{\frac{p_{4}^{+}}{p_{6}^{+}}}(q_{2\perp}^{*}+p_{4\perp}^{*}\frac{p_{5}^{+}}{p_{4}^{+}})\,,\\
\langle p_{3}|\not{p}_{4}+\not{p}_{5}|p_{6}]&\simeq-q_{3\perp}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{p_{3}^{+}}{p_{6}^{+}}}\left(p_{4\perp}+p_{5\perp}\right)\,,\\
\langle p_{5}|\not{p}_{6}+\not{p}_{1}|p_{2}]&\simeq-ip_{5\perp}\sqrt{\frac{p_{3}^{+}}{p_{5}^{+}}}(q_{2\perp}^{*}+p_{5\perp}^{*}\frac{p_{4}^{-}}{p_{5}^{-}})\,,\end{split}

(192)

while for the colour ordering
\{1,4,2,3,5,6\}
the relevant quantities are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle p_{1}|\not{p}_{4}+\not{p}_{2}|p_{3}]&\simeq-i|q_{3\perp}|\sqrt{\frac{p_{3}^{+}}{p_{6}^{+}}}(p_{5\perp}^{*}+q_{3\perp}^{*})\,,\\
\langle p_{2}|\not{p}_{3}+\not{p}_{5}|p_{6}]&\simeq iq_{3\perp}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{p_{3}^{+}}{p_{6}^{+}}}(p_{4\perp}+q_{3\perp})\,,\\
\langle p_{5}|\not{p}_{6}+\not{p}_{1}|p_{4}]&\simeq-p_{5\perp}q_{2\perp}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{p_{4}^{+}}{p_{5}^{+}}}-p_{4\perp}^{*}q^{\prime}_{2\perp}\sqrt{\frac{p_{5}^{+}}{p_{4}^{+}}}\,.\\
\end{split}

(193)

Appendix B
Lorentz invariant quantities in minimal coordinates

In this section we collect useful expressions for Lorentz-invariant quantities, in the central NMRK region of appendix
A.3
, written in terms of the minimal set of coordinates,
\{w,z,X\}
, introduced in eqs. (
33
) and (
34
). We use the shorthand notation for spinors,

\langle i\,j\rangle=\langle p_{i}\,p_{j}\rangle,\qquad[i\,j]=[p_{i}\,p_{j}],\qquad\langle i|k+l|j]=\langle p_{i}|\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mskip 1.0mu/$}\hss}p_{l}+\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\mskip 1.0mu/$}\hss}p_{k}|p_{j}].

(194)

Of particular importance to the central NMRK limit is the spinor product,

\displaystyle\langle 4\,5\rangle=-q_{1\perp}\,\frac{w+Xz}{w\sqrt{X}(z-1)}\,,

(195)

which is treated exactly in this limit. The three-particle invariants of eq. (
188
) become

\displaystyle\begin{split}s_{123}&=|q_{1\perp}|^{2}\frac{p_{4}^{+}}{p_{6}^{+}}\frac{(1+X)|w-1|^{2}|z|^{2}}{X|z-1|^{2}|w|^{2}}\,,\\
s_{234}&=-|q_{1\perp}|^{2}\,\frac{(1+X|z|^{2})}{X|z-1|^{2}}\,,\\
s_{235}&=-|q_{1\perp}|^{2}\frac{(|w+z-wz|^{2}+X|z|^{2})}{|z-1|^{2}|w|^{2}}\,,\\
s_{345}&=|q_{1\perp}|^{2}\frac{p_{3}^{+}}{p_{4}^{+}}\frac{(|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2})}{|z-1|^{2}|w|^{2}}\,.\\
\end{split}

(196)

The lightcone-momentum dependence cancels when considering physical quantities such as amplitudes.
The spinor strings of eq. (
192
) become

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle 1|2+3|4]&=-iq_{1\perp}^{*}|q_{1\perp}|\sqrt{\frac{p_{4}^{+}}{p_{6}^{+}}}\frac{|w-1||z|}{|z-1||w|}\frac{(1+X\bar{z})}{X(\bar{z}-1)}\,,\\
\langle 3|4+5|6]&=|q_{1\perp}|^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p_{3}^{+}}{p_{6}^{+}}}\,\frac{\bar{z}(\bar{w}-1)(w-z)}{|w|^{2}|z-1|^{2}}\,,\\
\langle 5|6+1|2]&=-i|q_{1\perp}|^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p_{3}^{+}}{p_{5}^{+}}}\frac{(w+X|z|^{2})}{wX|z-1|^{2}}\,,\end{split}

(197)

while the spinor strings of eq. (
193
) become

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle 1|4+2|3]&=-iq_{1\perp}^{*}|q_{1\perp}|\sqrt{\frac{p_{3}^{+}}{p_{6}^{+}}}\frac{|w-1||z|}{|z-1||w|}\frac{\bar{z}}{(\bar{z}-1)}\,,\\
\langle 2|3+5|6]&=-i|q_{1\perp}|^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p_{3}^{+}}{p_{6}^{+}}}\frac{\bar{z}(\bar{w}-1)(w+z-wz)}{|w|^{2}|z-1|^{2}}\,,\\
\langle 5|6+1|4]&=-|q_{1\perp}|^{2}\frac{(w+z-wz+X|z|^{2})}{w\sqrt{X}|z-1|^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(198)

The spurious surfaces listed in eqs. (
44
) and (
62
) can be recognised from eqs. (
197
) and (
198
) respectively.

B.1
Cross ratios in the NMRK limit

In general kinematics, the finite, dual conformally invariant part of the six-gluon amplitude in planar
{\cal N}=4
SYM can be written in terms of multiple polylogarithms with
the following 9-letter alphabet,

\{u,v,w,1-u,1-v,1-w,y_{u},y_{v},y_{w}\}.

(199)

The cross ratios
u
,
v
and
w
for the canonical ordering are given in eq. (
42
), although we appended a further
A
subscript to distinguish this from the other colour orderings that are relevant away from the planar limit. The remaining letters can be constructed from the cross ratios. For
x\in\{u,v,w\}
,
y_{x}
is defined by

\displaystyle\begin{split}y_{x}\equiv\frac{x-z_{+}}{x-z_{-}}\,,\end{split}

(200)

with

z_{\pm}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(u+v+w-1\pm\sqrt{\Delta}),\quad\qquad\Delta\equiv(1-u-v-w)^{2}-4uvw.

(201)

In this section we investigate these quantities for the two representative colour orderings used throughout this paper, namely
\sigma_{A}
and
\sigma_{B}
of eq. (
81
). The corresponding quantities for
\sigma_{A^{\prime}}
can be obtained using the relations given in eq. (
50
).

Permutation
\sigma_{A}

The cross ratios for this colour ordering,
u_{A}
,
v_{A}
and
w_{A}
, are given in eq. (
42
). In NMRK they lie in the range
[0,1]
. In the central NMRK region we list the other six letters,

\displaystyle\begin{split}1-u_{A}&=\frac{X|w-z|^{2}}{(1+X)(|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2})}\,,\\
1-v_{A}&=\frac{|1+Xz|^{2}}{(1+X|z|^{2})(1+X)}\,,\\
1-w_{A}&=\frac{|w+X|z|^{2}|^{2}}{(|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2})(1+X|z|^{2})}\,,\end{split}

(202)

\displaystyle\begin{split}y_{u_{A}}&=\frac{(w+Xz)(1+X\bar{z})(\bar{w}+X|z|^{2})}{(\bar{w}+X\bar{z})(1+Xz)(w+X|z|^{2})}\,,\\
y_{v_{A}}&=\frac{(\bar{w}-\bar{z})(z-1)(w+X|z|^{2})}{(w-z)(\bar{z}-1)(\bar{w}+X|z|^{2})}\,,\\
y_{w_{A}}&=\frac{\bar{z}(\bar{w}-1)(w-z)(1+Xz)}{z(w-1)(\bar{w}-\bar{z})(1+X\bar{z})}\,.\end{split}

(203)

We note that eq. (
203
) are complex phases, which results from the quantity,

\sqrt{\Delta_{A}}=\pm\frac{X\Big((|w|^{2}-X|z|^{2})(z-\bar{z})-|z|^{2}(1-X)(w-\bar{w})+(1-X|z|^{2})(w\bar{z}-\bar{w}z)\Big)}{(1+X)(1+X|z|^{2})(|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2})}\,,

(204)

being purely imaginary. Thus its square is negative,
\Delta_{A}<0
. One can get from any
2\to 4
scattering kinematics to NMRK by a dual conformal transformation
DelDuca:2009au
;
DelDuca:2010zg
. Since
\Delta_{A}
is dual conformally invariant, this means that
\Delta_{A}<0
for any nonsingular
2\to 4
scattering configuration. We further note that the minimal variables, introduced in section
3.1
, rationalise
\sqrt{\Delta_{A}}
.

Permutation
\sigma_{B}

The cross ratios for this colour ordering,
u_{B}
,
v_{B}
and
w_{B}
, are given in eq. (
56
), which in NMRK lie in the range
[0,1]
. In the central NMRK region we list the other six letters,

\displaystyle\begin{split}1-u_{B}&=\frac{|w+z-wz|^{2}}{(|w+z-wz|^{2}+X|z|^{2})}\,,\\
1-v_{B}&=\frac{X|z|^{2}}{(1+X|z|^{2})}\,,\\
1-w_{B}&=\frac{|w+z-wz+X|z|^{2}|^{2}}{(1+X|z|^{2})(|w+z-wz|^{2}+X|z|^{2})}\,,\end{split}

(205)

\displaystyle\begin{split}y_{u_{B}}&=\frac{w+z-wz+X|z|^{2}}{\bar{w}+\bar{z}-\bar{w}\bar{z}+X|z|^{2}}\,,\\
y_{v_{B}}&=\frac{(w+z-wz)(\bar{w}+\bar{z}-\bar{w}\bar{z}+X|z|^{2})}{(\bar{w}+\bar{z}-\bar{w}\bar{z})(w+z-wz+X|z|^{2})}\,,\\
y_{w_{B}}&=\frac{(w-1)(z-1)(\bar{w}+\bar{z}-\bar{w}\bar{z})}{(\bar{w}-1)(\bar{z}-1)(w+z-wz)}\,.\end{split}

(206)

Again we find that eq. (
206
) are phases which is a result of

\sqrt{\Delta_{B}}=\pm\frac{X|z|^{2}\Big((\bar{w}+\bar{z}-\bar{w}\bar{z})-(w+z-wz)\Big)}{(1+X|z|^{2})(|w+z-wz|^{2}+X|z|^{2})}\,,

(207)

being purely imaginary. We note that as in eq. (
204
), the minimal variables rationalise this quantity.

Appendix C
The power-suppressed helicity configurations

The tree-level six gluon amplitude (
25
) describes a total of 50 helicity configurations. However, the helicity-flip impact factors
C^{g(0)}(p_{2}^{\pm},p_{3}^{\pm})
and
C^{g(0)}(p_{1}^{\pm},p_{6}^{\pm})
are power suppressed in
t_{1}/s
and
t_{3}/s
,
respectively. Of the 20 NMHV configurations described by eq. (
25
), 12 are power suppressed in this manner, owing to one or two helicity-flip impact factors, as depicted in figure
12
.
Similarly, of the 30 MHV configurations described by eq. (
25
), 22 are power suppressed due to one or two helicity-flip impact factors and are depicted in figure
13
.

Figure 12:
Six of the NMHV helicity configurations that are power suppressed in the central NMRK limit. The remaining six
power-suppressed NMHV configurations can be obtained by flipping all helicities. The labelling of the gluons follows figure
11
.

The remaining 16 helicity configurations of eq. (
25
) are all associated to amplitudes which do not have a helicity-flip impact factor, and these are the only configurations that we consider in this paper.

Figure 13:
Eleven of the MHV helicity configurations that are power suppressed in the central NMRK limit. The remaining eleven power suppressed MHV configurations can be obtained by flipping all helicities. The labelling of the gluons follows figure
11
.

Appendix D
NMHV amplitudes in general kinematics

In this appendix we list the tree-level and one-loop six-gluon amplitudes in
{\cal N}=4
SYM which are used in the main text to obtain the two-gluon opposite-helicity CEV. For these amplitudes we use the shorthand notation for spinors listed in eq. (
194
).

D.1
Tree-level six-gluon NMHV amplitudes

Here we list a representation of the tree-level six-gluon NMHV colour-ordered amplitudes obtained through the BCFW on-shell recursion relations
Britto:2004ap
;
Britto:2005fq
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}M^{(0)}\left(p_{1}^{\ominus},p_{2}^{\ominus},p_{3}^{\ominus},p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\oplus},p_{6}^{\oplus}\right)=&\ \frac{\langle 1|2+3|4]^{4}}{D_{2}^{*}}+\frac{\langle 3|4+5|6]^{4}}{D_{3}},\end{split}

(208)

\displaystyle\begin{split}M^{(0)}\left(p_{1}^{\ominus},p_{2}^{\ominus},p_{3}^{\oplus},p_{4}^{\ominus},p_{5}^{\oplus},p_{6}^{\oplus}\right)=&\ \frac{\langle 4|1+2|3]^{4}}{D_{1}^{*}}+\frac{\langle 2\,4\rangle^{4}[5\,6]^{4}}{D_{2}}+\frac{\langle 1\,2\rangle^{4}[3\,5]^{4}}{D_{3}^{*}},\end{split}

(209)

\displaystyle\begin{split}M^{(0)}\left(p_{1}^{\ominus},p_{2}^{\oplus},p_{3}^{\ominus},p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus},p_{6}^{\oplus}\right)=&\ \frac{\langle 1\,3\rangle^{4}[4\,6]^{4}}{D_{1}}+\frac{[2\,4]^{4}\langle 1\,5\rangle^{4}}{D_{2}^{*}}+\frac{[2\,6]^{4}\langle 3\,5\rangle^{4}}{D_{3}}.\end{split}

(210)

The functions in the denominators are

D_{1}(1,2,3,4,5,6)=-\langle 1\,2\rangle\langle 2\,3\rangle\left[4\,5\right]\left[5\,6\right]s_{123}\langle 1|2+3|4]\langle 3|4+5|6]\,,

(211)

and cyclic permutations thereof. We use the shorthand notation

D_{i}(\sigma)=D_{i}(\sigma_{i},\sigma_{i+1},\sigma_{i+2},\sigma_{i+3},\sigma_{i+4},\sigma_{i+5}),

(212)

where for the amplitudes in this section it is assumed – and left implicit – that the ordering
\sigma
is always taken to be that of the parent
M^{(0)}
, i.e.
\left\{1,2,3,4,5,6\right\}
.

D_{i+1}(\sigma)
is obtained from
D_{i}(\sigma)
by replacing
\sigma_{i}\to\sigma_{i+1}
in the latter, with all indices taken modulo 6. However, there are only three independent cyclic orderings due to the relation
D_{i+3}(\sigma)=D_{i}^{*}(\sigma)
.

We will see that the individual rational terms that occur in eqs. (
208
–
210
) will appear also in the one-loop amplitudes listed in the following subsection. The coefficient of the
\epsilon^{-2}
pole of these one-loop amplitudes must be proportional to the tree-level amplitude, thus, using the one-loop amplitudes, we obtain an alternative set of representations for the tree-level amplitudes:

\displaystyle M^{(0)}\left(p_{1}^{\ominus},p_{2}^{\ominus},p_{3}^{\ominus},p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\oplus},p_{6}^{\oplus}\right)=

\displaystyle\ \frac{(s_{123})^{4}}{D_{1}^{*}}+\frac{\langle 2\,3\rangle^{4}[5\,6]^{4}}{D_{2}}+\frac{\langle 1\,2\rangle^{4}[4\,5]^{4}}{D_{3}^{*}},

(213)

\displaystyle M^{(0)}\left(p_{1}^{\ominus},p_{2}^{\ominus},p_{3}^{\oplus},p_{4}^{\ominus},p_{5}^{\oplus},p_{6}^{\oplus}\right)=

\displaystyle\ \frac{\langle 1\,2\rangle^{4}[5\,6]^{4}}{D_{1}}+\frac{\langle 1|2+4|3]^{4}}{D_{2}^{*}}+\frac{\langle 4|1+2|6]^{4}}{D_{3}},

(214)

\displaystyle M^{(0)}\left(p_{1}^{\ominus},p_{2}^{\oplus},p_{3}^{\ominus},p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus},p_{6}^{\oplus}\right)=

\displaystyle\ \frac{\langle 5|1+3|2]^{4}}{D_{1}^{*}}+\frac{\langle 3|2+4|6]^{4}}{D_{2}}+\frac{\langle 1|2+6|4]^{4}}{D_{3}^{*}}.

(215)

The representations eqs. (
208
–
210
) are complementary to the representations eqs. (
213
–
215
) in the sense that where the NMRK limit of the rational terms of the former give rise directly to
R_{xy}^{I}
(defined in eqs. (
46
) and (
58
)), the rational terms of the latter give rise to
{R}_{\bar{x}\bar{y}}^{I}
(defined in eqs. (
48
) and (
60
)), and vice versa.

D.2
One-loop six-gluon NMHV amplitudes in
{\cal N}=4
SYM

We will need to study the NMRK limit of the colour-ordered amplitudes derived in ref.
Bern:1994cg
, namely:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{(4\pi)^{2}}{\kappa_{\Gamma}}M^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}\left(p_{1}^{\ominus},p_{2}^{\ominus},p_{3}^{\ominus},p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\oplus},p_{6}^{\oplus}\right)=&\left(\frac{(s_{123})^{4}}{D_{1}^{*}}\right)W_{1}\\
+&\left(\frac{\langle 1|2+3|4]^{4}}{D_{2}^{*}}+\frac{\langle 2\,3\rangle^{4}[5\,6]^{4}}{D_{2}}\right)W_{2}\\
+&\left(\frac{\langle 3|4+5|6]^{4}}{D_{3}}+\frac{\langle 1\,2\rangle^{4}[4\,5]^{4}}{D_{3}^{*}}\right)W_{3}\,,\end{split}

(216)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{(4\pi)^{2}}{\kappa_{\Gamma}}M^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}\left(p_{1}^{\ominus},p_{2}^{\ominus},p_{3}^{\oplus},p_{4}^{\ominus},p_{5}^{\oplus},p_{6}^{\oplus}\right)=&\left(\frac{\langle 4|1+2|3]^{4}}{D_{1}^{*}}+\frac{\langle 1\,2\rangle^{4}[5\,6]^{4}}{D_{1}}\right)W_{1}\\
+&\left(\frac{\langle 1|2+4|3]^{4}}{D_{2}^{*}}+\frac{\langle 2\,4\rangle^{4}[5\,6]^{4}}{D_{2}}\right)W_{2}\\
+&\left(\frac{\langle 4|1+2|6]^{4}}{D_{3}}+\frac{\langle 1\,2\rangle^{4}[3\,5]^{4}}{D_{3}^{*}}\right)W_{3}\,,\end{split}

(217)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{(4\pi)^{2}}{\kappa_{\Gamma}}M^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}\left(p_{1}^{\ominus},p_{2}^{\oplus},p_{3}^{\ominus},p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus},p_{6}^{\oplus}\right)=&\left(\frac{\langle 1\,3\rangle^{4}[4\,6]^{4}}{D_{1}}+\frac{\langle 5|1+3|2]^{4}}{D_{1}^{*}}\right)W_{1}\\
+&\left(\frac{[2\,4]^{4}\langle 1\,5\rangle^{4}}{D_{2}^{*}}+\frac{\langle 3|2+4|6]^{4}}{D_{2}}\right)W_{2}\\
+&\left(\frac{[2\,6]^{4}\langle 3\,5\rangle^{4}}{D_{3}}+\frac{\langle 1|2+6|4]^{4}}{D_{3}^{*}}\right)W_{3}\,.\end{split}

(218)

The transcendental functions are given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}W_{i}(\sigma)&=-\frac{1}{2\epsilon^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{6}\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{-t^{[2]}_{j}}\right)^{\epsilon}-\log\left(\frac{-t^{[3]}_{i}}{-t^{[2]}_{i}}\right)\log\left(\frac{-t^{[3]}_{i}}{-t^{[2]}_{i+1}}\right)-\log\left(\frac{-t^{[3]}_{i}}{-t^{[2]}_{i+3}}\right)\log\left(\frac{-t^{[3]}_{i}}{-t^{[2]}_{i+4}}\right)\\
&\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}+\log\left(\frac{-t^{[3]}_{i}}{-t^{[2]}_{i+2}}\right)\log\left(\frac{-t^{[3]}_{i}}{-t^{[2]}_{i+5}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{-t^{[2]}_{i}}{-t^{[2]}_{i+3}}\right)\log\left(\frac{-t^{[2]}_{i+1}}{-t^{[2]}_{i+4}}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{-t^{[2]}_{i+5}}{-t^{[2]}_{i}}\right)\log\left(\frac{-t^{[2]}_{i+1}}{-t^{[2]}_{i+2}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{-t^{[2]}_{i+2}}{-t^{[2]}_{i+3}}\right)\log\left(\frac{-t^{[2]}_{i+4}}{-t^{[2]}_{i+5}}\right)+\frac{\pi^{2}}{3},\end{split}

(219)

where we use the same notation for the arguments and cyclic permutations as for the function
D_{i}
, discussed below eq. (
211
). While in
eqs. (
216
–
218
) the ordering argument is left implicit, it is useful to display the ordering explicitly when the transcendental functions of different colour orderings appear in the same expression, e.g. in eq. (
131
).
We note that the function
W_{i}
has cyclic symmetry in the form
W_{i+3}=W_{i}
so there are only three independent cyclic permutations. In eq. (
219
) we use the compact notation for Mandelstam invariants introduced in eq. (
90
), and we use the prescription of eq. (
91
). We emphasise that the function
W_{i}
does not depend on the individual helicities of the particles.

Appendix E
Additional forms of the opposite-helicity central-emission vertex

In ref.
Duhr:2009uxa
an alternative form of the opposite-helicity two-gluon central-emission vertex was found by using the CSW rules
Cachazo:2004kj
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus},q_{3})\right|_{4}=&\sqrt{\frac{x_{4}}{x_{5}}}\frac{\left|q_{1\perp}\right|^{2}x_{4}p_{5\perp}^{3}}{\langle 4\,5\rangle\left(x_{5}\left|p_{4\perp}\right|^{2}+x_{4}\left|p_{5\perp}\right|^{2}\right)p_{4\perp}\left(p_{4\perp}+p_{5\perp}\right)}\\
&+\sqrt{\frac{x_{4}}{x_{5}}}\frac{q_{1\perp}^{*}x_{4}\left(q_{3\perp}+p_{5\perp}\right)^{3}}{\left(q_{3\perp}\sqrt{\frac{x_{4}}{x_{5}}}-\langle 4\,5\rangle\right)\left(x_{5}\left|p_{4\perp}\right|^{2}+x_{4}\left|q_{2\perp}\right|^{2}\right)p_{4\perp}}\\
&+\frac{q_{1\perp}q_{3\perp}\sqrt{x_{4}x_{5}}x_{5}}{\langle 4\,5\rangle\left(x_{5}p_{4\perp}-x_{4}q_{2\perp}\right)}-\sqrt{\frac{x_{4}}{x_{5}}}\frac{q_{1\perp}^{*}q_{3\perp}x_{4}}{\left(p_{4\perp}+p_{5\perp}\right)[4\,5]}\,.\end{split}

(220)

In the minimal variables of section
3.1
this is

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus},q_{3})\right|_{4}=&-\frac{\bar{w}z^{3}|1-z|^{2}X^{2}}{(w-z)(w+Xz)(|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2})}+\frac{X^{2}z^{3}(1-\bar{z})}{(1+Xz)(1+X|z|^{2})}\\
&+\frac{(1-w)(1-z)zX}{(1+X)(1+Xz)(w+Xz)}+\frac{(1-w)(1-\bar{z})\bar{w}zX^{2}}{(1+X)(w-z)(\bar{w}+X\bar{z})}\,,\end{split}

(221)

which has the expected physical singularities of eqs. (
37
) and (
38
), and also spurious poles at
P_{u_{A}}=0
and
\bar{P}_{v_{A}}=0
.

We also list a compact representation we have found

\displaystyle\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\right|_{5}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{Xz(\bar{w}-1)(Xz+w+z-1)}{(X+1)(w+Xz)}-\frac{X\bar{w}(w-1)(\bar{w}+X)z}{(\bar{w}+X\bar{z})(w-z)(X+1)}

(222)

\displaystyle+\frac{X\bar{w}(z-1)z^{2}(Xz+|w|^{2})}{(|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2})(w-z)(w+Xz)}+\frac{Xz^{2}}{X|z|^{2}+1}\,,

which has only a single spurious pole at
P_{u_{A}}=0
. We finally list a representation which is compact when expressed in terms of light-cone coordinates, which was obtained by partial fractioning the third term of eq. (
47
),

\displaystyle\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\right|_{6}

(223)

\displaystyle\qquad=x_{5}-y_{5}\,\frac{|q_{1\perp}|^{2}}{p_{4\perp}p_{5\perp}^{*}}-\frac{q_{1\perp}^{*}(q_{1\perp}-p_{4\perp})^{2}}{p_{4\perp}s_{234}}-\frac{x_{5}p_{4\perp}^{*}(q_{1\perp}-p_{4\perp})}{x_{4}s_{234}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{q_{1\perp}^{*}}{p_{4\perp}\langle 4\,5\rangle^{*}}x_{4}\sqrt{\frac{x_{4}}{x_{5}}}(y_{4}q_{1\perp}-p_{4\perp})+\frac{q_{1\perp}}{p_{5\perp}^{*}\langle 4\,5\rangle}x_{5}\sqrt{\frac{x_{5}}{x_{4}}}(y_{5}q_{1\perp}^{*}-p_{5\perp}^{*})\,.

E.1
BCFW representation for the ordering
\sigma_{A^{\prime}}

In this appendix we present the BCFW representations of the opposite-helicity vertex for the ordering
\sigma_{A^{\prime}}
. These expressions
can be obtained from the results for the ordering
\sigma_{A}
in
section
3.3
by applying the rules listed in eq. (
50
).

For the
\sigma_{A^{\prime}}=\{1,2,3,5,4,6\}
ordering we list the NMRK limits of the cross ratios,

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}u_{A^{\prime}}&=&\!\!\!\!u_{A},\\
v_{A^{\prime}}&=&{\displaystyle\frac{s_{23}s_{46}}{s_{235}s_{123}}}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&{\displaystyle\frac{|w|^{2}|z-1|^{2}X}{(1+X)(|w+z-wz|^{2}+X|z|^{2})}}\,,\\
w_{A^{\prime}}&=&{\displaystyle\frac{s_{35}s_{61}}{s_{345}s_{235}}}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&{\displaystyle\frac{|z|^{4}|w-1|^{2}X}{(|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2})(|w+z-wz|^{2}+X|z|^{2})}}\,.\end{array}

(224)

From the BCFW representations of the NMHV amplitudes of eqs. (
208
,
210
,
214
)
we obtain the opposite-helicity central-emission vertex,

\displaystyle\begin{split}A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})=R^{A^{\prime}}_{uv}+R^{A^{\prime}}_{vw}+R^{A^{\prime}}_{wu}\,,\end{split}

(225)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{A^{\prime}}_{uv}&=\frac{|w-1|^{2}\bar{w}X^{2}|z|^{2}(\bar{z}-1)}{(\bar{w}+X\bar{z})(X+1)(w-z)(\bar{w}+\bar{z}-\bar{w}\bar{z}+X\bar{z})}\,,\\
R^{A^{\prime}}_{vw}&=\frac{wz(w-1)(z-1)(\bar{w}+\bar{z}-\bar{w}\bar{z})^{4}}{\bar{z}(|w+z-wz|^{2}+X|z|^{2})(\bar{w}+\bar{z}-\bar{w}\bar{z}+X\bar{z})(w(\bar{w}+\bar{z}-\bar{w}\bar{z})+X|z|^{2})}\,,\\
R^{A^{\prime}}_{wu}&=-\frac{(\bar{w}-1)|w|^{2}X^{2}|z-1|^{2}z^{2}|z|^{2}}{(w+Xz)(|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2})(w(\bar{w}+\bar{z}-\bar{w}\bar{z})+X|z|^{2})(w-z)}\,.\end{split}

(226)

Instead starting from the BCFW representations of eqs. (
209
,
213
,
215
), we obtain the representation,

\displaystyle\begin{split}A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})=R^{A^{\prime}}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}+R^{A^{\prime}}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}+R^{A^{\prime}}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}\,,\end{split}

(227)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{A^{\prime}}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}&=\frac{|w-1|^{2}w(z-1)|z|^{2}}{(X+1)(\bar{w}-\bar{z})(w+Xz)(w+z-wz+Xz)}\,,\\
R^{A^{\prime}}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}&=\frac{\bar{w}(\bar{w}-1)(\bar{z}-1)z^{2}|z|^{2}X^{2}}{(|w+z-wz|^{2}+X|z|^{2})(w+z-wz+Xz)(\bar{w}(w+z-wz)+X|z|^{2})}\,,\\
R^{A^{\prime}}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}&=-\frac{(w-1)|w|^{2}\bar{w}^{4}|z-1|^{2}z}{\bar{z}(|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2})(\bar{w}+X\bar{z})(\bar{w}(w+z-wz)+X|z|^{2})(\bar{w}-\bar{z})}\,.\end{split}

(228)

The physical-singularity surfaces of this ordering are

\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}\langle 4\,5\rangle=0&\leftrightarrow&p_{4\perp}\sqrt{\displaystyle\frac{p_{4}^{+}}{p_{5}^{+}}}-p_{5\perp}\sqrt{\displaystyle\frac{p_{5}^{+}}{p_{4}^{+}}}=0&\leftrightarrow&w+Xz=0\,,\\
s_{123}=0&\leftrightarrow&p_{4}^{+}+p_{5}^{+}=0&\leftrightarrow&1+X=0\,,\\
s_{235}=0&\leftrightarrow&|q_{2\perp}^{\prime}|^{2}+p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-}=0&\leftrightarrow&|w+z-wz|^{2}+X|z|^{2}=0\,,\\
s_{354}=0&\leftrightarrow&p_{4}^{-}+p_{5}^{-}=0&\leftrightarrow&|w|^{2}+X|z|^{2}=0\,,\end{array}

(229)

while the unphysical-singularity surfaces are

\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}\langle 1|2+3|5]=0&\leftrightarrow&P^{A^{\prime}}_{v}=0&\leftrightarrow&q^{\prime\ast}_{2\perp}-p_{5\perp}^{*}{\displaystyle\frac{p_{4}^{+}}{p_{5}^{+}}}=0&\leftrightarrow&\bar{w}+\bar{z}-\bar{w}\bar{z}+X\bar{z}=0\,,\\
\langle 3|5+4|6]=0&\leftrightarrow&P^{A^{\prime}}_{u}=0&\leftrightarrow&p_{4\perp}+p_{5\perp}=0&\leftrightarrow&w-z=0\,,\\
\langle 4|6+1|2]=0&\leftrightarrow&P^{A^{\prime}}_{w}=0&\leftrightarrow&q^{\prime\ast}_{2\perp}+p_{5\perp}^{*}{\displaystyle\frac{p_{5\perp}}{p_{4\perp}}\frac{p_{4}^{+}}{p_{5}^{+}}}=0&\leftrightarrow&w(\bar{w}+\bar{z}-\bar{w}\bar{z})+X|z|^{2}=0\,.\\
\end{array}

(230)

We note that

\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{A}_{xy}\ \ {\underset{p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}}{\leftrightarrow}}\ \ \left(R^{A^{\prime}}_{\bar{x}\bar{y}}\right)^{*}\,.\end{split}

(231)

Appendix F
Absorptive part of the one-loop six-gluon amplitude in NMRK

In eq. (
83
) we decomposed the
{\cal N}=4
SYM one-loop six-gluon amplitude in NMRK into a basis of symmetric
(+)
or antisymmetric
(-)
representations exchanged in the
t_{1}
and
t_{3}
channels. For one-loop amplitudes where an adjoint representation is circulating in the loop, the
(-,-)
component is given by eq. (
84
). We list here analogous expressions for the remaining components:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}^{(1)(+,+)}=\frac{g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}}{4}\\
&\times\bigg\{\mbox{tr\,}\left(\left\{F^{a_{2}},F^{a_{3}}\right\}F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}}\left\{F^{a_{6}},F^{a_{1}}\right\}\right)\left(M^{(1)}(\sigma_{A})+M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})+M^{(1)}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})+M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}}})\right)\\
&+\mbox{tr\,}\left(\left\{F^{a_{2}},F^{a_{3}}\right\}F^{a_{5}}F^{a_{4}}\left\{F^{a_{6}},F^{a_{1}}\right\}\right)\left(M^{(1)}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})+M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A^{\prime}}})+M^{(1)}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A^{\prime}}})+M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A^{\prime}}}})\right)\\
&+\mbox{tr\,}\left(\left\{F^{a_{2}},F^{a_{3}}\right\}F^{a_{4}}\left\{F^{a_{6}},F^{a_{1}}\right\}F^{a_{5}}\right)\left(M^{(1)}(\sigma_{B})+M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})+M^{(1)}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})+M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}}})\right)\bigg\}.\end{split}

(232)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}^{(1)(+,-)}=\frac{g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}}{4}\\
&\times\bigg\{\mbox{tr\,}\left(\left\{F^{a_{2}},F^{a_{3}}\right\}F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}}\left[F^{a_{6}},F^{a_{1}}\right]\right)\left(M^{(1)}(\sigma_{A})+M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})-M^{(1)}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})-M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}}})\right)\\
&+\mbox{tr\,}\left(\left\{F^{a_{2}},F^{a_{3}}\right\}F^{a_{5}}F^{a_{4}}\left[F^{a_{6}},F^{a_{1}}\right]\right)\left(M^{(1)}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})+M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A^{\prime}}})-M^{(1)}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A^{\prime}}})-M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A^{\prime}}}})\right)\\
&+\mbox{tr\,}\left(\left\{F^{a_{2}},F^{a_{3}}\right\}F^{a_{4}}\left[F^{a_{6}},F^{a_{1}}\right]F^{a_{5}}\right)\left(M^{(1)}(\sigma_{B})+M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})-M^{(1)}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})-M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}}})\right)\bigg\}.\end{split}

(233)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}^{(1)(-,+)}=\frac{g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}}{4}\\
&\times\bigg\{\mbox{tr\,}\left(\left[F^{a_{2}},F^{a_{3}}\right]F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}}\left\{F^{a_{6}},F^{a_{1}}\right\}\right)\left(M^{(1)}(\sigma_{A})-M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})+M^{(1)}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})-M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}}})\right)\\
&+\mbox{tr\,}\left(\left[F^{a_{2}},F^{a_{3}}\right]F^{a_{5}}F^{a_{4}}\left\{F^{a_{6}},F^{a_{1}}\right\}\right)\left(M^{(1)}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})-M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A^{\prime}}})+M^{(1)}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A^{\prime}}})-M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A^{\prime}}}})\right)\\
&+\mbox{tr\,}\left(\left[F^{a_{2}},F^{a_{3}}\right]F^{a_{4}}\left\{F^{a_{6}},F^{a_{1}}\right\}F^{a_{5}}\right)\left(M^{(1)}(\sigma_{B})-M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})+M^{(1)}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})-M^{(1)}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}}})\right)\bigg\}.\end{split}

(234)

In section
4
we found that in the central NMRK, the dispersive amplitude received a contribution from the
(-,-)
component alone. The same is not true for the absorptive part of the amplitude, which is the object of study of this appendix. To this end we list the NMRK limit of the imaginary parts of the transcendental functions eq. (
89
) and eq. (
219
), for the 12 colour orderings that appear in eqs. (
232
)–(
234
). We begin by giving the results for the
\sigma_{A}
orderings:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\rm Im}\Big[V_{6}(\sigma_{A})\Big]=2{\rm Im}\Big[W_{2}(\sigma_{A})\Big]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&-\frac{4\pi}{\epsilon}+\pi\log\left(\frac{t_{1}p_{4}^{-}s_{45}p_{5}^{+}t_{3}}{(\mu^{2})^{4}}\right)\,,\\
{\rm Im}\Big[V_{6}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})\Big]=2{\rm Im}\Big[W_{2}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})\Big]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&-\frac{2\pi}{\epsilon}+\pi\log\left(\frac{p_{4}^{-}s_{45}p_{5}^{+}t_{3}}{t_{1}(\mu^{2})^{2}}\right)\,,\\
{\rm Im}\Big[V_{6}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})\Big]=2{\rm Im}\Big[W_{2}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})\Big]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&-\frac{2\pi}{\epsilon}+\pi\log\left(\frac{t_{1}p_{4}^{-}s_{45}p_{5}^{+}}{t_{3}(\mu^{2})^{2}}\right)\,,\\
{\rm Im}\Big[V_{6}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}}})\Big]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&-\frac{2\pi}{\epsilon}+\pi\log\left(\frac{t_{1}p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+}t_{3}}{s_{45}(\mu^{2})^{2}}\right)+2\pi\log\left(\frac{u_{A}}{1-u_{A}}\right)\,,\\
2{\rm Im}\Big[W_{2}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}}})\Big]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&-\frac{2\pi}{\epsilon}+\pi\log\left(\frac{t_{1}p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+}t_{3}}{s_{45}(\mu^{2})^{2}}\right)-2\pi\log\left(\frac{v_{A}w_{A}}{u_{A}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(235)

We provide the results for
W_{1}
and
W_{3}
as differences from those for
W_{2}
. Interestingly,
\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}}}
is the only ordering which leads to differing imaginary parts for different cyclic permutations of
W_{i}
:

\begin{array}[]{cclcccl}{\rm Im}\Big[W_{1}(\sigma_{A})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A})\Big]&=&0\,,&&{\rm Im}\Big[W_{3}(\sigma_{A})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A})\Big]&=&0\,,\\
{\rm Im}\Big[W_{1}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})-W_{2}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})\Big]&=&0\,,&&{\rm Im}\Big[W_{3}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})-W_{2}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})\Big]&=&0\,,\\
{\rm Im}\Big[W_{1}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})-W_{2}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})\Big]&=&0\,,&&{\rm Im}\Big[W_{3}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})-W_{2}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}})\Big]&=&0\,,\\
{\rm Im}\Big[W_{1}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}}})-W_{2}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}}})\Big]&=&2\pi\log(v_{A})\,,&&{\rm Im}\Big[W_{3}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}}})-W_{2}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}}})\Big]&=&2\pi\log(w_{A})\,.\\
\end{array}

(236)

We further note that only for the ordering
\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{A}}}
does the analytic continuation of the dilogarithms in eq. (
89
) to our physical region produce an imaginary part. For this colour ordering, the cross ratio

\frac{s_{63}s_{45}}{s_{632}s_{245}},

which is related to
u_{A}
by interchange of
p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{6}
and
p_{2}\leftrightarrow p_{3}
, has two positive invariants in the numerator and two negative invariants in the denominator. The analytic continuation of the dilogarithm that depends on this cross ratio leads to factor of
i\pi\log(1-u_{A})
which is related to the factorisation breaking term discovered in ref.
Bartels:2008ce
. The interpretation of this quantity is different in our paper; ref.
Bartels:2008ce
considers the single planar colour ordering of the amplitude, analytically continued to the physical region where
s_{12},s_{45}>0
,
s_{23},s_{34},s_{56},s_{61},s_{234},s_{345}<0
,
while we consider a certain colour-ordered amplitude in the physical region where
s_{12},s_{34},s_{45},s_{56},s_{345}>0
,
s_{23},s_{61},s_{234}<0
. However, the consequence is the same, namely that this term is not compatible with Regge-pole factorisation of the amplitude, which only applies to its dispersive part. Instead it contributes to a Regge cut.

Expressions for the
\sigma_{A^{\prime}}
orderings are related by an interchange of
p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}
.

For the
\sigma_{B}
orderings we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\rm Im}\Big[V_{6}(\sigma_{B})\Big]=2{\rm Im}\Big[W_{1}(\sigma_{B})\Big]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&-\frac{2\pi}{\epsilon}+\pi\log\left(\frac{t_{1}p_{5}^{+}p_{5}^{-}t_{3}}{p_{4}^{+}p_{4}^{-}(\mu^{2})^{2}}\right)\,,\\
{\rm Im}\Big[V_{6}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})\Big]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&-\frac{2\pi}{\epsilon}+\pi\log\left(\frac{t_{1}p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-}t_{3}}{p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+}(\mu^{2})^{2}}\right)+2\pi\log\left(\frac{v_{B}}{1-v_{B}}\right)\,,\\
2{\rm Im}\Big[W_{1}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})\Big]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&-\frac{2\pi}{\epsilon}+\pi\log\left(\frac{t_{1}p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-}t_{3}}{p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+}(\mu^{2})^{2}}\right)+2\pi\log\left(\frac{u_{B}v_{B}}{w_{B}}\right)\,,\\
{\rm Im}\Big[V_{6}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})\Big]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&-\frac{2\pi}{\epsilon}+\pi\log\left(\frac{t_{1}p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+}t_{3}}{p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-}(\mu^{2})^{2}}\right)+2\pi\log\left(\frac{u_{B}}{1-u_{B}}\right)\,,\\
2{\rm Im}\Big[W_{1}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})\Big]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&-\frac{2\pi}{\epsilon}+\pi\log\left(\frac{t_{1}p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+}t_{3}}{p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-}(\mu^{2})^{2}}\right)+2\pi\log\left(\frac{u_{B}v_{B}}{w_{B}}\right)\,,\\
{\rm Im}\Big[V_{6}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}}})\Big]=2{\rm Im}\Big[W_{1}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}}})\Big]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK}]{}&-\frac{2\pi}{\epsilon}+\pi\log\left(\frac{t_{1}p_{4}^{+}p_{4}^{-}t_{3}}{p_{5}^{+}p_{5}^{-}(\mu^{2})^{2}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(237)

Again the orderings that lead to imaginary contributions from the dilogarithms (namely
\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}}
,
\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}}
) are the same orderings for which the imaginary parts of the distinct cyclic permutations of
W_{i}
differ:

\begin{array}[]{cclcccl}{\rm Im}\Big[W_{2}(\sigma_{B})-W_{1}(\sigma_{B})\Big]&=&0\,,&&{\rm Im}\Big[W_{3}(\sigma_{B})-W_{1}(\sigma_{B})\Big]&=&0\,,\\
{\rm Im}\Big[W_{2}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})-W_{1}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})\Big]&=&2\pi\log\left(\frac{w_{B}}{u_{B}}\right)\,,&&{\rm Im}\Big[W_{3}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})-W_{1}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})\Big]&=&-2\pi\log\left(u_{B}\right)\,,\\
{\rm Im}\Big[W_{2}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})-W_{1}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})\Big]&=&-2\pi\log\left(v_{B}\right)\,,&&{\rm Im}\Big[W_{3}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})-W_{1}(\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}})\Big]&=&2\pi\log\left(\frac{w_{B}}{v_{B}}\right)\,,\\
{\rm Im}\Big[W_{2}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}}})-W_{1}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}}})\Big]&=&0\,,&&{\rm Im}\Big[W_{3}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}}})-W_{1}(\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\underset{\leftrightarrow}{\sigma_{B}}})\Big]&=&0\,.\\
\end{array}

(238)

Absorptive part of the same-helicity amplitude

\nu_{4}=\nu_{5}=\oplus
:

In section
4.3
we show that the absorptive parts listed in eqs. (

–

) do not contribute to the NLO
gg\to gggg
squared matrix element. These terms will only contribute to the squared matrix element at higher orders in the coupling.

In section

we show that at the level of the NLO
