Appendix G
Kinematic limits of the two-gluon central-emission vertex

Starting from the central NMRK region, it is possible to take further kinematic limits to regions which are contained within NMRK. In these more restricted kinematic regions further simplifications occur. A key example are the regions where we additionally require
p_{4}^{+}\gg p_{5}^{+}
or
p_{5}^{+}\gg p_{4}^{+}
which we refer to as the MRK and
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limits respectively. These limits of the two-gluon CEV are studied in appendix
G.1
. From the central NMRK region we may alternatively take the limit where one of the central gluons becomes soft, which is the subject of appendix
G.2
, or the limit where the two central gluons become collinear to each other, which is the subject of appendix
G.3
. These further limits act as checks on the results presented in this paper, and also provide intuition for the kinematic dependence of the cross ratios and the two-gluon CEV. One may also investigate the limits where both of the central gluons become soft, but this is not pursued in this appendix.

G.1
MRK limit

Recall that the MRK (MRK
′
) limit is obtained by taking
X\to\infty
(
X\to 0
).
In the MRK or MRK
′
limit, the tree-level two-gluon CEV factorises into a product of Lipatov vertices times a
t
-channel pole. This factorization property applies for any helicity configuration, for example, for opposite helicities

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\right|_{\rm MRK}&=V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{2})\frac{1}{t_{2}}V^{g(0)}(q_{2},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\\
&=\frac{z}{\bar{z}}(\bar{w}-1)(\bar{z}-1),\\
\end{split}

(247)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})\right|_{{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}}&=V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},q^{\prime}_{2})\frac{1}{t^{\prime}_{2}}V^{g(0)}(q^{\prime}_{2},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})\\
&=\frac{z}{\bar{z}}\frac{(\bar{w}(\bar{z}-1)-\bar{z})}{(w(z-1)-z)}(w-1)(z-1)\,,\end{split}

(248)

with Lipatov vertex
V^{g(0)}
in eq. (
9
).
Considering the opposite helicity configuration at one loop, we saw in section
4.2
that different transcendental terms multiply rational terms which are not simply the tree amplitudes. This prompts an analysis of these individual rational terms in the MRK and
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limits.
As a consistency check on the MRK and
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limits of these individual rational terms, the relevant sums of these rational terms given in eq. (
45
), eq. (
47
), eq. (
225
) and eq. (
227
) should reproduce the limits eq. (
247
) and eq. (
248
).

For the
\sigma_{A}
ordering in the MRK, from eqs. (
46
) and (
48
) we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{A}_{uv}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}\frac{w-1}{w-z}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\right|_{\rm MRK}\\
R_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}^{A}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\right|_{\rm MRK}\\
R_{wu}^{A}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}-\frac{z-1}{w-z}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\right|_{\rm MRK}\\
\end{split}

(249)

while
R_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}^{A}
,
R_{vw}^{A}
and
R_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}^{A}
vanish in this limit. All six of these rational terms vanish in the
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limit. The limits listed in eq. (
249
) can be used to show eq. (
247
), starting from either of the representations eq. (
45
) or eq. (
47
). On the other hand, for the
\sigma_{A^{\prime}}
permutation, all rational terms vanish in MRK. In
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
,
R^{A^{\prime}}_{uv}
,
R_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}^{A^{\prime}}
and
R_{wu}^{A^{\prime}}
vanish, while the remaining terms tend to

\displaystyle\begin{split}R_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}^{A^{\prime}}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}-\frac{(\bar{w}-1)\bar{z}^{2}}{(\bar{w}-\bar{z})(\bar{w}(\bar{z}-1)-\bar{z})}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})\right|_{{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}}\\
R_{vw}^{A^{\prime}}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})\right|_{{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}}\\
R_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}^{A^{\prime}}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}\frac{\bar{w}^{2}(\bar{z}-1)}{(\bar{w}-\bar{z})(\bar{w}(\bar{z}-1)-\bar{z})}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})\right|_{{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

(250)

Using the representation eq. (
225
) or eq. (
227
) we see that these limits are consistent with the limit of the tree-level amplitude eq. (
248
).
Finally for the
B
configurations, we get terms that survive in the MRK limit,

\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{B}_{uv}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}-\frac{(w-1)(z-1)}{w(z-1)-z}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\right|_{\rm MRK}\\
R_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}^{B}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}0\\
R_{vw}^{B}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}0\\
R_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}^{B}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}\left.-A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\right|_{\rm MRK}\\
R_{wu}^{B}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}\frac{1}{w(z-1)-z}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5},q_{3})\right|_{\rm MRK}\\
R_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}^{B}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}0,\end{split}

(251)

and also terms that survive in the
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limit,

\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{B}_{uv}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}-\frac{(\bar{w}-1)(\bar{z}-1)}{\bar{w}(\bar{z}-1)-\bar{z}}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})\right|_{{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}}\\
R_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}^{B}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}0\\
R_{vw}^{B}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}\frac{1}{\bar{w}(\bar{z}-1)-\bar{z}}\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})\right|_{{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}}\\
R_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}^{B}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}0\\
R_{wu}^{B}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}0\\
R_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}^{B}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}-\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\ominus}_{5},p^{\oplus}_{4},q_{3})\right|_{{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

(252)

The MRK and
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limits of the one-loop two gluon CEV in
{\cal N}=4
SYM are discussed in section
4
. The notation introduced in that section makes it trivial to obtain the MRK and
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limits of the transcendental terms, with the exception of terms that can be compactly described in terms of the cross ratios of eqs. (
42
), (
224
) and (
56
). To facilitate the investigation of these latter transcendental functions, we list the cross ratios in the MRK and
{\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}}
limits:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}u_{A}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}1,&v_{A}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}\frac{(-t_{1})p_{5}^{+}}{(-t_{2})p_{4}^{+}},&w_{A}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}\frac{(-t_{3})p_{4}^{-}}{(-t_{2})p_{5}^{-}},\\
u_{A^{\prime}}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}1,&v_{A^{\prime}}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}\frac{(-t_{1})p_{4}^{+}}{(-t_{2}^{\prime})p_{5}^{+}},&w_{A^{\prime}}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}\frac{(-t_{1})p_{5}^{-}}{(-t_{2}^{\prime})p_{4}^{-}},\\
u_{B}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}1,&v_{B}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}\frac{p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+}}{(-t_{2})},&w_{B}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}]{}\frac{(-t_{1})(-t_{3})}{(-t_{2})p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-}},\\
u_{B}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}\frac{p_{4}^{+}p_{5}^{-}}{(-t_{2}^{\prime})},&v_{B}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}1,&w_{B}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{MRK}^{\prime}]{}\frac{(-t_{1})(-t_{3})}{(-t_{2}^{\prime})p_{4}^{-}p_{5}^{+}}.\\
\end{array}

(253)

G.2
Soft limits

In the limit that either of the gluons of the tree two-gluon CEV become soft,
\displaystyle p_{4}\to 0
or
\displaystyle p_{5}\to 0
,
the amplitude given in eq. (
26
) is singular. It factorises into an eikonal factor, which behaves like
\displaystyle 1/p_{4}
or
\displaystyle 1/p_{5}
,
and a five-gluon amplitude in MRK. For example, taking the limit where all components of
\displaystyle p_{4}
tend to zero at the same rate, eq. (
26
) factorises as

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left.M^{(0)}_{6g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}})\right|_{\mathrm{NMRK}}

(254)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left[\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}(p_{3},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p_{5})\right]_{\mathrm{NMRK}}\,\left[M^{(0)}_{5g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}})\right]_{\mathrm{MRK}}\,,

where the five-gluon amplitude in MRK is

\left.M^{(0)}_{5g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}})\right|_{\mathrm{MRK}}=s\,C^{g(0)}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}})\,\frac{1}{t_{1}}\,V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})\,\frac{1}{t_{3}}\,C^{g(0)}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}})\,,

(255)

with
\displaystyle q_{3}=q_{1}-p_{5}
and
\displaystyle V^{g(0)}
given in eq. (
9
).
In eq. (
254
), the positive-helicity soft factor, or eikonal factor is

\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}(p_{3},p^{\oplus}_{4},p_{5})=\frac{\langle 3\,5\rangle}{\langle 3\,4\rangle\langle 4\,5\rangle}\,.

(256)

Note that this eikonal factor does not depend on the helicities of particles 3 and 5, and in fact not even on their parton flavour. The negative-helicity eikonal factor is obtained by complex conjugation. In the NMRK limit the positive-helicity eikonal factor becomes

\left.\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}(p_{3},p^{\oplus}_{4},p_{5})\right|_{\mathrm{NMRK}}=\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}_{A}(p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5})=\frac{p_{5\perp}}{p_{4\perp}}\sqrt{\frac{p_{4}^{+}}{p_{5}^{+}}}\,\frac{1}{\langle 4\,5\rangle}\,,

(257)

where in the first equality we have introduced a notation which makes it clear that this function no longer depends on
\displaystyle p_{3}
once we have taken the NMRK limit. Using this notation, eq. (
254
) implies that the two-gluon CEV factorises as

A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}_{A}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5})\,V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})\,.

(258)

For the equal-helicity vertex eq. (
27
), which comes from a MHV amplitude, this limit is straightforward to verify. In fact, for such an MHV amplitude, the relation at the amplitude level, eq. (
254
), holds
exactly in general kinematics without even requiring that
\displaystyle p_{4}\to 0
.
For the opposite-helicity vertex, in order to check eq. (
258
) we have found it most convenient to use the representation in eq. (
45
).

As a step towards obtaining the soft limit of the one-loop amplitude, we first consider the rational coefficients. Specifically, for the limit where all components of
\displaystyle p_{4}
tend to zero at the same rate, the minimal variables of eq. (
33
) and eq. (
34
) satisfy

\displaystyle\displaystyle X\to 0,\hskip 22.99988ptz\to-\frac{q_{1\perp}}{p_{4\perp}},\hskip 22.99988ptw\to\frac{q_{1\perp}}{p_{5\perp}},\hskip 22.99988ptXz\to\ {\rm finite}.

(259)

In this limit
\displaystyle R^{A}_{uv}
,
\displaystyle R^{A}_{vw}
and
\displaystyle R^{A}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}
tend to zero. The others are given by

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}&R^{A}_{wu}\to\frac{Xz^{2}(\bar{w}-1)}{w+Xz},\\
&R^{A}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}\to-\frac{Xz^{2}|w-1|^{2}}{(1+Xz)(w+Xz)},\\
\hskip 22.99988pt&R^{A}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}\to\frac{Xz^{2}(\bar{w}-1)}{1+Xz}.\end{split}

(260)

Therefore
\displaystyle R^{A}_{uv}+R^{A}_{vw}+R^{A}_{wu}
and
\displaystyle R^{A}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}+R^{A}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}+R^{A}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}
tend to the same limit (that of eq. (
258
)). The results for the
\displaystyle A^{\prime}
ordering are no longer related to these by
\displaystyle p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}
because the specific choice of taking
\displaystyle p_{4}\to 0
breaks the symmetry. We therefore list them here. The rational coefficients
\displaystyle R^{A^{\prime}}_{uv}
,
\displaystyle R^{A^{\prime}}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}
and
\displaystyle R^{A^{\prime}}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}
tend to zero. The remaining non-zero coefficients are

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}&R^{A^{\prime}}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}\to-\frac{wz(1-\bar{w})}{w+Xz},\\
&R^{A^{\prime}}_{vw}\to\frac{wz(1-\bar{w})^{2}}{w\bar{w}-w-Xz},\\
&R^{A^{\prime}}_{wu}\to\frac{w\bar{w}Xz^{2}(1-\bar{w})}{(w+Xz)(w\bar{w}-w-Xz)},\end{split}

(261)

such that again
\displaystyle R^{A^{\prime}}_{uv}+R^{A^{\prime}}_{vw}+R^{A^{\prime}}_{wu}
and
\displaystyle R^{A^{\prime}}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}+R^{A^{\prime}}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}+R^{A^{\prime}}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}
give the expected tree-level limit:

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{wz(1-\bar{w})}{w+Xz}=\ \mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}_{A^{\prime}}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5})\,V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p_{5}^{\ominus},q_{3}),

(262)

where, analogous to eq. (
257
), we have introduced the notation

\left.\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}(p_{5},p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{6})\right|_{\mathrm{NMRK}}=\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}_{A^{\prime}}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5})=-\sqrt{\frac{p_{5}^{+}}{p_{4}^{+}}}\,\frac{1}{\langle 4\,5\rangle}\,.

(263)

Finally, for the
\displaystyle B
colour ordering
\displaystyle R^{B}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}
,
\displaystyle R^{B}_{vw}
and
\displaystyle R^{B}_{wu}
tend to zero, leaving

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}&R^{B}_{uv}\to-(\bar{w}-1)z,\\
&R^{B}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}\to-\frac{Xz^{2}(\bar{w}-1)}{1+Xz-\bar{w}},\\
&R^{B}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}\to-\frac{z(\bar{w}-1)^{2}}{\bar{w}-Xz-1}.\end{split}

(264)

These then lead to the expected tree-level result where
\displaystyle R^{B}_{uv}+R^{B}_{vw}+R^{B}_{wu}
and
\displaystyle R^{B}_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}+R^{B}_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}+R^{B}_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}
both sum to

\displaystyle\displaystyle-(\bar{w}-1)z=\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}_{B}(p_{4})\,V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p_{5}^{\ominus},q_{3}),

(265)

where as for the other colour orderings we have introduced

\left.\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}(p_{1},p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{2})\right|_{\mathrm{NMRK}}=\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}_{B}(p_{4})=\frac{1}{p_{4\perp}},

(266)

which does not even depend on the helicity of the soft particle. The three relevant eikonal functions in the NMRK obey

\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}_{A}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5})+\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}_{A^{\prime}}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5})+\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}_{B}(p_{4})=0,

(267)

consistent with the more general eq. (
54
), and the eikonal identity.

We now turn to the colour-ordered amplitudes at one-loop. Beginning with the same-helicity vertex, taking the limit
\displaystyle p_{4}\to 0
in eqs. (
97
), (
103
) and (
106
), we find

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{\left.V_{6}(\sigma_{A})\right|_{\rm NMRK}}\right]}\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}\,\,&E(t_{1},s_{35};t_{3},s_{56})+v^{g(1)}(t_{1},|p_{5\perp}|^{2},t_{3})+N_{c}\,s^{(1)}_{A}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5})\\
N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{\left.V_{6}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})\right|_{\rm NMRK}}\right]}\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}\,\,&E(t_{1},s_{35};t_{3},s_{56})+v^{g(1)}(t_{1},|p_{5\perp}|^{2},t_{3})+\,N_{c}\,s^{(1)}_{A^{\prime}}(p_{4},p_{5}),\\
N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{\left.V_{6}(\sigma_{B})\right|_{\rm NMRK}}\right]}\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}\,\,&E(t_{1},s_{35};t_{3},s_{56})+v^{g(1)}(t_{1},|p_{5\perp}|^{2},t_{3})+N_{c}\,s^{(1)}_{B}(p_{4}),\end{split}

(268)

where
\displaystyle E(t_{1},s_{35};t_{3},s_{56})
is the natural collection of impact factors and Regge trajectories in the absence of
\displaystyle p_{4}
and
\displaystyle v^{g(1)}
is the one-loop Lipatov vertex correction given in eq. (
86
). The one-loop soft factor
Bern:1998sc
is

\displaystyle\displaystyle s^{(1)}(p_{3},p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5})=-\kappa_{\Gamma}\left(\frac{\mu^{2}(-s_{35})}{(-s_{34})(-s_{45})}\right)^{\epsilon}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2}),

(269)

which must first be analytically continued to our physical region, discussed in section
A.1
, and then taken in the NMRK limit. In eq. (
268
) we have used the notation

\displaystyle\displaystyle s^{(1)}_{A}(p_{4},p_{5})

\displaystyle\displaystyle={\mathrm{Re}\left[{s^{(1)}(p_{3},p_{4},p_{5})\Big|_{\mathrm{NMRK}}}\right]}\,,

(270)

\displaystyle\displaystyle s^{(1)}_{A^{\prime}}(p_{4},p_{5})

\displaystyle\displaystyle={\mathrm{Re}\left[{s^{(1)}(p_{4},p_{5},p_{6})\Big|_{\mathrm{NMRK}}}\right]}\,,

(271)

\displaystyle\displaystyle s^{(1)}_{B}(p_{4})

\displaystyle\displaystyle={\mathrm{Re}\left[{s^{(1)}(p_{1},p_{4},p_{2})\Big|_{\mathrm{NMRK}}}\right]}\,,

(272)

which, as for the analogous tree-level functions, more clearly indicate the simpler kinematic dependence of these one-loop soft functions in the NMRK limit.

Eq. (
268
) exactly matches the expected sum of the one-loop corrections to the non-central pieces, the Lipatov vertex for the single emission of
\displaystyle p_{5}
and the one-loop soft function. From eq. (
94
), these are multiplied by the soft limit of the corresponding tree-level amplitude for that ordering.

Turning now to the opposite-helicity case in eq. (
132
), and starting with the
\displaystyle A
colour ordering, we find

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{wu}^{A}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}_{A}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5})V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p_{5}^{\ominus},q_{3}),\\
{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{1}(\sigma_{A})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A})}\right]}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}0,\\
{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{3}(\sigma_{A})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A})}\right]}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}\log\left(\frac{s_{34}t_{3}}{s_{35}t_{1}}\right)\log\left(\frac{|p_{5\perp}|^{2}}{s_{45}}\right).\end{split}

(273)

This allows us to extract a common factor of the tree amplitude multiplying

{\mathrm{Re}\left[{2W_{2}(\sigma_{A})+(W_{3}(\sigma_{A})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A}))}\right]}.

Using eqs. (
139
) and (
140
), we find

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left.{\mathrm{Re}\left[{2W_{2}(\sigma_{A})+(W_{3}(\sigma_{A})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A}))}\right]}\right|_{\rm NMRK}\\
=&\left.{\mathrm{Re}\left[{V_{6}(\sigma_{A})-\Delta V_{A}(u_{A},v_{A},w_{A})+\Delta W_{A}(u_{A},v_{A},w_{A})+(W_{3}(\sigma_{A})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A}))}\right]}\right|_{\rm NMRK}\\
\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}&\left.{\mathrm{Re}\left[{V_{6}(\sigma_{A})}\right]}\right|_{{\rm NMRK},\,p_{4}\to 0},\end{split}

(274)

where the cancellation in the last line arises from the soft limit of eqs. (
101
), (
141
) and (
273
). Eq. (
268
) then immediately gives the expected one-loop structure which equals that found for the same-helicity vertex. The helicity-dependence in the soft limit is contained only in the tree-level factors.

Similarly for the
\displaystyle A^{\prime}
-ordering we find

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{uv}^{A^{\prime}}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}_{A^{\prime}}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5})V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p_{5}^{\ominus},q_{3}),\\
\left.{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{1}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})}\right]}\right|_{\rm NMRK}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}\log\left(\frac{s_{46}t_{1}}{s_{56}t_{3}}\right)\log\left(\frac{|p_{5\perp}|^{2}}{s_{45}}\right),\\
\left.{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{3}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})}\right]}\right|_{\rm NMRK}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}0.\end{split}

(275)

Extracting a common coefficient of the tree amplitude leaves

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left.{\mathrm{Re}\left[{2W_{2}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})+(W_{1}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})-W_{2}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}}))}\right]}\right|_{\rm NMRK}\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}\left.{\mathrm{Re}\left[{V_{6}(\sigma_{A^{\prime}})}\right]}\right|_{{\rm NMRK},\,p_{4}\to 0},

(276)

as expected. Finally for the
\displaystyle B
ordering,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{vw}^{B}+S_{wu}^{B}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}\mathrm{Soft}^{(0)}_{B}(p_{4})V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p_{5}^{\ominus},q_{3}),\\
\left.{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{2}(\sigma_{B})-W_{1}(\sigma_{B})}\right]}\right|_{\rm NMRK}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}\log\left(\frac{s_{24}s_{56}}{s_{12}t_{1}}\right)\log\left(\frac{s_{14}s_{35}}{s_{12}t_{3}}\right),\\
\left.{\mathrm{Re}\left[{W_{3}(\sigma_{B})-W_{1}(\sigma_{B})}\right]}\right|_{\rm NMRK}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}\log\left(\frac{s_{24}s_{56}}{s_{12}t_{1}}\right)\log\left(\frac{s_{14}s_{35}}{s_{12}t_{3}}\right).\end{split}

(277)

As the final two limits are equal, one again factors out the tree amplitude leaving

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left.{\mathrm{Re}\left[{2W_{1}(\sigma_{B})+(W_{2}(\sigma_{B})-W_{1}(\sigma_{B}))}\right]}\right|_{\rm NMRK}\xrightarrow[p_{4}\to 0]{}\left.{\mathrm{Re}\left[{V_{6}(\sigma_{B})}\right]}\right|_{\mathrm{NMRK},\,p_{4}\to 0}.

(278)

Finally, performing the change of basis of colour structures in eq. (
113
), we collect the results of this appendix in the following expression for general helicities,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathrm{Disp}\left[\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{{\cal N}=4}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5}^{\nu_{5}})\right]\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK,}\ p_{4}\to 0]{}\\
&\frac{g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}}{(4\pi)^{2}}\,s_{12}F^{a_{3}a_{2}c_{1}}C^{g(0)}\left(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{-\nu_{2}}\right)\frac{1}{t_{1}}V^{g(0)}(q_{1},p_{5}^{\nu_{5}},q_{3})\times\\
&\Bigg\{(F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}})_{c_{1}c_{3}}\bigg[\mathrm{Soft}_{A}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5})\left(3\,v^{g(1)}(t_{1},|p_{5\perp}|^{2},t_{3})+N_{c}\left(2s^{(1)}_{A}(p_{4},p_{5})+s^{(1)}_{B}(p_{4})\right)\right.\\
&\left.+3\,E(t_{1},s_{35};t_{3},s_{56})\right)+N_{c}\mathrm{Soft}_{A^{\prime}}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5})\left(s^{(1)}_{A^{\prime}}(p_{4},p_{5})-s^{(1)}_{B}(p_{4})\right)\bigg]\\
&+(F^{a_{5}}F^{a_{4}})_{c_{1}c_{3}}\bigg[\mathrm{Soft}_{A^{\prime}}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5})\left(3\,v^{g(1)}(t_{1},|p_{5\perp}|^{2},t_{3})+N_{c}\left(2s^{(1)}_{A^{\prime}}(p_{4},p_{5})+s^{(1)}_{B}(p_{4})\right)\right.\\
&\left.+3\,E(t_{1},s_{35};t_{3},s_{56})\right)+N_{c}\mathrm{Soft}_{A}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5})\left(s^{(1)}_{A}(p_{4},p_{5})-s^{(1)}_{B}(p_{4})\right)\bigg]\\
&+d_{A}^{c_{1}a_{\sigma_{4}}a_{\sigma_{5}}c_{3}}\bigg[\mathrm{Soft}_{A}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5})\left(s^{(1)}_{A}(p_{4},p_{5})-s^{(1)}_{B}(p_{4})\right)\\
&+\mathrm{Soft}_{A^{\prime}}(p_{4}^{\nu_{4}},p_{5})\left(s^{(1)}_{A^{\prime}}(p_{4},p_{5})-s^{(1)}_{B}(p_{4})\right)\bigg]\Bigg\}\,\times\frac{1}{t_{3}}F^{a_{6}a_{1}c_{3}}C^{g(0)}\left(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{6}^{-\nu_{1}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(279)

G.3
Collinear limits

In this appendix we investigate the behaviour of the one-loop amplitude eq. (
146
) in the collinear limit,

p_{4}\to z_{4}P\,,\hskip 22.99988ptp_{5}\to z_{5}P\,,\hskip 22.99988ptz_{4}+z_{5}=1\,.

(280)

We begin by recalling the collinear limit of the colour ordered six-gluon tree-level amplitude in general kinematics.
It factorises into a divergent splitting amplitude, which behaves like
\displaystyle 1/\langle 45\rangle
or
\displaystyle 1/[45]
,
and a five-gluon amplitude,

M^{(0)}_{6g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}})\xrightarrow{p_{4}||p_{5}}\sum_{\nu=\pm}\mathrm{Split}^{(0)}_{-\nu}(p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5})\,M^{(0)}_{5g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},P^{\nu},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}}),

(281)

where we sum over the helicities of the intermediate state
\displaystyle P^{\nu}
. For an equal-helicity pair, the tree-level splitting amplitudes are

{\rm{Split}}^{(0)}_{\ominus}(p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{z_{4}z_{5}}\langle 4\,5\rangle}\,,\hskip 22.99988pt{\rm{Split}}^{(0)}_{\oplus}(p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\oplus}_{5})=0\,,

(282)

while for an opposite-helicity pair, they are

{\rm{Split}}^{(0)}_{\oplus}(p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5})=\frac{z^{2}_{5}}{\sqrt{z_{4}z_{5}}\langle 4\,5\rangle}\,,\hskip 22.99988pt{\rm{Split}}^{(0)}_{\ominus}(p^{\oplus}_{4},p^{\ominus}_{5})=-\frac{z^{2}_{4}}{\sqrt{z_{4}z_{5}}[4\,5]}\,.

(283)

In the central NMRK limit, eq. (
281
) becomes

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left.M^{(0)}_{6g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}})\right|_{\mathrm{NMRK}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\xrightarrow{p_{4}||p_{5}}\sum_{\nu=\pm}\mathrm{Split}^{(0)}_{-\nu}(p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5})\,\left.M^{(0)}_{5g}(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},P^{\nu},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}})\right|_{\mathrm{MRK}},

(284)

where the five-gluon amplitude in MRK is given in eq. (
255
), with
\displaystyle q_{3}=q_{1}-P
.
In particular, the two-gluon CEV factorises as

A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p_{4}||p_{5}}}{{=}}\sum_{\nu=\pm}\mathrm{Split}^{(0)}_{-\nu}(p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5})\,V^{g(0)}(q_{1},P^{\nu},q_{3})\,.

(285)

For the equal-helicity vertex eq. (
27
), it is straightforward to verify eq. (
285
).
For the opposite-helicity vertex, we have found it more convenient to check eq. (
285
) by starting from eq. (
45
) or eq. (
47
) rather than eq. (
28
), where as we have mentioned in section
3.2
, the BCFW representations make clear the collinear singularity structure of the amplitudes. Working in the minimal variables, the collinear limit can be expressed as

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}w&\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}-\frac{z_{4}}{z_{5}}z\,,\\
X&\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}\frac{z_{4}}{z_{5}}\,.\end{split}

(286)

For the
\displaystyle\sigma_{A}
ordering we find that four of the six rational terms factorise into a splitting function and Lipatov vertex, while two of the rational terms do not diverge in this limit (unless we move to the further soft-collinear limits of
\displaystyle z_{4}\to 0
or
\displaystyle z_{4}\to 1
),

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{A}_{uv}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}V^{g(0)}(q_{1},P^{\oplus},q_{3})\mathrm{Split}^{(0)}_{\ominus}(p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus}),\\
R_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}^{A}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}V^{g(0)}(q_{1},P^{\ominus},q_{3})\mathrm{Split}^{(0)}_{\oplus}(p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus}),\\
R_{vw}^{A}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}-\frac{(z-1)(zz_{4}+z_{5})z_{5}}{(\bar{z}-1)(\bar{z}z_{4}+z_{5})(|z|^{2}z_{4}+z_{5})},\\
R_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}^{A}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}-\frac{z^{4}z_{4}^{2}(\bar{z}-1)(\bar{z}z_{4}+z_{5})}{z_{5}(z-1)(zz_{4}+z_{5})(|z|^{2}z_{4}+z_{5})},\\
R_{wu}^{A}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}V^{g(0)}(q_{1},P^{\ominus},q_{3})\mathrm{Split}^{(0)}_{\oplus}(p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus}),\\
R_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}^{A}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}V^{g(0)}(q_{1},P^{\oplus},q_{3})\mathrm{Split}^{(0)}_{\ominus}(p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus}).\end{split}

(287)

Analogous results for the
\displaystyle\sigma_{A^{\prime}}
ordering can be found by exchanging
\displaystyle p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}
and taking the complex conjugate, as in eq. (
231
).
For the
\displaystyle\sigma_{B}
ordering, as expected, none of the rational terms diverge in this limit,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{B}_{uv}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}\frac{|z-1|^{2}|zz_{4}+z_{5}|^{2}}{z_{5}((z-1)z_{4}+z_{5})\bar{z}},\\
R_{\bar{u}\bar{v}}^{B}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}0,\\
R_{vw}^{B}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}\frac{z_{5}(z-1)(zz_{4}+z_{5})}{\bar{z}(|z|^{2}z_{4}+z_{5})((z+\bar{z}-1)z_{4}+z_{5})},\\
R_{\bar{v}\bar{w}}^{B}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}\frac{z^{3}z_{4}^{2}(\bar{z}-1)(\bar{z}z_{4}+z_{5})}{z_{5}(|z|^{2}z_{4}+z_{5})((z+\bar{z}-1)z_{4}+z_{5})},\\
R_{wu}^{B}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}\frac{z_{5}z_{4}^{2}(\bar{z}-1)(\bar{z}z_{4}+z_{5})}{((z-1)z_{4}+z_{5})((z+\bar{z}-1)z_{4}+z_{5})\left(|z_{5}+(z-1)z_{4}|^{2}+z_{4}z_{5}\right)},\\
R_{\bar{w}\bar{u}}^{B}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}\frac{(z-1)(zz_{4}+z_{5})((\bar{z}-1)z_{4}+z_{5})^{3}}{z_{5}((z+\bar{z}-1)z_{4}+z_{5})\left(|z_{5}+(z-1)z_{4}|^{2}+z_{4}z_{5}\right)}.\end{split}

(288)

From these lists of rational terms we can immediately verify that the divergent pieces satisfy the condition in eq. (
285
) from the representations eq. (
45
) or eq. (
47
). We note that in this limit the divergent pieces also satisfy

\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})\right|_{p_{4}||p_{5}}=-\left.A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},q_{3})\right|_{p_{4}||p_{5}}.

(289)

In addition to eq. (
280
), the amplitude in eq. (
26
) is divergent in the collinear regions
\displaystyle p_{3\perp}=-q_{1\perp}\rightarrow 0
or

\displaystyle p_{6\perp}=q_{3\perp}\rightarrow 0
. In those regions, eq. (
26
) must not diverge more rapidly than
\displaystyle 1/|q_{i\perp}|
,
with
\displaystyle i=1,3
, in order for the related cross section not to diverge more than logarithmically.
Since eq. (
26
) displays poles as
\displaystyle|q_{1\perp}|^{2}
and
\displaystyle|q_{3\perp}|^{2}

go to zero, the central-emission vertex must be at least linear in
\displaystyle|q_{i\perp}|
as
\displaystyle|q_{i\perp}|\rightarrow 0
,

A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},q_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle|q_{i\perp}|\rightarrow 0}}{{=}}{\cal O}(|q_{i\perp}|)\,,

(290)

with
\displaystyle i=1,3
, which is indeed fulfilled by eq. (
28
).

Let us now consider the collinear limit of the one-loop amplitudes in
\displaystyle{\cal N}=4
SYM in the NMRK limit. It is most convenient if we work at the level of colour-ordered amplitudes, where in general kinematics the colour-ordered one-loop six gluon amplitude has the collinear limit
Bern:1994zx
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}M^{(1)}_{6g}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}},p_{1}^{\nu_{1}})\xrightarrow{p_{4}||p_{5}}\sum_{\nu=\pm}\Big(&\mathrm{Split}_{-\nu}^{(0)}(p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5})\,M^{(1)}_{5g}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},P^{\nu},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}},p_{1}^{\nu_{1}})\\
+&\mathrm{Split}_{-\nu}^{(1)}(p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5})\,M^{(0)}_{5g}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},P^{\nu},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}},p_{1}^{\nu_{1}})\Big)\,,\end{split}

(291)

where the one-loop splitting functions were obtained in
Bern:1994zx
. In
\displaystyle\mathcal{N}=4
SYM, these splitting functions are proportional to the tree-level splitting function,

\mathrm{Split}_{-\nu}^{(1)}(p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5})=\kappa_{\Gamma}\,\mathrm{Split}_{-\nu}^{(0)}(p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5})\,r_{S}^{\mathrm{SUSY}}(z_{4},s_{45}),

(292)

where the loop factor,

r_{S}^{\mathrm{SUSY}}(z_{4},s_{45})=-\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{z_{4}(1-z_{4})(-s_{45})}\right)^{\epsilon}+2\log(z_{4})\log(1-z_{4})-\frac{\pi^{2}}{6},

(293)

is independent of the helicities. In the central NMRK limit we expect to obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.M^{(1)}_{6g}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}},p_{1}^{\nu_{1}})\right|&{}_{\mathrm{NMRK}}\xrightarrow{p_{4}||p_{5}}\\
\sum_{\nu=\pm}\bigg(&\mathrm{Split}_{-\nu}^{(0)}(p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5})\,\left.M^{(1)}_{5g}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},P^{\nu},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}},p_{1}^{\nu_{1}})\right|_{\mathrm{MRK}}\\
+&\mathrm{Split}_{-\nu}^{(1)}(p^{\nu_{4}}_{4},p^{\nu_{5}}_{5})\,\left.M^{(0)}_{5g}(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{\nu_{3}},P^{\nu},p_{6}^{\nu_{6}},p_{1}^{\nu_{1}})\right|_{\mathrm{MRK}}\bigg).\end{split}

(294)

Let us check this first for the same-helicity case. As we are working at the level of colour-ordered amplitudes, we consider the three representative orderings of eq. (
94
). For the
\displaystyle\sigma_{A}
colour ordering, we find

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}s_{234}&\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}z_{5}t_{1}+z_{4}t_{3}\,,\\
\end{split}

(295)

so that in the collinear limit,

v_{A}+w_{A}\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}1\,.

(296)

In particular, this allows us to use the identity,

{\rm Li_{2}}\left(1-x\right)+{\rm Li_{2}}\left(x\right)=\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}-\log\left(1-x\right)\log\left(x\right).

(297)

to write

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}\Delta V_{A}(u_{A},v_{A},w_{A})\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}\bigg(&-\frac{1}{4}\log^{2}\left(\frac{s_{45}}{|P_{\perp}|^{2}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{s_{45}}{|P_{\perp}|^{2}}\right)\log\left(\frac{t_{1}t_{3}z_{4}z_{5}}{s_{234}^{2}}\right)\\
&+\log\left(\frac{z_{4}t_{3}}{s_{234}}\right)\log\left(\frac{z_{5}t_{1}}{s_{234}}\right)\bigg)\,.\end{split}

(298)

For the full transcendental function of this colour ordering we find

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}N_{c}\kappa_{\Gamma}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{\left.V_{6}(\sigma_{A})\right|_{\mathrm{NMRK}}}\right]}\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}&E(t_{1},p_{3}^{+}P^{-};t_{3},P^{+}p_{6}^{-})+v^{g(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}\left(t_{1},|P_{\perp}|^{2},t_{3}\right)\\
+&N_{c}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{r^{\mathrm{SUSY}}(z_{4},s_{45})}\right]}\,,\end{split}

(299)

which is invariant under
\displaystyle p_{4}\leftrightarrow p_{5}
.
After manipulating the colour factors to obtain the natural association of one adjoint generator with each three-point tree-level factor, we obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}(p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\oplus})}\right]}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK},\,p_{4}||p_{5}]{}\\
\frac{g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}}{(4\pi)^{2}}\kappa_{\Gamma}s_{12}&\left[F^{a_{3}a_{2}c_{1}}C^{g(0)}\left(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{-\nu_{2}}\right)\right]\frac{1}{t_{1}}\\
\times&\left[F^{c_{1}c_{3}c_{2}}V^{g(0)}(q_{1},P^{\oplus},q_{3})\right]\left[F^{c_{2}a_{5}a_{4}}\mathrm{Split}^{(0)}_{\ominus}(p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\oplus})\right]\\
\times&\frac{1}{t_{3}}\left[F^{a_{6}a_{1}c_{3}}C^{g(0)}\left(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{6}^{-\nu_{1}}\right)\right]\times\bigg\{N_{c}\left.{\mathrm{Re}\left[{V_{6}(\sigma_{A})}\right]}\right|_{\mathrm{NMRK},\,p_{4}||p_{5}}\bigg\}\,.\end{split}

(300)

The opposite-helicity case proceeds similarly.
From eq. (
287
) we see that in the collinear limit, both
\displaystyle S^{A}_{uv}
and
\displaystyle S^{A}_{wu}
are equal to the tree-level amplitude, and these terms combine with the
\displaystyle\Delta W_{A}
term to play the same role as
\displaystyle\Delta V_{A}
in this limit,

\displaystyle\displaystyle A^{gg(0)}(q_{1},p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{4}^{\ominus},q_{3})\Delta W_{A}(u_{A},v_{A},w_{A})+S^{A}_{uv}\log(v_{A})\left(\frac{w_{A}}{u_{A}}\right)+S^{A}_{wu}\log(w_{A})\left(\frac{v_{A}}{u_{A}}\right)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\xrightarrow[p_{4}||p_{5}]{}\left(\sum_{\nu=\pm}V^{g(0)}(q_{1},P^{\nu},q_{3})\mathrm{Split}^{(0)}_{-\nu}(p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus})\right)\left.\Delta V_{A}(u_{A},v_{A},w_{A})\right|_{p_{4}||p_{5}}

(301)

This means we can straightforwardly write

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathrm{Re}\left[{\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{N}=4}(p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus})}\right]}&\xrightarrow[\mathrm{NMRK},p_{4}||p_{5}]{}\\
\frac{g_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{6}}{(4\pi)^{2}}\kappa_{\Gamma}s_{12}&\left[F^{a_{3}a_{2}c_{1}}C^{g(0)}\left(p_{2}^{\nu_{2}},p_{3}^{-\nu_{2}}\right)\right]\frac{1}{t_{1}}\\
\times&\sum_{\nu=\pm}\left(\left[F^{c_{1}c_{3}c_{2}}V^{g(0)}(q_{1},P^{\nu},q_{3})\right]\left[F^{c_{2}a_{5}a_{4}}\mathrm{Split}^{(0)}_{-\nu}(p_{4}^{\oplus},p_{5}^{\ominus})\right]\right)\\
\times&\frac{1}{t_{3}}\left[F^{a_{6}a_{1}c_{3}}C^{g(0)}\left(p_{1}^{\nu_{1}},p_{6}^{-\nu_{1}}\right)\right]\times\bigg\{N_{c}\left.{\mathrm{Re}\left[{V_{6}(\sigma_{A})}\right]}\right|_{\mathrm{NMRK},\,p_{4}||p_{5}}\bigg\}.\end{split}

(302)
